LG1891	1	ONE'S-SELF I sing, a simple separate person,
LG1891	2	Yet utter the word Democratic, the word En-Masse.
LG1891	3	Of physiology from top to toe I sing,
LG1891	4	Not physiognomy alone nor brain alone is worthy for the Muse, I say the Form complete is worthier far,
LG1891	5	The Female equally with the Male I sing.
LG1891	6	Of Life immense in passion, pulse, and power,
LG1891	7	Cheerful, for freest action form'd under the laws divine,
LG1891	8	The Modern Man I sing.
LG1891	9	As I ponder'd in silence,
LG1891	10	Returning upon my poems, considering, lingering long,
LG1891	11	A Phantom arose before me with distrustful aspect,
LG1891	12	Terrible in beauty, age, and power,
LG1891	13	The genius of poets of old lands,
LG1891	14	As to me directing like flame its eyes,
LG1891	15	With finger pointing to many immortal songs,
LG1891	16	And menacing voice, What singest thou? it said,
LG1891	17	Know'st thou not there is but one theme for ever-enduring bards?
LG1891	18	And that is the theme of War, the fortune of battles,
LG1891	19	The making of perfect soldiers.
LG1891	20	Be it so, then I answer'd,
LG1891	21	I too haughty Shade also sing war, and a longer and greater one than any,
LG1891	22	Waged in my book with varying fortune, with flight, advance and retreat, victory deferr'd and wavering,
LG1891	23	(Yet methinks certain, or as good as certain, at the last,) the field the world,
LG1891	24	For life and death, for the Body and for the eternal Soul,
LG1891	25	Lo, I too am come, chanting the chant of battles,
LG1891	26	I above all promote brave soldiers.
LG1891	27	IN cabin'd ships at sea,
LG1891	28	The boundless blue on every side expanding,
LG1891	29	With whistling winds and music of the waves, the large imperious waves,
LG1891	30	Or some lone bark buoy'd on the dense marine,
LG1891	31	Where joyous full of faith, spreading white sails,
LG1891	32	She cleaves the ether mid the sparkle and the foam of day, or under many a star at night,
LG1891	33	By sailors young and old haply will I, a reminiscence of the land,be read,
LG1891	34	In full rapport at last.
LG1891	35	Here are our thoughts, voyagers' thoughts,
LG1891	36	Here not the land, firm land, alone appears, may then by them be said,
LG1891	37	The sky o'erarches here, we feel the undulating deck beneath our feet,
LG1891	38	We feel the long pulsation, ebb and flow of endless motion,
LG1891	39	The tones of unseen mystery, the vague and vast suggestions of the briny world, the liquid-flowing syllables,
LG1891	40	The perfume, the faint creaking of the cordage, the melancholy rhythm,
LG1891	41	The boundless vista and the horizon far and dim are all here,
LG1891	42	And this is ocean's poem.
LG1891	43	Then falter not O book, fulfilfulfill your destiny,
LG1891	44	You not a reminiscence of the land alone,
LG1891	45	You too as a lone bark cleaving the ether, purpos'd I know not whither, yet ever full of faith,
LG1891	46	Consort to every ship that sails, sail you!
LG1891	47	Bear forth to them folded my love, (dear mariners, for you I fold it here in every leaf;)
LG1891	48	Speed on my book! spread your white sails my little bark athwart the imperious waves,
LG1891	49	Chant on, sail on, bear o'er the boundless blue from me to every sea,
LG1891	50	This song for mariners and all their ships.
LG1891	51	I HEARD that you ask'd for something to prove this puzzle the New World,
LG1891	52	And to define America, her athletic Democracy,
LG1891	53	Therefore I send you my poems that you behold in them what you wanted.
LG1891	54	YOU who celebrate bygones,
LG1891	55	Who have explored the outward, the surfaces of the races, the life that has exhibited itself,
LG1891	56	Who have treated of man as the creature of politics, aggregates, rulers and priests,
LG1891	57	I, habitan of the Alleghanies, treating of him as he is in himself in his own rights,
LG1891	58	Pressing the pulse of the life that has seldom exhibited itself, (the great pride of man in himself,)
LG1891	59	Chanter of Personality, outlining what is yet to be,
LG1891	60	I project the history of the future.
LG1891	61	TO thee old cause!
LG1891	62	Thou peerless, passionate, good cause,
LG1891	63	Thou stern, remorseless, sweet idea,
LG1891	64	Deathless throughout the ages, races, lands,
LG1891	65	After a strange sad war, great war for thee,
LG1891	66	(I think all war through time was really fought, and ever will be really fought, for thee,)
LG1891	67	These chants for thee, the eternal march of thee.
LG1891	68	(A war O soldiers not for itself alone,
LG1891	69	Far, far more stood silently waiting behind, now to advance in this book.)
LG1891	70	Thou orb of many orbs!
LG1891	71	Thou seething principle! thou well-kept, latent germ! thou centre!
LG1891	72	Around the idea of thee the war revolving,
LG1891	73	With all its angry and vehement play of causes,
LG1891	74	(With vast results to come for thrice a thousand years,)
LG1891	75	These recitatives for thee,—my book and the war are one,
LG1891	76	Merged in its spirit I and mine, as the contest hinged on thee,
LG1891	77	As a wheel on its axis turns, this book unwitting to itself,
LG1891	78	Around the idea of thee.
LG1891	79	I MET a seer,
LG1891	80	Passing the hues and objects of the world,
LG1891	81	The fields of art and learning, pleasure, sense,
LG1891	82	To glean eidólons.
LG1891	83	Put in thy chants said he,
LG1891	84	No more the puzzling hour nor day, nor segments, parts, put in,
LG1891	85	Put first before the rest as light for all and entrance-song of all,
LG1891	86	That of eidólons.
LG1891	87	Ever the dim beginning,
LG1891	88	Ever the growth, the rounding of the circle,
LG1891	89	Ever the summit and the merge at last, (to surely start again,)
LG1891	90	Eidólons! eidólons!
LG1891	91	Ever the mutable,
LG1891	92	Ever materials, changing, crumbling, re-cohering,
LG1891	93	Ever the ateliers, the factories divine,
LG1891	94	Issuing eidólons.
LG1891	95	Lo, I or you,
LG1891	96	Or woman, man, or state, known or unknown,
LG1891	97	We seeming solid wealth, strength, beauty build,
LG1891	98	But really build eidólons.
LG1891	99	The ostent evanescent,
LG1891	100	The substance of an artist's mood or savan's studies long,
LG1891	101	Or warrior's, martyr's, hero's toils,
LG1891	102	To fashion his eidólon.
LG1891	103	Of every human life,
LG1891	104	(The units gather'd, posted, not a thought, emotion, deed, left out,)
LG1891	105	The whole or large or small summ'd, added up,
LG1891	106	In its eidólon.
LG1891	107	The old, old urge,
LG1891	108	Based on the ancient pinnacles, lo, newer, higher pinnacles,
LG1891	109	From science and the modern still impell'd,
LG1891	110	The old, old urge, eidólons.
LG1891	111	The present now and here,
LG1891	112	America's busy, teeming, intricate whirl,
LG1891	113	Of aggregate and segregate for only thence releasing,
LG1891	114	To-day's eidólons.
LG1891	115	These with the past,
LG1891	116	Of vanish'd lands, of all the reigns of kings across the sea,
LG1891	117	Old conquerors, old campaigns, old sailors' voyages,
LG1891	118	Joining eidólons.
LG1891	119	Densities, growth, façades,
LG1891	120	Strata of mountains, soils, rocks, giant trees,
LG1891	121	Far-born, far-dying, living long, to leave,
LG1891	122	Eidólons everlasting.
LG1891	123	Exaltè, rapt, ecstatic,
LG1891	124	The visible but their womb of birth,
LG1891	125	Of orbic tendencies to shape and shape and shape,
LG1891	126	The mighty earth-eidólon.
LG1891	127	All space, all time,
LG1891	128	(The stars, the terrible perturbations of the suns,
LG1891	129	Swelling, collapsing, ending, serving their longer, shorter use,)
LG1891	130	Fill'd with eidólons only.
LG1891	131	The noiseless myriads,
LG1891	132	The infinite oceans where the rivers empty,
LG1891	133	The separate countless free identities, like eyesight,
LG1891	134	The true realities, eidólons.
LG1891	135	Not this the world,
LG1891	136	Nor these the universes, they the universes,
LG1891	137	Purport and end, ever the permanent life of life,
LG1891	138	Eidólons, eidólons.
LG1891	139	Beyond thy lectures learn'd professor,
LG1891	140	Beyond thy telescope or spectroscope observer keen, beyond all mathematics,
LG1891	141	Beyond the doctor's surgery, anatomy, beyond the chemist with his chemistry,
LG1891	142	The entities of entities, eidólons.
LG1891	143	Unfix'd yet fix'd,
LG1891	144	Ever shall be, ever have been and are,
LG1891	145	Sweeping the present to the infinite future,
LG1891	146	Eidólons, eidólons, eidólons.
LG1891	147	The prophet and the bard,
LG1891	148	Shall yet maintain themselves, in higher stages yet,
LG1891	149	Shall mediate to the Modern, to Democracy, interpret yet to them,
LG1891	150	God and eidólons.
LG1891	151	And thee my soul,
LG1891	152	Joys, ceaseless exercises, exaltations,
LG1891	153	Thy yearning amply fed at last, prepared to meet,
LG1891	154	Thy mates, eidólons.
LG1891	155	Thy body permanent,
LG1891	156	The body lurking there within thy body,
LG1891	157	The only purport of the form thou art, the real I myself,
LG1891	158	An image, an eidólon.
LG1891	159	Thy very songs not in thy songs,
LG1891	160	No special strains to sing, none for itself,
LG1891	161	But from the whole resulting, rising at last and floating,
LG1891	162	A round full-orb'd eidólon.
LG1891	163	FOR him I sing,
LG1891	164	I raise the present on the past,
LG1891	165	(As some perennial tree out of its roots, the present on the past,)
LG1891	166	With time and space I him dilate and fuse the immortal laws,
LG1891	167	To make himself by them the law unto himself.
LG1891	168	WHEN I read the book, the biography famous,
LG1891	169	And is this then (said I) what the author calls a man's life?
LG1891	170	And so will some one when I am dead and gone write my life?
LG1891	171	(As if any man really knew aught of my life,
LG1891	172	Why even I myself I often think know little or nothing of my real life,
LG1891	173	Only a few hints, a few diffused faint clews and indirections
LG1891	174	I seek for my own use to trace out here.)
LG1891	175	BEGINNING my studies the first step pleas'd me so much,
LG1891	176	The mere fact consciousness, these forms, the power of motion,
LG1891	177	The least insect or animal, the senses, eyesight, love,
LG1891	178	The first step I say awed me and pleas'd me so much,
LG1891	179	I have hardly gone and hardly wish'd to go any farther,
LG1891	180	But stop and loiter all the time to sing it in ecstatic songs.
LG1891	181	HOW they are provided for upon the earth, (appearing at inter-valsintervals,)
LG1891	182	How dear and dreadful they are to the earth,
LG1891	183	How they inure to themselves as much as to any—what a paradox appears their age,
LG1891	184	How people respond to them, yet know them not,
LG1891	185	How there is something relentless in their fate all times,
LG1891	186	How all times mischoose the objects of their adulation and re-wardreward,
LG1891	187	And how the same inexorable price must still be paid for the same great purchase.
LG1891	188	TO the States or any one of them, or any city of the States, Resist much, obey little,
LG1891	189	Once unquestioning obedience, once fully enslaved,
LG1891	190	Once fully enslaved, no nation, state, city of this earth, ever after-wardafterward resumes its liberty.
LG1891	191	ON journeys through the States we start,
LG1891	192	(Ay through the world, urged by these songs,
LG1891	193	Sailing henceforth to every land, to every sea,)
LG1891	194	We willing learners of all, teachers of all, and lovers of all.
LG1891	195	We have watch'd the seasons dispensing themselves and passing on,
LG1891	196	And have said, Why should not a man or woman do as much as the seasons, and effuse as much?
LG1891	197	We dwell a while in every city and town,
LG1891	198	We pass through KanadaCanada, the North-east, the vast valley of the Mississippi, and the Southern States,
LG1891	199	We confer on equal terms with each of the States,
LG1891	200	We make trial of ourselves and invite men and women to hear,
LG1891	201	We say to ourselves, Remember, fear not, be candid, promulge the body and the soul,
LG1891	202	Dwell a while and pass on, be copious, temperate, chaste, mag-neticmagnetic,
LG1891	203	And what you effuse may then return as the seasons return,
LG1891	204	And may be just as much as the seasons.
LG1891	205	HERE, take this gift,
LG1891	206	I was reserving it for some hero, speaker, or general,
LG1891	207	One who should serve the good old cause, the great idea, the prog-ressprogress and freedom of the race,
LG1891	208	Some brave confronter of despots, some daring rebel;
LG1891	209	But I see that what I was reserving belongs to you just as much as to any.
LG1891	210	ME imperturbe, standing at ease in Nature,
LG1891	211	Master of all or mistress of all, aplomb in the midst of irrational things,
LG1891	212	Imbued as they, passive, receptive, silent as they,
LG1891	213	Finding my occupation, poverty, notoriety, foibles, crimes, less im-portantimportant than I thought,
LG1891	214	Me toward the Mexican sea, or in the Mannahatta or the Tennes-seeTennessee, or far north or inland,
LG1891	215	A river man, or a man of the woods or of any farm-life of these States or of the coast, or the lakes or KanadaCanada,
LG1891	216	Me wherever my life is lived, O to be self-balanced for contingen-ciescontingencies,
LG1891	217	To confront night, storms, hunger, ridicule, accidents, rebuffs, as the trees and animals do.
LG1891	218	THITHER as I look I see each result and glory retracing itself and nestling close, always obligated,
LG1891	219	Thither hours, months, years—thither trades, compacts, establish-mentsestablishments, even the most minute,
LG1891	220	Thither every-day life, speech, utensils, politics, persons, estates;
LG1891	221	Thither we also, I with my leaves and songs, trustful, admirant,
LG1891	222	As a father to his father going takes his children along with him.
LG1891	223	LO, the unbounded sea,
LG1891	224	On its breast a ship starting, spreading all sails, carrying even her moonsails,
LG1891	225	The pennant is flying aloft as she speeds she speeds so stately—below emulous waves press forward,
LG1891	226	They surround the ship with shining curving motions and foam.
LG1891	227	I HEAR America singing, the varied carols I hear,
LG1891	228	Those of mechanics, each one singing his as it should be blithe and strong,
LG1891	229	The carpenter singing his as he measures his plank or beam,
LG1891	230	The mason singing his as he makes ready for work, or leaves off work,
LG1891	231	The boatman singing what belongs to him in his boat, the deck-handdeckhand singing on the steamboat deck,
LG1891	232	The shoemaker singing as he sits on his bench, the hatter singing as he stands,
LG1891	233	The wood-cutter's song, the ploughboy's on his way in the morn-ingmorning, or at noon intermission or at sundown,
LG1891	234	The delicious singing of the mother, or of the young wife at work,or of the girl sewing or washing,
LG1891	235	Each singing what belongs to him or her and to none else,
LG1891	236	The day what belongs to the day—at night the party of young fellows, robust, friendly,
LG1891	237	Singing with open mouths their strong melodious songs.
LG1891	238	WHAT place is besieged, and vainly tries to raise the siege?
LG1891	239	Lo, I send to that place a commander, swift, brave, immortal,
LG1891	240	And with him horse and foot, and parks of artillery,
LG1891	241	And artillery-men, the deadliest that ever fired gun.
LG1891	242	STILL though the one I sing,
LG1891	243	(One, yet of contradictions made,) I dedicate to Nationality,
LG1891	244	I leave in him revolt, (O latent right of insurrection! O quench-lessquenchless, indispensable fire!)
LG1891	245	SHUT not your doors to me proud libraries,
LG1891	246	For that which was lacking on all your well-fill'd shelves, yet needed most, I bring,
LG1891	247	Forth from the war emerging, a book I have made,
LG1891	248	The words of my book nothing, the drift of it every thing,
LG1891	249	A book separate, not link'd with the rest nor felt by the intellect,
LG1891	250	But you ye untold latencies will thrill to every page.
LG1891	251	POETS to come! orators, singers, musicians to come!
LG1891	252	Not to-day is to justify me and answer what I am for,
LG1891	253	But you, a new brood, native, athletic, continental, greater than before known,
LG1891	254	Arouse! for you must justify me.
LG1891	255	I myself but write one or two indicative words for the future,
LG1891	256	I but advance a moment only to wheel and hurry back in the darkness.
LG1891	257	I am a man who, sauntering along without fully stopping, turns a casual look upon you and then averts his face,
LG1891	258	Leaving it to you to prove and define it,
LG1891	259	Expecting the main things from you.
LG1891	260	STRANGER, if you passing meet me and desire to speak to me, why should you not speak to me?
LG1891	261	And why should I not speak to you?
LG1891	262	THOU reader throbbest life and pride and love the same as I,
LG1891	263	Therefore for thee the following chants.
LG1891	264	STARTING from fish-shape Paumanok where I was born,
LG1891	265	Well-begotten, and rais'd by a perfect mother,
LG1891	266	After roaming many lands, lover of populous pavements,
LG1891	267	Dweller in Mannahatta my city, or on southern savannas,
LG1891	268	Or a soldier camp'd or carrying my knapsack and gun, or a miner in California,
LG1891	269	Or rude in my home in Dakota's woods, my diet meat, my drink from the spring,
LG1891	270	Or withdrawn to muse and meditate in some deep recess,
LG1891	271	Far from the clank of crowds intervals passing rapt and happy,
LG1891	272	Aware of the fresh free giver the flowing Missouri, aware of mighty Niagara,
LG1891	273	Aware of the buffalo herds grazing the plains, the hirsute and strong-breasted bull,
LG1891	274	Of earth, rocks, Fifth-month flowers experienced, stars, rain, snow,my amaze,
LG1891	275	Having studied the mocking-bird's tones and the flight of the mountain-hawk,
LG1891	276	And heard at dawn the unrivall'd one, the hermit thrush from the swamp-cedars,
LG1891	277	Solitary, singing in the West, I strike up for a New World.
LG1891	278	Victory, union, faith, identity, time,
LG1891	279	The indissoluble compacts, riches, mystery,
LG1891	280	Eternal progress, the kosmos, and the modern reports.
LG1891	281	This then is life,
LG1891	282	Here is what has come to the surface after so many throes and convulsions.
LG1891	283	How curious! how real!
LG1891	284	Underfoot the divine soil, overhead the sun.
LG1891	285	See revolving the globe,
LG1891	286	The ancestor-continents away group'd together,
LG1891	287	The present and future continents north and south, with the isthmus between.
LG1891	288	See, vast trackless spaces,
LG1891	289	As in a dream they change, they swiftly fill,
LG1891	290	Countless masses debouch upon them,
LG1891	291	They are now cover'd with the foremost people, arts, institutions,known.
LG1891	292	See, projected through time,
LG1891	293	For me an audience interminable.
LG1891	294	With firm and regular step they wend, they never stop,
LG1891	295	Successions of men, Americanos, a hundred millions,
LG1891	296	One generation playing its part and passing on,
LG1891	297	Another generation playing its part and passing on in its turn,
LG1891	298	With faces turn'd sideways or backward towards me to listen,
LG1891	299	With eyes retrospective towards me.
LG1891	300	Americanos! conquerors! marches humanitarian!
LG1891	301	Foremost! century marches! Libertad! masses!
LG1891	302	For you a programme of chants.
LG1891	303	Chants of the prairies,
LG1891	304	Chants of the long-running Mississippi, and down to the Mexican sea,
LG1891	305	Chants of Ohio, Indiana, Illinois, Iowa, Wisconsin and Minnesota,
LG1891	306	Chants going forth from the centre from Kansas, and thence equi-distantequidistant,
LG1891	307	Shooting in pulses of fire ceaseless to vivify all.
LG1891	308	Take my leaves America, take them South and take them North,
LG1891	309	Make welcome for them everywhere, for they are your own off-springoffspring,
LG1891	310	Surround them East and West, for they would surround you,
LG1891	311	And you precedents, connect lovingly with them, for they connect lovingly with you.
LG1891	312	I conn'd old times,
LG1891	313	I sat studying at the feet of the great masters,
LG1891	314	Now if eligible O that the great masters might return and study me.
LG1891	315	In the name of these States shall I scorn the antique?
LG1891	316	Why these are the children of the antique to justify it.
LG1891	317	Dead poets, philosophs, priests,
LG1891	318	Martyrs, artists, inventors, governments long since,
LG1891	319	Language-shapers on other shores,
LG1891	320	Nations once powerful, now reduced, withdrawn, or desolate,
LG1891	321	I dare not proceed till I respectfully credit what you have left wafted hither,
LG1891	322	I have perused it, own it is admirable, (moving awhile among it,)
LG1891	323	Think nothing can ever be greater, nothing can ever deserve more than it deserves,
LG1891	324	Regarding it all intently a long while, then dismissing it,
LG1891	325	I stand in my place with my own day here.
LG1891	326	Here lands female and male,
LG1891	327	Here the heir-ship and heiress-ship of the world, here the flame of materials,
LG1891	328	Here spirituality the translatress, the openly-avow'd,
LG1891	329	The ever-tending, the finalè of visible forms,
LG1891	330	The satisfier, after due long-waiting now advancing,
LG1891	331	Yes here comes my mistress the soul.
LG1891	332	The soul,
LG1891	333	Forever and forever—longer than soil is brown and solid—longer than water ebbs and flows.
LG1891	334	I will make the poems of materials, for I think they are to be the most spiritual poems,
LG1891	335	And I will make the poems of my body and of mortality,
LG1891	336	For I think I shall then supply myself with the poems of my soul and of immortality.
LG1891	337	I will make a song for these States that no one State may under any circumstances be subjected to another State,
LG1891	338	And I will make a song that there shall be comity by day and by night between all the States, and between any two of them,
LG1891	339	And I will make a song for the ears of the President, full of weap-onsweapons with menacing points,
LG1891	340	And behind the weapons countless dissatisfied faces;
LG1891	341	And a song make I of the One form'd out of all,
LG1891	342	The fang'd and glittering One whose head is over all,
LG1891	343	Resolute warlike One including and over all,
LG1891	344	(However high the head of any else that head is over all.)
LG1891	345	I will acknowledge contemporary lands,
LG1891	346	I will trail the whole geography of the globe and salute courte-ouslycourteously every city large and small,
LG1891	347	And employments! I will put in my poems that with you is hero-ismheroism upon land and sea,
LG1891	348	And I will report all heroism from an American point of view.
LG1891	349	I will sing the song of companionship,
LG1891	350	I will show what alone must finally compact these,
LG1891	351	I believe these are to found their own ideal of manly love, indi-catingindicating it in me,
LG1891	352	I will therefore let flame from me the burning fires that were threatening to consume me,
LG1891	353	I will lift what has too long kept down those smouldering fires,
LG1891	354	I will give them complete abandonment,
LG1891	355	I will write the evangel-poem of comrades and of love,
LG1891	356	For who but I should understand love with all its sorrow and joy?
LG1891	357	And who but I should be the poet of comrades?
LG1891	358	I am the credulous man of qualities, ages, races,
LG1891	359	I advance from the people in their own spirit,
LG1891	360	Here is what sings unrestricted faith.
LG1891	361	Omnes! omnes! let others ignore what they may,
LG1891	362	I make the poem of evil also, I commemorate that part also,
LG1891	363	I am myself just as much evil as good, and my nation is—and I say there is in fact no evil,
LG1891	364	(Or if there is I say it is just as important to you, to the land or to me, as any thing else.)
LG1891	365	I too, following many and follow'd by many, inaugurate a religion,I descend into the arena,
LG1891	366	(It may be I am destin'd to utter the loudest cries there, the win-ner'swinner's pealing shouts,
LG1891	367	Who knows? they may rise from me yet, and soar above every thing.)
LG1891	368	Each is not for its own sake,
LG1891	369	I say the whole earth and all the stars in the sky are for religion's sake.
LG1891	370	I say no man has ever yet been half devout enough,
LG1891	371	None has ever yet adored or worship'd half enough,
LG1891	372	None has begun to think how divine he himself is, and how cer-taincertain the future is.
LG1891	373	I say that the real and permanent grandeur of these States must be their religion,
LG1891	374	Otherwise there is no real and permanent grandeur;
LG1891	375	(Nor character nor life worthy the name without religion,
LG1891	376	Nor land nor man or woman without religion.)
LG1891	377	What are you doing young man?
LG1891	378	Are you so earnest, so given up to literature, science, art, amours?
LG1891	379	These ostensible realities, politics, points?
LG1891	380	Your ambition or business whatever it may be?
LG1891	381	It is well—against such I say not a word, I am their poet also,
LG1891	382	But behold! such swiftly subside, burnt up for religion's sake,
LG1891	383	For not all matter is fuel to heat, impalpable flame, the essential life of the earth,
LG1891	384	Any more than such are to religion.
LG1891	385	What do you seek so pensive and silent?
LG1891	386	What do you need camerado?
LG1891	387	Dear son do you think it is love?
LG1891	388	Listen dear son—listen America, daughter or son,
LG1891	389	It is a painful thing to love a man or woman to excess, and yet it satisfies, it is great,
LG1891	390	But there is something else very great, it makes the whole coin-cidecoincide,
LG1891	391	It, magnificent, beyond materials, with continuous hands sweeps and provides for all.
LG1891	392	Know you, solely to drop in the earth the germs of a greater religion,
LG1891	393	The following chants each for its kind I sing.
LG1891	394	My comrade!
LG1891	395	For you to share with me two greatnesses, and a third one rising inclusive and more resplendent,
LG1891	396	The greatness of Love and Democracy, and the greatness of Reli-gionReligion.
LG1891	397	Melange mine own, the unseen and the seen,
LG1891	398	Mysterious ocean where the streams empty,
LG1891	399	Prophetic spirit of materials shifting and flickering around me,
LG1891	400	Living beings, identities now doubtless near us in the air that we know not of,
LG1891	401	Contact daily and hourly that will not release me,
LG1891	402	These selecting, these in hints demanded of me.
LG1891	403	Not he with a daily kiss onward from childhood kissing me,
LG1891	404	Has winded and twisted around me that which holds me to him,
LG1891	405	Any more than I am held to the heavens and all the spiritual world,
LG1891	406	After what they have done to me, suggesting themes.
LG1891	407	O such themes—equalities! O divine average!
LG1891	408	Warblings under the sun, usher'd as now, or at noon, or set-ingsetting,
LG1891	409	Strains musical flowing through ages, now reaching hither,
LG1891	410	I take to your reckless and composite chords, add to them, and cheerfully pass them forward.
LG1891	411	As I have walk'd in Alabama my morning walk,
LG1891	412	I have seen where the she-bird the mocking-bird sat on her nest in the briers hatching her brood.
LG1891	413	I have seen the he-bird also,
LG1891	414	I have paus'd to hear him near at hand inflating his throat and joyfully singing.
LG1891	415	And while I paus'd it came to me that what he really sang for was not there only,
LG1891	416	Nor for his mate nor himself only, nor all sent back by the echoes,
LG1891	417	But subtle, clandestine, away beyond,
LG1891	418	A charge transmitted and gift occult for those being born.
LG1891	419	Democracy! near at hand to you a throat is now inflating itself and joyfully singing.
LG1891	420	Ma femme! for the brood beyond us and of us,
LG1891	421	For those who belong here and those to come,
LG1891	422	I exultant to be ready for them will now shake out carols stronger and haughtier than have ever yet been heard upon earth.
LG1891	423	I will make the songs of passion to give them their way,
LG1891	424	And your songs outlaw'd offenders, for I scan you with kindred eyes, and carry you with me the same as any.
LG1891	425	I will make the true poem of riches,
LG1891	426	To earn for the body and the mind whatever adheres and goes forward and is not dropt by death;
LG1891	427	I will effuse egotism and show it underlying all, and I will be the bard of personality,
LG1891	428	And I will show of male and female that either is but the equal of the other,
LG1891	429	And sexual organs and acts! do you concentrate in me, for I am determin'd to tell you with courageous clear voice to prove you illustrious,
LG1891	430	And I will show that there is no imperfection in the present, and can be none in the future,
LG1891	431	And I will show that whatever happens to anybody it may be turn'd to beautiful results,
LG1891	432	And I will show that nothing can happen more beautiful than death,
LG1891	433	And I will thread a thread through my poems that time and events are compact,
LG1891	434	And that all the things of the universe are perfect miracles, each as profound as any.
LG1891	435	I will not make poems with reference to parts,
LG1891	436	But I will make poems, songs, thoughts, with reference to ensemble,
LG1891	437	And I will not sing with reference to a day, but with reference to all days,
LG1891	438	And I will not make a poem nor the least part of a poem but has reference to the soul,
LG1891	439	Because having look'd at the objects of the universe, I find there is no one nor any particle of one but has reference to the soul.
LG1891	440	Was somebody asking to see the soul?
LG1891	441	See, your own shape and countenance, persons, substances, beasts,the trees, the running rivers, the rocks and sands.
LG1891	442	All hold spiritual joys and afterwards loosen them;
LG1891	443	How can the real body ever die and be buried?
LG1891	444	Of your real body and any man's or woman's real body,
LG1891	445	Item for item it will elude the hands of the corpse-cleaners and pass to fitting spheres,
LG1891	446	Carrying what has accrued to it from the moment of birth to the moment of death.
LG1891	447	Not the types set up by the printer return their impression, the meaning, the main concern,
LG1891	448	Any more than a man's substance and life or a woman's substance and life return in the body and the soul,
LG1891	449	Indifferently before death and after death.
LG1891	450	Behold, the body includes and is the meaning, the main concern,and includes and is the soul;
LG1891	451	Whoever you are, how superb and how divine is your body, or any part of it!
LG1891	452	Whoever you are, to you endless announcements!
LG1891	453	Daughter of the lands did you wait for your poet?
LG1891	454	Did you wait for one with a flowing mouth and indicative hand?
LG1891	455	Toward the male of the States, and toward the female of the States,
LG1891	456	Exulting words, words to Democracy's lands.
LG1891	457	Interlink'd, food-yielding lands!
LG1891	458	Land of coal and iron! land of gold! land of cotton, sugar, rice!
LG1891	459	Land of wheat, beef, pork! land of wool and hemp! land of the apple and the grape!
LG1891	460	Land of the pastoral plains, the grass-fields of the world! land of those sweet-air'd interminable plateaus!
LG1891	461	Land of the herd, the garden, the healthy house of adobie!
LG1891	462	Lands where the north-west Columbia winds, and where the south-westsouth-west Colorado winds!
LG1891	463	Land of the eastern Chesapeake! land of the Delaware!
LG1891	464	Land of Ontario, Erie, Huron, Michigan!
LG1891	465	Land of the Old Thirteen! Massachusetts land! land of Vermont and Connecticut!
LG1891	466	Land of the ocean shores! land of sierras and peaks!
LG1891	467	Land of boatmen and sailors! fishermen's land!
LG1891	468	Inextricable lands! the clutch'd together! the passionate ones!
LG1891	469	The side by side! the elder and younger brothers! the bony-limb'dbony-limb'd!
LG1891	470	The great women's land! the feminine! the experienced sisters and the inexperienced sisters!
LG1891	471	Far breath'd land! Arctic braced! Mexican breez'd! the diverse!the compact!
LG1891	472	The Pennsylvanian! the Virginian! the double Carolinian!
LG1891	473	O all and each well-loved by me! my intrepid nations! O I at any rate include you all with perfect love!
LG1891	474	I cannot be discharged from you! not from one any sooner than another!
LG1891	475	O death! O for all that, I am yet of you unseen this hour with irrepressible love,
LG1891	476	Walking New England, a friend, a traveler,
LG1891	477	Splashing my bare feet in the edge of the summer ripples on Pau-manok'sPaumanok's sands,
LG1891	478	Crossing the prairies, dwelling again in Chicago, dwelling in every town,
LG1891	479	Observing shows, births, improvements, structures, arts,
LG1891	480	Listening to orators and oratresses in public halls,
LG1891	481	Of and through the States as during life, each man and woman my neighbor,
LG1891	482	The Louisianian, the Georgian, as near to me, and I as near to him and her,
LG1891	483	The Mississippian and Arkansian yet with me, and I yet with any of them,
LG1891	484	Yet upon the plains west of the spinal river, yet in my house of adobie,
LG1891	485	Yet returning eastward, yet in the Seaside State or in Maryland,
LG1891	486	Yet Kanadian cheerily braving the winter, the snow and ice wel-comewelcome to me,
LG1891	487	Yet a true son either of Maine or of the Granite State, or the Narragansett Bay State, or the Empire State,
LG1891	488	Yet sailing to other shores to annex the same, yet welcoming every new brother,
LG1891	489	Hereby applying these leaves to the new ones from the hour they unite with the old ones,
LG1891	490	Coming among the new ones myself to be their companion and equal, coming personally to you now,
LG1891	491	Enjoining you to acts, characters, spectacles, with me.
LG1891	492	With me with firm holding, yet haste, haste on.
LG1891	493	For your life adhere to me,
LG1891	494	(I may have to be persuaded many times before I consent to give myself really to you, but what of that?
LG1891	495	Must not Nature be persuaded many times?)
LG1891	496	No dainty dolce affettuoso I,
LG1891	497	Bearded, sun-burnt, gray-neck'd, forbidding, I have arrived,
LG1891	498	To be wrestled with as I pass for the solid prizes of the universe,
LG1891	499	For such I afford whoever can persevere to win them.
LG1891	500	On my way a moment I pause,
LG1891	501	Here for you! and here for America!
LG1891	502	Still the present I raise aloft, still the future of the States I harbinge glad and sublime,
LG1891	503	And for the past I pronounce what the air holds of the red aborigines.
LG1891	504	The red aborigines,
LG1891	505	Leaving natural breaths, sounds of rain and winds, calls as of birds and animals in the woods, syllabled to us for names,
LG1891	506	Okonee, Koosa, Ottawa, Monongahela, Sauk, Natchez, Chatta-hoocheeChattahoochee, Kaqueta, Oronoco,
LG1891	507	Wabash, Miami, Saginaw, Chippewa, Oshkosh, Walla-Walla,
LG1891	508	Leaving such to the States they melt, they depart, charging the water and the land with names.
LG1891	509	Expanding and swift, henceforth,
LG1891	510	Elements, breeds, adjustments, turbulent, quick and audacious,
LG1891	511	A world primal again, vistas of glory incessant and branching,
LG1891	512	A new race dominating previous ones and grander far, with new contests,
LG1891	513	New politics, new literatures and religions, new inventions and arts.
LG1891	514	These, my voice announcing—I will sleep no more but arise,
LG1891	515	You oceans that have been calm within me! how I feel you, fathom-lessfathomless, stirring, preparing unprecedented waves and storms.
LG1891	516	See, steamers steaming through my poems,
LG1891	517	See, in my poems immigrants continually coming and landing,
LG1891	518	See, in arriere, the wigwam, the trail, the hunter's hut, the flat-boat,the maize-leaf, the claim, the rude fence, and the backwoods village,
LG1891	519	See, on the one side the Western Sea and on the other the Eastern Sea, how they advance and retreat upon my poems as upon their own shores,
LG1891	520	See, pastures and forests in my poems—see, animals wild and tame—see, beyond the Kaw, countless herds of buffalo feeding on short curly grass,
LG1891	521	See, in my poems, cities, solid, vast, inland, with paved streets,with iron and stone edifices, ceaseless vehicles, and com-mercecommerce,
LG1891	522	See, the many-cylinder'd steam printing-press—see, the electric telegraph stretching across the continent,
LG1891	523	See, through Atlantica's depths pulses American Europe reaching,pulses of Europe duly return'd,
LG1891	524	See, the strong and quick locomotive as it departs, panting, blowing the steam-whistle,
LG1891	525	See, ploughmen ploughing farms—see, miners digging mines—see, the numberless factories,
LG1891	526	See, mechanics busy at their benches with tools—see from among them superior judges, philosophs, Presidents, emerge, drest in working dresses,
LG1891	527	See, lounging through the shops and fields of the States, me well-belov'dwell-belov'd, close-held by day and night,
LG1891	528	Hear the loud echoes of my songs there—read the hints come at last.
LG1891	529	O camerado close! O you and me at last, and us two only.
LG1891	530	O a word to clear one's path ahead endlessly!
LG1891	531	O something ecstatic and undemonstrable! O music wild!
LG1891	532	O now I triumph—and you shall also;
LG1891	533	O hand in hand—O wholesome pleasure—O one more desirer and lover!
LG1891	534	O to haste firm holding—to haste, haste on with me.
LG1891	535	I CELEBRATE myself, and sing myself,
LG1891	536	And what I assume you shall assume,
LG1891	537	For every atom belonging to me as good belongs to you.
LG1891	538	I loafe and invite my soul,
LG1891	539	I lean and loafe at my ease observing a spear of summer grass.
LG1891	540	My tongue, every atom of my blood, form'd from this soil, this air,
LG1891	541	Born here of parents born here from parents the same, and their parents the same,
LG1891	542	I, now thirty-seven years old in perfect health begin,
LG1891	543	Hoping to cease not till death.
LG1891	544	Creeds and schools in abeyance,
LG1891	545	Retiring back a while sufficed at what they are, but never forgotten,
LG1891	546	I harbor for good or bad, I permit to speak at every hazard,
LG1891	547	Nature without check with original energy.
LG1891	548	Houses and rooms are full of perfumes, the shelves are crowded with perfumes,
LG1891	549	I breathe the fragrance myself and know it and like it,
LG1891	550	The distillation would intoxicate me also, but I shall not let it.
LG1891	551	The atmosphere is not a perfume, it has no taste of the distillation,it is odorless,
LG1891	552	It is for my mouth forever, I am in love with it,
LG1891	553	I will go to the bank by the wood and become undisguised and naked,
LG1891	554	I am mad for it to be in contact with me.
LG1891	555	The smoke of my own breath,
LG1891	556	Echoes, ripples, buzz'd whispers, love-root, silk-thread, crotch and vine,
LG1891	557	My respiration and inspiration, the beating of my heart, the pass-ingpassing of blood and air through my lungs,
LG1891	558	The sniff of green leaves and dry leaves, and of the shore and dark-color'd sea-rocks, and of hay in the barn,
LG1891	559	The sound of the belch'd words of my voice loos'd to the eddies of the wind,
LG1891	560	A few light kisses, a few embraces, a reaching around of arms,
LG1891	561	The play of shine and shade on the trees as the supple boughs wag,
LG1891	562	The delight alone or in the rush of the streets, or along the fields and hill-sides,
LG1891	563	The feeling of health, the full-noon trill, the song of me rising from bed and meeting the sun.
LG1891	564	Have you reckon'd a thousand acres much? have you reckon'd the earth much?
LG1891	565	Have you practis'd so long to learn to read?
LG1891	566	Have you felt so proud to get at the meaning of poems?
LG1891	567	Stop this day and night with me and you shall possess the origin of all poems,
LG1891	568	You shall possess the good of the earth and sun, (there are millions of suns left,)
LG1891	569	You shall no longer take things at second or third hand, nor look through the eyes of the dead, nor feed on the spectres in books,
LG1891	570	You shall not look through my eyes either, nor take things from me,
LG1891	571	You shall listen to all sides and filter them from your self.
LG1891	572	I have heard what the talkers were talking, the talk of the begin-ningbeginning and the end,
LG1891	573	But I do not talk of the beginning or the end.
LG1891	574	There was never any more inception than there is now,
LG1891	575	Nor any more youth or age than there is now,
LG1891	576	And will never be any more perfection than there is now,
LG1891	577	Nor any more heaven or hell than there is now.
LG1891	578	Urge and urge and urge,
LG1891	579	Always the procreant urge of the world.
LG1891	580	Out of the dimness opposite equals advance, always substance and increase, always sex,
LG1891	581	Always a knit of identity, always distinction, always a breed of life.
LG1891	582	To elaborate is no avail, learn'd and unlearn'd feel that it is so.
LG1891	583	Sure as the most certain sure, plumb in the uprights, well entretied,braced in the beams,
LG1891	584	Stout as a horse, affectionate, haughty, electrical,
LG1891	585	I and this mystery here we stand.
LG1891	586	Clear and sweet is my soul, and clear and sweet is all that is not my soul.
LG1891	587	Lack one lacks both, and the unseen is proved by the seen,
LG1891	588	Till that becomes unseen and receives proof in its turn.
LG1891	589	Showing the best and dividing it from the worst age vexes age,
LG1891	590	Knowing the perfect fitness and equanimity of things, while they discuss I am silent, and go bathe and admire myself.
LG1891	591	Welcome is every organ and attribute of me, and of any man hearty and clean,
LG1891	592	Not an inch nor a particle of an inch is vile, and none shall be less familiar than the rest.
LG1891	593	I am satisfied—I see, dance, laugh, sing;
LG1891	594	As the hugging and loving bed-fellow sleeps at my side through the night, and withdraws at the peep of the day with stealthy tread,
LG1891	595	Leaving me baskets cover'd with white towels swelling the house with their plenty,
LG1891	596	Shall I postpone my acceptation and realization and scream at my eyes,
LG1891	597	That they turn from gazing after and down the road,
LG1891	598	And forthwith cipher and show me to a cent,
LG1891	599	Exactly the value of one and exactly the value of two, and which is ahead?
LG1891	600	Trippers and askers surround me,
LG1891	601	People I meet, the effect upon me of my early life or the ward and city I live in, or the nation,
LG1891	602	The latest dates, discoveries, inventions, societies, authors old and new,
LG1891	603	My dinner, dress, associates, looks, compliments, dues,
LG1891	604	The real or fancied indifference of some man or woman I love,
LG1891	605	The sickness of one of my folks or of myself, or ill-doing or loss or lack of money, or depressions or exaltations,
LG1891	606	Battles, the horrors of fratricidal war, the fever of doubtful news,the fitful events;
LG1891	607	These come to me days and nights and go from me again,
LG1891	608	But they are not the Me myself.
LG1891	609	Apart from the pulling and hauling stands what I am,
LG1891	610	Stands amused, complacent, compassionating, idle, unitary,
LG1891	611	Looks down, is erect, or bends an arm on an impalpable certain rest,
LG1891	612	Looking with side-curved head curious what will come next,
LG1891	613	Both in and out of the game and watching and wondering at it.
LG1891	614	Backward I see in my own days where I sweated through fog with linguists and contenders,
LG1891	615	I have no mockings or arguments, I witness and wait.
LG1891	616	I believe in you my soul, the other I am must not abase itself to you,
LG1891	617	And you must not be abased to the other.
LG1891	618	Loafe with me on the grass, loose the stop from your throat,
LG1891	619	Not words, not music or rhyme I want, not custom or lecture, not even the best,
LG1891	620	Only the lull I like, the hum of your valvèd voice.
LG1891	621	I mind how once we lay such a transparent summer morning,
LG1891	622	How you settled your head athwart my hips and gently turn'd over upon me,
LG1891	623	And parted the shirt from my bosom-bone, and plunged your tongue to my bare-stript heart,
LG1891	624	And reach'd till you felt my beard, and reach'd till you held my feet.
LG1891	625	Swiftly arose and spread around me the peace and knowledge that pass all the argument of the earth,
LG1891	626	And I know that the hand of God is the promise of my own,
LG1891	627	And I know that the spirit of God is the brother of my own,
LG1891	628	And that all the men ever born are also my brothers, and the women my sisters and lovers,
LG1891	629	And that a kelson of the creation is love,
LG1891	630	And limitless are leaves stiff or drooping in the fields,
LG1891	631	And brown ants in the little wells beneath them,
LG1891	632	And mossy scabs of the worm fence, heap'd stones, elder, mullein and poke-weed.
LG1891	633	A child said What is the grass? fetching it to me with full hands;
LG1891	634	How could I answer the child? I do not know what it is any more than he.
LG1891	635	I guess it must be the flag of my disposition, out of hopeful green stuff woven.
LG1891	636	Or I guess it is the handkerchief of the Lord,
LG1891	637	A scented gift and remembrancer designedly dropt,
LG1891	638	Bearing the owner's name someway in the corners, that we may see and remark, and say Whose?
LG1891	639	Or I guess the grass is itself a child, the produced babe of the vegetation.
LG1891	640	Or I guess it is a uniform hieroglyphic,
LG1891	641	And it means, Sprouting alike in broad zones and narrow zones,
LG1891	642	Growing among black folks as among white,
LG1891	643	Kanuck, Tuckahoe, Congressman, Cuff, I give them the same, I receive them the same.
LG1891	644	And now it seems to me the beautiful uncut hair of graves.
LG1891	645	Tenderly will I use you curling grass,
LG1891	646	It may be you transpire from the breasts of young men,
LG1891	647	It may be if I had known them I would have loved them,
LG1891	648	It may be you are from old people, or from offspring taken soon out of their mothers' laps,
LG1891	649	And here you are the mothers' laps.
LG1891	650	This grass is very dark to be from the white heads of old mothers,
LG1891	651	Darker than the colorless beards of old men,
LG1891	652	Dark to come from under the faint red roofs of mouths.
LG1891	653	O I perceive after all so many uttering tongues,
LG1891	654	And I perceive they do not come from the roofs of mouths for nothing.
LG1891	655	I wish I could translate the hints about the dead young men and women,
LG1891	656	And the hints about old men and mothers, and the offspring taken soon out of their laps.
LG1891	657	What do you think has become of the young and old men?
LG1891	658	And what do you think has become of the women and chil-drenchildren?
LG1891	659	They are alive and well somewhere,
LG1891	660	The smallest sprout shows there is really no death,
LG1891	661	And if ever there was it led forward life, and does not wait at the end to arrest it,
LG1891	662	And ceas'd the moment life appear'd.
LG1891	663	All goes onward and outward, nothing collapses,
LG1891	664	And to die is different from what any one supposed, and luckier.
LG1891	665	Has any one supposed it lucky to be born?
LG1891	666	I hasten to inform him or her it is just as lucky to die, and I know it.
LG1891	667	I pass death with the dying and birth with the new-wash'd babe,and am not contain'd between my hat and boots,
LG1891	668	And peruse manifold objects, no two alike and every one good,
LG1891	669	The earth good and the stars good, and their adjuncts all good.
LG1891	670	I am not an earth nor an adjunct of an earth,
LG1891	671	I am the mate and companion of people, all just as immortal and fathomless as myself,
LG1891	672	(They do not know how immortal, but I know.)
LG1891	673	Every kind for itself and its own, for me mine male and female,
LG1891	674	For me those that have been boys and that love women,
LG1891	675	For me the man that is proud and feels how it stings to be slighted,
LG1891	676	For me the sweet-heart and the old maid, for me mothers and the mothers of mothers,
LG1891	677	For me lips that have smiled, eyes that have shed tears,
LG1891	678	For me children and the begetters of children.
LG1891	679	Undrape! you are not guilty to me, nor stale nor discarded,
LG1891	680	I see through the broadcloth and gingham whether or no,
LG1891	681	And am around, tenacious, acquisitive, tireless, and cannot be shaken away.
LG1891	682	The little one sleeps in its cradle,
LG1891	683	I lift the gauze and look a long time, and silently brush away flies with my hand.
LG1891	684	The youngster and the red-faced girl turn aside up the bushy hill,
LG1891	685	I peeringly view them from the top.
LG1891	686	The suicide sprawls on the bloody floor of the bedroom,
LG1891	687	I witness the corpse with its dabbled hair, I note where the pistol has fallen.
LG1891	688	The blab of the pave, tires of carts, sluff of boot-soles, talk of the promenaders,
LG1891	689	The heavy omnibus, the driver with his interrogating thumb, the clank of the shod horses on the granite floor,
LG1891	690	The snow-sleighs, clinking, shouted jokes, pelts of snow-balls,
LG1891	691	The hurrahs for popular favorites, the fury of rous'd mobs,
LG1891	692	The flap of the curtain'd litter, a sick man inside borne to the hospital,
LG1891	693	The meeting of enemies, the sudden oath, the blows and fall,
LG1891	694	The excited crowd, the policeman with his star quickly working his passage to the centre of the crowd,
LG1891	695	The impassive stones that receive and return so many echoes,
LG1891	696	What groans of over-fed or half-starv'd who fall sunstruck or in fits,
LG1891	697	What exclamations of women taken suddenly who hurry home and give birth to babes,
LG1891	698	What living and buried speech is always vibrating here, what howls restrain'd by decorum,
LG1891	699	Arrests of criminals, slights, adulterous offers made, acceptances,rejections with convex lips,
LG1891	700	I mind them or the show or resonance of them—I come and I depart.
LG1891	701	The big doors of the country barn stand open and ready,
LG1891	702	The dried grass of the harvest-time loads the slow-drawn wagon,
LG1891	703	The clear light plays on the brown gray and green intertinged,
LG1891	704	The armfuls are pack'd to the sagging mow.
LG1891	705	I am there, I help, I came stretch'd atop of the load,
LG1891	706	I felt its soft jolts, one leg reclined on the other,
LG1891	707	I jump from the cross-beams and seize the clover and timothy,
LG1891	708	And roll head over heels and tangle my hair full of wisps.
LG1891	709	Alone far in the wilds and mountains I hunt,
LG1891	710	Wandering amazed at my own lightness and glee,
LG1891	711	In the late afternoon choosing a safe spot to pass the night,
LG1891	712	Kindling a fire and broiling the fresh-kill'd game,
LG1891	713	Falling asleep on the gather'd leaves with my dog and gun by my side.
LG1891	714	The Yankee clipper is under her sky-sails, she cuts the sparkle and scud,
LG1891	715	My eyes settle the land, I bend at her prow or shout joyously from the deck.
LG1891	716	The boatmen and clam-diggers arose early and stopt for me,
LG1891	717	I tuck'd my trowser-ends in my boots and went and had a good time;
LG1891	718	You should have been with us that day round the chowder-kettle.
LG1891	719	I saw the marriage of the trapper in the open air in the far west,the bride was a red girl,
LG1891	720	Her father and his friends sat near cross-legged and dumbly smoking, they had moccasins to their feet and large thick blankets hanging from their shoulders,
LG1891	721	On a bank lounged the trapper, he was drest mostly in skins, his luxuriant beard and curls protected his neck, he held his bride by the hand,
LG1891	722	She had long eyelashes, her head was bare, her coarse straight locks descended upon her voluptuous limbs and reach'd to her feet.
LG1891	723	The runaway slave came to my house and stopt outside,
LG1891	724	I heard his motions crackling the twigs of the woodpile,
LG1891	725	Through the swung half-door of the kitchen I saw him limpsy and weak,
LG1891	726	And went where he sat on a log and led him in and assured him,
LG1891	727	And brought water and fill'd a tub for his sweated body and bruis'd feet,
LG1891	728	And gave him a room that enter'd from my own, and gave him some coarse clean clothes,
LG1891	729	And remember perfectly well his revolving eyes and his awkwardness,
LG1891	730	And remember putting plasters on the galls of his neck and ankles;
LG1891	731	He staid with me a week before he was recuperated and pass'd north,
LG1891	732	I had him sit next me at table, my fire-lock lean'd in the corner.
LG1891	733	Twenty-eight young men bathe by the shore,
LG1891	734	Twenty-eight young men and all so friendly;
LG1891	735	Twenty-eight years of womanly life and all so lonesome.
LG1891	736	She owns the fine house by the rise of the bank,
LG1891	737	She hides handsome and richly drest aft the blinds of the window.
LG1891	738	Which of the young men does she like the best?
LG1891	739	Ah the homeliest of them is beautiful to her.
LG1891	740	Where are you off to, lady? for I see you,
LG1891	741	You splash in the water there, yet stay stock still in your room.
LG1891	742	Dancing and laughing along the beach came the twenty-ninth bather,
LG1891	743	The rest did not see her, but she saw them and loved them.
LG1891	744	The beards of the young men glisten'd with wet, it ran from their long hair,
LG1891	745	Little streams pass'd all over their bodies.
LG1891	746	An unseen hand also pass'd over their bodies,
LG1891	747	It descended tremblingly from their temples and ribs.
LG1891	748	The young men float on their backs, their white bellies bulge to the sun, they do not ask who seizes fast to them,
LG1891	749	They do not know who puffs and declines with pendant and bend-ingbending arch,
LG1891	750	They do not think whom they souse with spray.
LG1891	751	The butcher-boy puts off his killing-clothes, or sharpens his knife at the stall in the market,
LG1891	752	I loiter enjoying his repartee and his shuffle and break-down.
LG1891	753	Blacksmiths with grimed and hairy chests environ the anvil,
LG1891	754	Each has his main-sledge, they are all out, there is a great heat in the fire.
LG1891	755	From the cinder-strew'd threshold I follow their movements,
LG1891	756	The lithe sheer of their waists plays even with their massive arms,
LG1891	757	Overhand the hammers swing, overhand so slow, overhand so sure,
LG1891	758	They do not hasten, each man hits in his place.
LG1891	759	The negro holds firmly the reins of his four horses, the block swags underneath on its tied-over chain,
LG1891	760	The negro that drives the long dray of the stone-yard, steady and tall he stands pois'd on one leg on the string-piece,
LG1891	761	His blue shirt exposes his ample neck and breast and loosens over his hip-band,
LG1891	762	His glance is calm and commanding, he tosses the slouch of his hat away from his forehead,
LG1891	763	The sun falls on his crispy hair and mustache, falls on the black of his polish'd and perfect limbs.
LG1891	764	I behold the picturesque giant and love him, and I do not stop there,
LG1891	765	I go with the team also.
LG1891	766	In me the caresser of life wherever moving, backward as well as forward sluing,
LG1891	767	To niches aside and junior bending, not a person or object miss-ingmissing,
LG1891	768	Absorbing all to myself and for this song.
LG1891	769	Oxen that rattle the yoke and chain or halt in the leafy shade,what is that you express in your eyes?
LG1891	770	It seems to me more than all the print I have read in my life.
LG1891	771	My tread scares the wood-drake and wood-duck on my distant and day-long ramble,
LG1891	772	They rise together, they slowly circle around.
LG1891	773	I believe in those wing'd purposes,
LG1891	774	And acknowledge red, yellow, white, playing within me,
LG1891	775	And consider green and violet and the tufted crown intentional,
LG1891	776	And do not call the tortoise unworthy because she is not something else,
LG1891	777	And the jay in the woods never studied the gamut, yet trills pretty well to me,
LG1891	778	And the look of the bay mare shames silliness out of me.
LG1891	779	The wild gander leads his flock through the cool night,
LG1891	780	Ya-honk he says, and sounds it down to me like an invitation,
LG1891	781	The pert may suppose it meaningless, but I listening close,
LG1891	782	Find its purpose and place up there toward the wintry sky.
LG1891	783	The sharp-hoof'd moose of the north, the cat on the house-sill,the chickadee, the prairie-dog,
LG1891	784	The litter of the grunting sow as they tug at her teats,
LG1891	785	The brood of the turkey-hen and she with her half-spread wings,
LG1891	786	I see in them and myself the same old law.
LG1891	787	The press of my foot to the earth springs a hundred affections,
LG1891	788	They scorn the best I can do to relate them.
LG1891	789	I am enamour'd of growing out-doors,
LG1891	790	Of men that live among cattle or taste of the ocean or woods,
LG1891	791	Of the builders and steerers of ships and the wielders of axes and mauls, and the drivers of horses,
LG1891	792	I can eat and sleep with them week in and week out.
LG1891	793	What is commonest, cheapest, nearest, easiest, is Me,
LG1891	794	Me going in for my chances, spending for vast returns,
LG1891	795	Adorning myself to bestow myself on the first that will take me,
LG1891	796	Not asking the sky to come down to my good will,
LG1891	797	Scattering it freely forever.
LG1891	798	The pure contralto sings in the organ loft,
LG1891	799	The carpenter dresses his plank, the tongue of his foreplane whistles its wild ascending lisp,
LG1891	800	The married and unmarried children ride home to their Thanks-givingThanksgiving dinner,
LG1891	801	The pilot seizes the king-pin, he heaves down with a strong arm,
LG1891	802	The mate stands braced in the whale-boat, lance and harpoon are ready,
LG1891	803	The duck-shooter walks by silent and cautious stretches,
LG1891	804	The deacons are ordain'd with cross'd hands at the altar,
LG1891	805	The spinning-girl retreats and advances to the hum of the big wheel,
LG1891	806	The farmer stops by the bars as he walks on a First-day loafe and looks at the oats and rye,
LG1891	807	The lunatic is carried at last to the asylum a confirm'd case,
LG1891	808	(He will never sleep any more as he did in the cot in his mother's bed-room;)
LG1891	809	The jour printer with gray head and gaunt jaws works at his case,
LG1891	810	He turns his quid of tobacco while his eyes blurr with the manu-scriptmanuscript;
LG1891	811	The malform'd limbs are tied to the surgeon's table,
LG1891	812	What is removed drops horribly in a pail;
LG1891	813	The quadroon girl is sold at the auction-stand, the drunkard nods by the bar-room stove,
LG1891	814	The machinist rolls up his sleeves, the policeman travels his beat,the gate-keeper marks who pass,
LG1891	815	The young fellow drives the express-wagon, (I love him, though I do not know him;)
LG1891	816	The half-breed straps on his light boots to compete in the race,
LG1891	817	The western turkey-shooting draws old and young, some lean on their rifles, some sit on logs,
LG1891	818	Out from the crowd steps the marksman, takes his position, levels his piece;
LG1891	819	The groups of newly-come immigrants cover the wharf or levee,
LG1891	820	As the woolly-pates hoe in the sugar-field, the overseer views them from his saddle,
LG1891	821	The bugle calls in the ball-room, the gentlemen run for their part-nerspartners, the dancers bow to each other,
LG1891	822	The youth lies awake in the cedar-roof'd garret and harks to the musical rain,
LG1891	823	The Wolverine sets traps on the creek that helps fill the Huron,
LG1891	824	The squaw wrapt in her yellow-hemm'd cloth is offering moccasins and bead-bags for sale,
LG1891	825	The connoisseur peers along the exhibition-gallery with half-shut eyes bent sideways,
LG1891	826	As the deck-hands make fast the steamboat the plank is thrown for the shore-going passengers,
LG1891	827	The young sister holds out the skein while the elder sister winds it off in a ball, and stops now and then for the knots,
LG1891	828	The one-year wife is recovering and happy having a week ago borne her first child,
LG1891	829	The clean-hair'd Yankee girl works with her sewing-machine or in the factory or mill,
LG1891	830	The paving-man leans on his two-handed rammer, the reporter's lead flies swiftly over the note-book, the sign-painter is lettering with blue and gold,
LG1891	831	The canal boy trots on the tow-path, the book-keeper counts at his desk, the shoemaker waxes his thread,
LG1891	832	The conductor beats time for the band and all the performers follow him,
LG1891	833	The child is baptized, the convert is making his first professions,
LG1891	834	The regatta is spread on the bay, the race is begun, (how the white sails sparkle!)
LG1891	835	The drover watching his drove sings out to them that would stray,
LG1891	836	The pedler sweats with his pack on his back, (the purchaser hig-glinghiggling about the odd cent;)
LG1891	837	The bride unrumples her white dress, the minute-hand of the clock moves slowly,
LG1891	838	The opium-eater reclines with rigid head and just-open'd lips,
LG1891	839	The prostitute draggles her shawl, her bonnet bobs on her tipsy and pimpled neck,
LG1891	840	The crowd laugh at her blackguard oaths, the men jeer and wink to each other,
LG1891	841	(Miserable! I do not laugh at your oaths nor jeer you;)
LG1891	842	The President holding a cabinet council is surrounded by the great Secretaries,
LG1891	843	On the piazza walk three matrons stately and friendly with twined arms,
LG1891	844	The crew of the fish-smack pack repeated layers of halibut in the hold,
LG1891	845	The Missourian crosses the plains toting his wares and his cattle,
LG1891	846	As the fare-collector goes through the train he gives notice by the jingling of loose change,
LG1891	847	The floor-men are laying the floor, the tinners are tinning the roof,the masons are calling for mortar,
LG1891	848	In single file each shouldering his hod pass onward the laborers;
LG1891	849	Seasons pursuing each other the indescribable crowd is gather'd,it is the fourth of Seventh-month, (what salutes of cannon and small arms!)
LG1891	850	Seasons pursuing each other the plougher ploughs, the mower mows, and the winter-grain falls in the ground;
LG1891	851	Off on the lakes the pike-fisher watches and waits by the hole in the frozen surface,
LG1891	852	The stumps stand thick round the clearing, the squatter strikes deep with his axe,
LG1891	853	Flatboatmen make fast towards dusk near the cotton-wood or pecan-trees,
LG1891	854	Coon-seekers go through the regions of the Red river or through those drain'd by the Tennessee, or through those of the Arkansas,
LG1891	855	Torches shine in the dark that hangs on the Chattahooche or Altamahaw,
LG1891	856	Patriarchs sit at supper with sons and grandsons and great-grand-sonsgreat-grandsons around them,
LG1891	857	In walls of adobie, in canvas tents, rest hunters and trappers after their day's sport,
LG1891	858	The city sleeps and the country sleeps,
LG1891	859	The living sleep for their time, the dead sleep for their time,
LG1891	860	The old husband sleeps by his wife and the young husband sleeps by his wife;
LG1891	861	And these tend inward to me, and I tend outward to them,
LG1891	862	And such as it is to be of these more or less I am,
LG1891	863	And of these one and all I weave the song of myself.
LG1891	864	I am of old and young, of the foolish as much as the wise,
LG1891	865	Regardless of others, ever regardful of others,
LG1891	866	Maternal as well as paternal, a child as well as a man,
LG1891	867	Stuff'd with the stuff that is coarse and stuff'd with the stuff that is fine,
LG1891	868	One of the Nation of many nations, the smallest the same and the largest the same,
LG1891	869	A Southerner soon as a Northerner, a planter nonchalant and hospitable down by the Oconee I live,
LG1891	870	A Yankee bound my own way ready for trade, my joints the limberest joints on earth and the sternest joints on earth,
LG1891	871	A Kentuckian walking the vale of the Elkhorn in my deer-skin leggings, a Louisianian or Georgian,
LG1891	872	A boatman over lakes or bays or along coasts, a Hoosier, Badger,Buckeye;
LG1891	873	At home on Kanadian snow-shoes or up in the bush, or with fishermen off Newfoundland,
LG1891	874	At home in the fleet of ice-boats, sailing with the rest and tack-ingtacking,
LG1891	875	At home on the hills of Vermont or in the woods of Maine, or the Texan ranch,
LG1891	876	Comrade of Californians, comrade of free North-Westerners, (lov-ingloving their big proportions,)
LG1891	877	Comrade of raftsmen and coalmen, comrade of all who shake hands and welcome to drink and meat,
LG1891	878	A learner with the simplest, a teacher of the thoughtfullest,
LG1891	879	A novice beginning yet experient of myriads of seasons,
LG1891	880	Of every hue and caste am I, of every rank and religion,
LG1891	881	A farmer, mechanic, artist, gentleman, sailor, quaker,
LG1891	882	Prisoner, fancy-man, rowdy, lawyer, physician, priest.
LG1891	883	I resist any thing better than my own diversity,
LG1891	884	Breathe the air but leave plenty after me,
LG1891	885	And am not stuck up, and am in my place.
LG1891	886	(The moth and the fish-eggs are in their place,
LG1891	887	The bright suns I see and the dark suns I cannot see are in their place,
LG1891	888	The palpable is in its place and the impalpable is in its place.)
LG1891	889	These are really the thoughts of all men in all ages and lands, they are not original with me,
LG1891	890	If they are not yours as much as mine they are nothing, or next to nothing,
LG1891	891	If they are not the riddle and the untying of the riddle they are nothing,
LG1891	892	If they are not just as close as they are distant they are nothing.
LG1891	893	This is the grass that grows wherever the land is and the water is,
LG1891	894	This the common air that bathes the globe.
LG1891	895	With music strong I come, with my cornets and my drums,
LG1891	896	I play not marches for accepted victors only, I play marches for conquer'd and slain persons.
LG1891	897	Have you heard that it was good to gain the day?
LG1891	898	I also say it is good to fall, battles are lost in the same spirit in which they are won.
LG1891	899	I beat and pound for the dead,
LG1891	900	I blow through my embouchures my loudest and gayest for them.
LG1891	901	Vivas to those who have fail'd!
LG1891	902	And to those whose war-vessels sank in the sea!
LG1891	903	And to those themselves who sank in the sea!
LG1891	904	And to all generals that lost engagements, and all overcome heroes!
LG1891	905	And the numberless unknown heroes equal to the greatest heroes known!
LG1891	906	This is the meal equally set, this the meat for natural hunger,
LG1891	907	It is for the wicked just the same as the righteous, I make appoint-mentsappointments with all,
LG1891	908	I will not have a single person slighted or left away,
LG1891	909	The kept-woman, sponger, thief, are hereby invited,
LG1891	910	The heavy-lipp'd slave is invited, the venerealee is invited;
LG1891	911	There shall be no difference between them and the rest.
LG1891	912	This is the press of a bashful hand, this the float and odor of hair,
LG1891	913	This the touch of my lips to yours, this the murmur of yearning,
LG1891	914	This the far-off depth and height reflecting my own face,
LG1891	915	This the thoughtful merge of myself, and the outlet again.
LG1891	916	Do you guess I have some intricate purpose?
LG1891	917	Well I have, for the Fourth-month showers have, and the mica on the side of a rock has.
LG1891	918	Do you take it I would astonish?
LG1891	919	Does the daylight astonish? does the early redstart twittering through the woods?
LG1891	920	Do I astonish more than they?
LG1891	921	This hour I tell things in confidence,
LG1891	922	I might not tell everybody, but I will tell you.
LG1891	923	Who goes there? hankering, gross, mystical, nude;
LG1891	924	How is it I extract strength from the beef I eat?
LG1891	925	What is a man anyhow? what am I? what are you?
LG1891	926	All I mark as my own you shall offset it with your own,
LG1891	927	Else it were time lost listening to me.
LG1891	928	I do not snivel that snivel the world over,
LG1891	929	That months are vacuums and the ground but wallow and filth.
LG1891	930	Whimpering and truckling fold with powders for invalids, con-formityconformity goes to the fourth-remov'd,
LG1891	931	I wear my hat as I please indoors or out.
LG1891	932	Why should I pray? why should I venerate and be ceremonious?
LG1891	933	Having pried through the strata, analyzed to a hair, counsel'd with doctors and calculated close,
LG1891	934	I find no sweeter fat than sticks to my own bones.
LG1891	935	In all people I see myself, none more and not one a barley-corn less,
LG1891	936	And the good or bad I say of myself I say of them.
LG1891	937	I know I am solid and sound,
LG1891	938	To me the converging objects of the universe perpetually flow,
LG1891	939	All are written to me, and I must get what the writing means.
LG1891	940	I know I am deathless,
LG1891	941	I know this orbit of mine cannot be swept by a carpenter's compass,
LG1891	942	I know I shall not pass like a child's carlacue cut with a burnt stick at night.
LG1891	943	I know I am august,
LG1891	944	I do not trouble my spirit to vindicate itself or be understood,
LG1891	945	I see that the elementary laws never apologize,
LG1891	946	(I reckon I behave no prouder than the level I plant my house by,after all.)
LG1891	947	I exist as I am, that is enough,
LG1891	948	If no other in the world be aware I sit content,
LG1891	949	And if each and all be aware I sit content.
LG1891	950	One world is aware and by far the largest to me, and that is my-selfmyself,
LG1891	951	And whether I come to my own to-day or in ten thousand or ten million years,
LG1891	952	I can cheerfully take it now, or with equal cheerfulness I can wait.
LG1891	953	My foothold is tenon'd and mortis'd in granite,
LG1891	954	I laugh at what you call dissolution,
LG1891	955	And I know the amplitude of time.
LG1891	956	I am the poet of the Body and I am the poet of the Soul,
LG1891	957	The pleasures of heaven are with me and the pains of hell are with me,
LG1891	958	The first I graft and increase upon myself, the latter I translate into a new tongue.
LG1891	959	I am the poet of the woman the same as the man,
LG1891	960	And I say it is as great to be a woman as to be a man,
LG1891	961	And I say there is nothing greater than the mother of men.
LG1891	962	I chant the chant of dilation or pride,
LG1891	963	We have had ducking and deprecating about enough,
LG1891	964	I show that size is only development.
LG1891	965	Have you outstript the rest? are you the President?
LG1891	966	It is a trifle, they will more than arrive there every one, and still pass on.
LG1891	967	I am he that walks with the tender and growing night,
LG1891	968	I call to the earth and sea half-held by the night.
LG1891	969	Press close bare-bosom'd night—press close magnetic nourishing night!
LG1891	970	Night of south winds—night of the large few stars!
LG1891	971	Still nodding night—mad naked summer night.
LG1891	972	Smile O voluptuous cool-breath'd earth!
LG1891	973	Earth of the slumbering and liquid trees!
LG1891	974	Earth of departed sunset—earth of the mountains misty-topt!
LG1891	975	Earth of the vitreous pour of the full moon just tinged with blue!
LG1891	976	Earth of shine and dark mottling the tide of the river!
LG1891	977	Earth of the limpid gray of clouds brighter and clearer for my sake!
LG1891	978	Far-swooping elbow'd earth—rich apple-blossom'd earth!
LG1891	979	Smile, for your lover comes.
LG1891	980	Prodigal, you have given me love—therefore I to you give love!
LG1891	981	O unspeakable passionate love.
LG1891	982	You sea! I resign myself to you also—I guess what you mean,
LG1891	983	I behold from the beach your crooked inviting fingers,
LG1891	984	I believe you refuse to go back without feeling of me,
LG1891	985	We must have a turn together, I undress, hurry me out of sight of the land,
LG1891	986	Cushion me soft, rock me in billowy drowse,
LG1891	987	Dash me with amorous wet, I can repay you.
LG1891	988	Sea of stretch'd ground-swells,
LG1891	989	Sea breathing broad and convulsive breaths,
LG1891	990	Sea of the brine of life and of unshovell'd yet always-ready graves,
LG1891	991	Howler and scooper of storms, capricious and dainty sea,
LG1891	992	I am integral with you, I too am of one phase and of all phases.
LG1891	993	Partaker of influx and efflux I, extoller of hate and conciliation,
LG1891	994	Extoller of amies and those that sleep in each others' arms.
LG1891	995	I am he attesting sympathy,
LG1891	996	(Shall I make my list of things in the house and skip the house that supports them?)
LG1891	997	I am not the poet of goodness only, I do not decline to be the poet of wickedness also.
LG1891	998	What blurt is this about virtue and about vice?
LG1891	999	Evil propels me and reform of evil propels me, I stand indifferent,
LG1891	1000	My gait is no fault-finder's or rejecter's gait,
LG1891	1001	I moisten the roots of all that has grown.
LG1891	1002	Did you fear some scrofula out of the unflagging pregnancy?
LG1891	1003	Did you guess the celestial laws are yet to be work'd over and rectified?
LG1891	1004	I find one side a balance and the antipodal side a balance,
LG1891	1005	Soft doctrine as steady help as stable doctrine,
LG1891	1006	Thoughts and deeds of the present our rouse and early start.
LG1891	1007	This minute that comes to me over the past decillions,
LG1891	1008	There is no better than it and now.
LG1891	1009	What behaved well in the past or behaves well to-day is not such a wonder,
LG1891	1010	The wonder is always and always how there can be a mean man or an infidel.
LG1891	1011	Endless unfolding of words of ages!
LG1891	1012	And mine a word of the modern, the word En-Masse.
LG1891	1013	A word of the faith that never balks,
LG1891	1014	Here or henceforward it is all the same to me, I accept Time abso-lutelyabsolutely.
LG1891	1015	It alone is without flaw, it alone rounds and completes all,
LG1891	1016	That mystic baffling wonder alone completes all.
LG1891	1017	I accept Reality and dare not question it,
LG1891	1018	Materialism first and last imbuing.
LG1891	1019	Hurrah for positive science! long live exact demonstration!
LG1891	1020	Fetch stonecrop mixt with cedar and branches of lilac,
LG1891	1021	This is the lexicographer, this the chemist, this made a grammar of the old cartouches,
LG1891	1022	These mariners put the ship through dangerous unknown seas.
LG1891	1023	This is the geologist, this works with the scalpel, and this is a mathematician.
LG1891	1024	Gentlemen, to you the first honors always!
LG1891	1025	Your facts are useful, and yet they are not my dwelling,
LG1891	1026	I but enter by them to an area of my dwelling.
LG1891	1027	Less the reminders of properties told my words,
LG1891	1028	And more the reminders they of life untold, and of freedom and extrication,
LG1891	1029	And make short account of neuters and geldings, and favor men and women fully equipt,
LG1891	1030	And beat the gong of revolt, and stop with fugitives and them that plot and conspire.
LG1891	1031	Walt Whitman, a kosmos, of Manhattan the son,
LG1891	1032	Turbulent, fleshy, sensual, eating, drinking and breeding,
LG1891	1033	No sentimentalist, no stander above men and women or apart from them,
LG1891	1034	No more modest than immodest.
LG1891	1035	Unscrew the locks from the doors!
LG1891	1036	Unscrew the doors themselves from their jambs!
LG1891	1037	Whoever degrades another degrades me,
LG1891	1038	And whatever is done or said returns at last to me.
LG1891	1039	Through me the afflatus surging and surging, through me the cur-rentcurrent and index.
LG1891	1040	I speak the pass-word primeval, I give the sign of democracy,

LG1891	1053	I do not press my fingers across my mouth,
LG1891	1054	I keep as delicate around the bowels as around the head and heart,

LG1891	1056	I believe in the flesh and the appetites,

LG1891	1063	Shaded ledges and rests it shall be you!

LG1891	1696	Before I was born out of my mother generations guided me,
