LG1891	1698	For it the nebula cohered to an orb,
LG1891	1699	The long slow strata piled to rest it on,
LG1891	1700	Vast vegetables gave it sustenance,
LG1891	1701	Monstrous sauroids transported it in their mouths and deposited it with care.
LG1891	1702	All forces have been steadily employ'd to complete and delight me,
LG1891	1703	Now on this spot I stand with my robust soul.
LG1891	1704	O span of youth! ever-push'd elasticity!
LG1891	1705	O manhood, balanced, florid and full.
LG1891	1706	My lovers suffocate me,
LG1891	1707	Crowding my lips, thick in the pores of my skin,
LG1891	1708	Jostling me through streets and public halls, coming naked to me at night,
LG1891	1709	Crying by day Ahoy! from the rocks of the river, swinging and chirping over my head,
LG1891	1710	Calling my name from flower-beds, vines, tangled underbrush,
LG1891	1711	Lighting on every moment of my life,
LG1891	1712	Bussing my body with soft balsamic busses,
LG1891	1713	Noiselessly passing handfuls out of their hearts and giving them to be mine.
LG1891	1714	Old age superbly rising! O welcome, ineffable grace of dying days!
LG1891	1715	Every condition promulges not only itself, it promulges what grows after and out of itself,
LG1891	1716	And the dark hush promulges as much as any.
LG1891	1717	I open my scuttle at night and see the far-sprinkled systems,
LG1891	1718	And all I see multiplied as high as I can cipher edge but the rim of the farther systems.
LG1891	1719	Wider and wider they spread, expanding, always expanding,
LG1891	1720	Outward and outward and forever outward.
LG1891	1721	My sun has his sun and round him obediently wheels,
LG1891	1722	He joins with his partners a group of superior circuit,
LG1891	1723	And greater sets follow, making specks of the greatest inside them.
LG1891	1724	There is no stoppage and never can be stoppage,
LG1891	1725	If I, you, and the worlds, and all beneath or upon their surfaces,were this moment reduced back to a pallid float, it would not avail in the long run,
LG1891	1726	We should surely bring up again where we now stand,
LG1891	1727	And surely go as much farther, and then farther and farther.
LG1891	1728	A few quadrillions of eras, a few octillions of cubic leagues, do not hazard the span or make it impatient,
LG1891	1729	They are but parts, any thing is but a part.
LG1891	1730	See ever so far, there is limitless space outside of that,
LG1891	1731	Count ever so much, there is limitless time around that.
LG1891	1732	My rendezvous is appointed, it is certain,
LG1891	1733	The Lord will be there and wait till I come on perfect terms,
LG1891	1734	The great Camerado, the lover true for whom I pine will be there.
LG1891	1735	I know I have the best of time and space, and was never measured and never will be measured.
LG1891	1736	I tramp a perpetual journey, (come listen all!)
LG1891	1737	My signs are a rain-proof coat, good shoes, and a staff cut from the woods,
LG1891	1738	No friend of mine takes his ease in my chair,
LG1891	1739	I have no chair, no church, no philosophy,
LG1891	1740	I lead no man to a dinner-table, library, exchange,
LG1891	1741	But each man and each woman of you I lead upon a knoll,
LG1891	1742	My left hand hooking you round the waist,
LG1891	1743	My right hand pointing to landscapes of continents and the public road.
LG1891	1744	Not I, not any one else can travel that road for you,
LG1891	1745	You must travel it for yourself.
LG1891	1746	It is not far, it is within reach,
LG1891	1747	Perhaps you have been on it since you were born and did not know,
LG1891	1748	Perhaps it is everywhere on water and on land.
LG1891	1749	Shoulder your duds dear son, and I will mine, and let us hasten forth,

LG1891	1753	For after we start we never lie by again.
LG1891	1754	This day before dawn I ascended a hill and look'd at the crowded heaven,
LG1891	1755	And I said to my spirit When we become the enfolders of those orbs, and the pleasure and knowledge of every thing in them, shall we be fill'd and satisfied then?
LG1891	1756	And my spirit said No, we but level that lift to pass and continue beyond.
LG1891	1757	You are also asking me questions and I hear you,
LG1891	1758	I answer that I cannot answer, you must find out for yourself.

LG1891	1771	The boy I love, the same becomes a man not through derived power, but in his own right,

LG1891	1773	Fond of his sweetheart, relishing well his steak,

LG1891	1779	I follow you whoever you are from the present hour,

LG1891	1801	The girl and the wife rest the needle a moment and forget where they are,
LG1891	1802	They and all would resume what I have told them.
LG1891	1803	I have said that the soul is not more than the body,
LG1891	1804	And I have said that the body is not more than the soul,
LG1891	1805	And nothing, not God, is greater to one than one's self is,

LG1891	1981	I knew a man, a common farmer, the father of five sons,
LG1891	1982	And in them the fathers of sons, and in them the fathers of sons.
LG1891	1983	This man was of wonderful vigor, calmness, beauty of person,

LG1891	1986	He was six feet tall, he was over eighty years old, his sons were massive, clean, bearded, tan-faced, handsome,
LG1891	1987	They and his daughters loved him, all who saw him loved him,
LG1891	1988	They did not love him by allowance, they loved him with personal love,

LG1891	2341	Indeed O death, I think now these leaves mean precisely the same as you mean,
LG1891	2342	Grow up taller sweet leaves that I may see! grow up out of my breast!
LG1891	2343	Spring away from the conceal'd heart there!
LG1891	2344	Do not fold yourself so in your pink-tinged roots timid leaves!
LG1891	2345	Do not remain down there so ashamed, herbage of my breast!
LG1891	2346	Come I am determin'd to unbare this broad breast of mine, I have long enough stifled and choked;
LG1891	2347	Emblematic and capricious blades I leave you, now you serve me not,
LG1891	2348	I will say what I have to say by itself,
LG1891	2349	I will sound myself and comrades only, I will never again utter a call only their call,
LG1891	2350	I will raise with it immortal reverberations through the States,
LG1891	2351	I will give an example to lovers to take permanent shape and will through the States,
LG1891	2352	Through me shall the words be said to make death exhilarating,
LG1891	2353	Give me your tone therefore O death, that I may accord with it,
LG1891	2354	Give me yourself, for I see that you belong to me now above all,and are folded inseparably together, you love and death are,
LG1891	2355	Nor will I allow you to balk me any more with what I was calling life,
LG1891	2356	For now it is convey'd to me that you are the purports essential,
LG1891	2357	That you hide in these shifting forms of life, for reasons, and that they are mainly for you,
LG1891	2358	That you beyond them come forth to remain, the real reality,
LG1891	2359	That behind the mask of materials you patiently wait, no matter how long,
LG1891	2360	That you will one day perhaps take control of all,
LG1891	2361	That you will perhaps dissipate this entire show of appearance,
LG1891	2362	That may-be you are what it is all for, but it does not last so very long,
LG1891	2363	But you will last very long.
LG1891	2364	WHOEVER you are holding me now in hand,
LG1891	2365	Without one thing all will be useless,
LG1891	2366	I give you fair warning before you attempt me further,
LG1891	2367	I am not what you supposed, but far different.
LG1891	2368	Who is he that would become my follower?
LG1891	2369	Who would sign himself a candidate for my affections?
LG1891	2370	The way is suspicious, the result uncertain, perhaps destructive,
LG1891	2371	You would have to give up all else, I alone would expect to be your sole and exclusive standard,

LG1891	2373	The whole past theory of your life and all conformity to the lives around you would have to be abandon'd,
LG1891	2374	Therefore release me now before troubling yourself any further, let go your hand from my shoulders,
LG1891	2375	Put me down and depart on your way.
LG1891	2376	Or else by stealth in some wood for trial,
LG1891	2377	Or back of a rock in the open air,
LG1891	2378	(For in any roof'd room of a house I emerge not, nor in com-panycompany,
LG1891	2379	And in libraries I lie as one dumb, a gawk, or unborn, or dead,)
LG1891	2380	But just possibly with you on a high hill, first watching lest any person for miles around approach unawares,
LG1891	2381	Or possibly with you sailing at sea, or on the beach of the sea or some quiet island,
LG1891	2382	Here to put your lips upon mine I permit you,
LG1891	2383	With the comrade's long-dwelling kiss or the new husband's kiss,
LG1891	2384	For I am the new husband and I am the comrade.
LG1891	2385	Or if you will, thrusting me beneath your clothing,
LG1891	2386	Where I may feel the throbs of your heart or rest upon your hip,

LG1891	2388	For thus merely touching you is enough, is best,
LG1891	2389	And thus touching you would I silently sleep and be carried eternally.
LG1891	2390	But these leaves conning you con at peril,
LG1891	2391	For these leaves and me you will not understand,
LG1891	2392	They will elude you at first and still more afterward, I will certainly elude you,
LG1891	2393	Even while you should think you had unquestionably caught me, behold!
LG1891	2394	Already you see I have escaped from you.
LG1891	2395	For it is not for what I have put into it that I have written this book,
LG1891	2396	Nor is it by reading it you will acquire it,
LG1891	2397	Nor do those know me best who admire me and vauntingly praise me,
LG1891	2398	Nor will the candidates for my love (unless at most a very few)prove victorious,

LG1891	2400	For all is useless without that which you may guess at many times and not hit, that which I hinted at;
LG1891	2401	Therefore release me and depart on your way.
LG1891	2402	COME, I will make the continent indissoluble,
LG1891	2403	I will make the most splendid race the sun ever shone upon,
LG1891	2404	I will make divine magnetic lands,With the love of comrades,With the life-long love of comrades.
LG1891	2405	I will plant companionship thick as trees along all the rivers of America, and along the shores of the great lakes, and all over the prairies,
LG1891	2406	I will make inseparable cities with their arms about each other's necks,By the love of comrades,By the manly love of comrades.
LG1891	2407	For you these from me, O Democracy, to serve you ma femme!
LG1891	2408	For you, for you I am trilling these songs.
LG1891	2409	THESE I singing in spring collect for lovers,
LG1891	2410	(For who but I should understand lovers and all their sorrow and joy?
LG1891	2411	And who but I should be the poet of comrades?)
LG1891	2412	Collecting I traverse the garden the world, but soon I pass the gates,
LG1891	2413	Now along the pond-side, now wading in a little, fearing not the wet,
LG1891	2414	Now by the post-and-rail fences where the old stones thrown there,pick'd from the fields, have accumulated,
LG1891	2415	(Wild-flowers and vines and weeds come up through the stones and partly cover them, beyond these I pass,)
LG1891	2416	Far, far in the forest, or sauntering later in summer, before I think where I go,
LG1891	2417	Solitary, smelling the earthy smell, stopping now and then in the silence,
LG1891	2418	Alone I had thought, yet soon a troop gathers around me,
LG1891	2419	Some walk by my side and some behind, and some embrace my arms or neck,
LG1891	2420	They the spirits of dear friends dead or alive, thicker they come,a great crowd, and I in the middle,
LG1891	2421	Collecting, dispensing, singing, there I wander with them,
LG1891	2422	Plucking something for tokens, tossing toward whoever is near me,
LG1891	2423	Here, lilac, with a branch of pine,
LG1891	2424	Here, out of my pocket, some moss which I pull'd off a live-oak in Florida as it hung trailing down,

LG1891	2433	Wandering, point to or touch as I pass, or throw them loosely from me,

LG1891	2435	But what I drew from the water by the pond-side, that I reserve,
LG1891	2436	I will give of it, but only to them that love as I myself am capable of loving.
LG1891	2437	NOT heaving from my ribb'd breast only,
LG1891	2438	Not in sighs at night in rage dissatisfied with myself,
LG1891	2439	Not in those long-drawn, ill-supprest sighs,
LG1891	2440	Not in many an oath and promise broken,
LG1891	2441	Not in my wilful and savage soul's volition,
LG1891	2442	Not in the subtle nourishment of the air,

LG1891	2444	Not in the curious systole and diastole within which will one day cease,
LG1891	2445	Not in many a hungry wish told to the skies only,

LG1891	2452	Not in any or all of them O adhesiveness! O pulse of my life!
LG1891	2453	Need I that you exist and show yourself any more than in these songs.
LG1891	2454	OF the terrible doubt of appearances,
LG1891	2455	Of the uncertainty after all, that we may be deluded,
LG1891	2456	That may-be reliance and hope are but speculations after all,
LG1891	2457	That may-be identity beyond the grave is a beautiful fable only,

LG1891	2469	He ahold of my hand has completely satisfied me.
LG1891	2470	AND now gentlemen,
LG1891	2471	A word I give to remain in your memories and minds,
LG1891	2472	As base and finalè too for all metaphysics.
LG1891	2473	(So to the students the old professor,
LG1891	2474	At the close of his crowded course.)
LG1891	2475	Having studied the new and antique, the Greek and Germanic systems,
LG1891	2476	Kant having studied and stated, Fichte and Schelling and Hegel,
LG1891	2477	Stated the lore of Plato, and Socrates greater than Plato,

LG1891	2480	See the philosophies all, Christian churches and tenets see,

LG1891	2483	Of the well-married husband and wife, of children and parents,

LG1891	2485	RECORDERS ages hence,
LG1891	2486	Come, I will take you down underneath this impassive exterior, I will tell you what to say of me,
LG1891	2487	Publish my name and hang up my picture as that of the tenderest lover,
LG1891	2488	The friend the lover's portrait, of whom his friend his lover was fondest,
LG1891	2489	Who was not proud of his songs, but of the measureless ocean of love within him, and freely pour'd it forth,
LG1891	2490	Who often walk'd lonesome walks thinking of his dear friends, his lovers,
LG1891	2491	Who pensive away from one he lov'd often lay sleepless and dissat-isfieddissatisfied at night,

LG1891	2495	WHEN I heard at the close of the day how my name had been receiv'd with plaudits in the capitol, still it was not a happy night for me that follow'd,
LG1891	2496	And else when I carous'd, or when my plans were accomplish'd,still I was not happy,
LG1891	2497	But the day when I rose at dawn from the bed of perfect health,refresh'd, singing, inhaling the ripe breath of autumn,

LG1891	2500	And when I thought how my dear friend my lover was on his way coming, O then I was happy,
LG1891	2501	O then each breath tasted sweeter, and all that day my food nourish'd me more, and the beautiful day pass'd well,
LG1891	2502	And the next came with equal joy, and with the next at evening came my friend,

LG1891	2507	And his arm lay lightly around my breast—and that night I was happy.
LG1891	2508	ARE you the new person drawn toward me?
LG1891	2509	To begin with take warning, I am surely far different from what you suppose;

LG1891	2548	CITY of orgies, walks and joys,

LG1891	2564	I SAW in Louisiana a live-oak growing,
LG1891	2565	All alone stood it and the moss hung down from the branches,
LG1891	2566	Without any companion it grew there uttering joyous leaves of dark green,
LG1891	2567	And its look, rude, unbending, lusty, made me think of myself,
LG1891	2568	But I wonder'd how it could utter joyous leaves standing alone there without its friend near, for I knew I could not,

LG1891	2577	PASSING stranger! you do not know how longingly I look upon you,
LG1891	2578	You must be he I was seeking, or she I was seeking, (it comes to me as of a dream,)

LG1891	2580	All is recall'd as we flit by each other, fluid, affectionate, chaste,matured,

LG1891	2585	I am to wait, I do not doubt I am to meet you again,
LG1891	2586	I am to see to it that I do not lose you.
LG1891	2587	THIS moment yearning and thoughtful sitting alone,
LG1891	2588	It seems to me there are other men in other lands yearning and thoughtful,
LG1891	2589	It seems to me I can look over and behold them in Germany,Italy, France, Spain,

LG1891	2594	I HEAR it was charged against me that I sought to destroy institu-tionsinstitutions,

LG1891	2596	(What indeed have I in common with them? or what with the destruction of them?)

LG1891	2601	THE prairie-grass dividing, its special odor breathing,
LG1891	2602	I demand of it the spiritual corresponding,
LG1891	2603	Demand the most copious and close companionship of men,

LG1891	2611	WHEN I peruse the conquer'd fame of heroes and the victories of mighty generals, I do not envy the generals,

LG1891	2617	WE two boys together clinging,
LG1891	2618	One the other never leaving,
LG1891	2619	Up and down the roads going, North and South excursions making,

LG1891	2631	HERE the frailest leaves of me and yet my strongest lasting,

LG1891	2634	NO labor-saving machine,

LG1891	2641	A GLIMPSE through an interstice caught,

LG1891	2659	I DREAM'D in a dream I saw a city invincible to the attacks of the whole of the rest of the earth,
LG1891	2660	I dream'd that was the new city of Friends,
LG1891	2661	Nothing was greater there than the quality of robust love, it led the rest,
LG1891	2662	It was seen every hour in the actions of the men of that city,
LG1891	2663	And in all their looks and words.
LG1891	2664	WHAT think you I take my pen in hand to record?
LG1891	2665	The battle-ship, perfect-model'd, majestic, that I saw pass the offing to-day under full sail?
LG1891	2666	The splendors of the past day? or the splendor of the night that envelops me?
LG1891	2667	Or the vaunted glory and growth of the great city spread around me?—no;
LG1891	2668	But merely of two simple men I saw to-day on the pier in the midst of the crowd, parting the parting of dear friends,
LG1891	2669	The one to remain hung on the other's neck and passionately kiss'd him,
LG1891	2670	While the one to depart tightly prest the one to remain in his arms.
LG1891	2671	TO the East and to the West,
LG1891	2672	To the man of the Seaside State and of Pennsylvania,
LG1891	2673	To the KanadianCanadian of the north, to the Southerner I love,
LG1891	2674	These with perfect trust to depict you as myself, the germs are in all men,
LG1891	2675	I believe the main purport of these States is to found a superb friendship, exaltè, previously unknown,
LG1891	2676	Because I perceive it waits, and has been always waiting, latent in all men.
LG1891	2677	SOMETIMES with one I love I fill myself with rage for fear I effuse unreturn'd love,
LG1891	2678	But now I think there is no unreturn'd love, the pay is certain one way or another,
LG1891	2679	(I loved a certain person ardently and my love was not return'd,
LG1891	2680	Yet out of that I have written these songs.)
LG1891	2681	MANY things to absorb I teach to help you become eleve of mine;
LG1891	2682	Yet if blood like mine circle not in your veins,
LG1891	2683	If you be not silently selected by lovers and do not silently select lovers,
LG1891	2684	Of what use is it that you seek to become eleve of mine?
LG1891	2685	FAST-ANCHOR'D eternal O love! O woman I love!
LG1891	2686	O bride! O wife! more resistless than I can tell, the thought of you!
LG1891	2687	Then separate, as disembodied or another born,
LG1891	2688	Ethereal, the last athletic reality, my consolation,
LG1891	2689	I ascend, I float in the regions of your love O man,
LG1891	2690	O sharer of my roving life.
LG1891	2691	AMONG the men and women the multitude,
LG1891	2692	I perceive one picking me out by secret and divine signs,
LG1891	2693	Acknowledging none else, not parent, wife, husband, brother,child, any nearer than I am,
LG1891	2694	Some are baffled, but that one is not—that one knows me.
LG1891	2695	Ah lover and perfect equal,
LG1891	2696	I meant that you should discover me so by faint indirections,
LG1891	2697	And I when I meet you mean to discover you by the like in you.
LG1891	2698	O YOU whom I often and silently come where you are that I may be with you,
LG1891	2699	As I walk by your side or sit near, or remain in the same room with you,
LG1891	2700	Little you know the subtle electric fire that for your sake is play-ingplaying within me.
LG1891	2701	THAT shadow my likeness that goes to and fro seeking a liveli-hoodlivelihood, chattering, chaffering,
LG1891	2702	How often I find myself standing and looking at it where it flits,
LG1891	2703	How often I question and doubt whether that is really me;
LG1891	2704	But among my lovers and caroling these songs,
LG1891	2705	O I never doubt whether that is really me.
LG1891	2706	FULL of life now, compact, visible,
LG1891	2707	I, forty years old the eighty-third year of the States,
LG1891	2708	To one a century hence or any number of centuries hence,
LG1891	2709	To you yet unborn these, seeking you.
LG1891	2710	When you read these I that was visible am become invisible,
LG1891	2711	Now it is you, compact, visible, realizing my poems, seeking me,
LG1891	2712	Fancying how happy you were if I could be with you and become your comrade;
LG1891	2713	Be it as if I were with you. (Be not too certain but I am now with you.)
LG1891	2714	O TAKE my hand Walt Whitman!
LG1891	2715	Such gliding wonders! such sights and sounds!
LG1891	2716	Such join'd unended links, each hook'd to the next,
LG1891	2717	Each answering all, each sharing the earth with all.
LG1891	2718	What widens within you Walt Whitman?
LG1891	2719	What waves and soils exuding?
LG1891	2720	What climes? what persons and cities are here?
LG1891	2721	Who are the infants, some playing, some slumbering?
LG1891	2722	Who are the girls? who are the married women?
LG1891	2723	Who are the groups of old men going slowly with their arms about each other's necks?
LG1891	2724	What rivers are these? what forests and fruits are these?
LG1891	2725	What are the mountains call'd that rise so high in the mists?
LG1891	2726	What myriads of dwellings are they fill'd with dwellers?
LG1891	2727	Within me latitude widens, longitude lengthens,
LG1891	2728	Asia, Africa, Europe, are to the east—America is provided for in the west,
LG1891	2729	Banding the bulge of the earth winds the hot equator,
LG1891	2730	Curiously north and south turn the axis-ends,
LG1891	2731	Within me is the longest day, the sun wheels in slanting rings, it does not set for months,
LG1891	2732	Stretch'd in due time within me the midnight sun just rises above the horizon and sinks again,
LG1891	2733	Within me zones, seas, cataracts, forests, volcanoes, groups,
LG1891	2734	Malaysia, Polynesia, and the great West Indian islands.
LG1891	2735	What do you hear Walt Whitman?
LG1891	2736	I hear the workman singing and the farmer's wife singing,
LG1891	2737	I hear in the distance the sounds of children and of animals early in the day,
LG1891	2738	I hear emulous shouts of Australians pursuing the wild horse,
LG1891	2739	I hear the Spanish dance with castanets in the chestnut shade, to the rebeck and guitar,
LG1891	2740	I hear continual echoes from the Thames,
LG1891	2741	I hear fierce French liberty songs,
LG1891	2742	I hear of the Italian boat-sculler the musical recitative of old poems,
LG1891	2743	I hear the locusts in Syria as they strike the grain and grass with the showers of their terrible clouds,
LG1891	2744	I hear the Coptic refrain toward sundown, pensively falling on the breast of the black venerable vast mother the Nile,
LG1891	2745	I hear the chirp of the Mexican muleteer, and the bells of the mule,
LG1891	2746	I hear the Arab muezzin calling from the top of the mosque,
LG1891	2747	I hear the Christian priests at the altars of their churches, I hear the responsive base and soprano,

LG1891	2749	I hear the wheeze of the slave-coffle as the slaves march on, as the husky gangs pass on by twos and threes, fasten'd together with wrist-chains and ankle-chains,

LG1891	2751	I hear the rhythmic myths of the Greeks, and the strong legends of the Romans,

LG1891	2754	What do you see Walt Whitman?
LG1891	2755	Who are they you salute, and that one after another salute you?
LG1891	2756	I see a great round wonder rolling through space,
LG1891	2757	I see diminute farms, hamlets, ruins, graveyards, jails, factories,palaces, hovels, huts of barbarians, tents of nomads upon the surface,
LG1891	2758	I see the shaded part on one side where the sleepers are sleeping,and the sunlit part on the other side,
LG1891	2759	I see the curious rapid change of the light and shade,
LG1891	2760	I see distant lands, as real and near to the inhabitants of them as my land is to me.
LG1891	2761	I see plenteous waters,
LG1891	2762	I see mountain peaks, I see the sierras of Andes where they range,

LG1891	2777	Some are in storms, some in the night with the watch on the look-outlookout,

LG1891	2876	You whoever you are!

LG1891	2924	(You will come forward in due time to my side.)
LG1891	2925	My spirit has pass'd in compassion and determination around the whole earth,
LG1891	2926	I have look'd for equals and lovers and found them ready for me in all lands,
LG1891	2927	I think some divine rapport has equalized me with them.
LG1891	2928	You vapors, I think I have risen with you, moved away to distant continents, and fallen down there, for reasons,
LG1891	2929	I think I have blown with you you winds;
LG1891	2930	You waters I have finger'd every shore with you,
LG1891	2931	I have run through what any river or strait of the globe has run through,
LG1891	2932	I have taken my stand on the bases of peninsulas and on the high embedded rocks, to cry thence:
LG1891	2933	Salut au monde!
LG1891	2934	What cities the light or warmth penetrates I penetrate those cities myself,
LG1891	2935	All islands to which birds wing their way I wing my way myself.
LG1891	2936	Toward you all, in America's name,
LG1891	2937	I raise high the perpendicular hand, I make the signal,
LG1891	2938	To remain after me in sight forever,
LG1891	2939	For all the haunts and homes of men.
LG1891	2940	AFOOT and light-hearted I take to the open road,
LG1891	2941	Healthy, free, the world before me,
LG1891	2942	The long brown path before me leading wherever I choose.
LG1891	2943	Henceforth I ask not good-fortune, I myself am good-fortune,
LG1891	2944	Henceforth I whimper no more, postpone no more, need nothing,
LG1891	2945	Done with indoor complaints, libraries, querulous criticisms,
LG1891	2946	Strong and content I travel the open road.
LG1891	2947	The earth, that is sufficient,
LG1891	2948	I do not want the constellations any nearer,
LG1891	2949	I know they are very well where they are,
LG1891	2950	I know they suffice for those who belong to them.
LG1891	2951	(Still here I carry my old delicious burdens,
LG1891	2952	I carry them, men and women, I carry them with me wherever I go,
LG1891	2953	I swear it is impossible for me to get rid of them,
LG1891	2954	I am fill'd with them, and I will fill them in return.)
LG1891	2955	You road I enter upon and look around, I believe you are not all that is here,
LG1891	2956	I believe that much unseen is also here.
LG1891	2957	Here the profound lesson of reception, nor preference nor denial,
LG1891	2958	The black with his woolly head, the felon, the diseas'd, the illiterate person, are not denied;
LG1891	2959	The birth, the hasting after the physician, the beggar's tramp, the drunkard's stagger, the laughing party of mechanics,
LG1891	2960	The escaped youth, the rich person's carriage, the fop, the eloping couple,
LG1891	2961	The early market-man, the hearse, the moving of furniture into the town, the return back from the town,
LG1891	2962	They pass, I also pass, any thing passes, none can be interdicted,
LG1891	2963	None but are accepted, none but shall be dear to me.
LG1891	2964	You air that serves me with breath to speak!
LG1891	2965	You objects that call from diffusion my meanings and give them shape!
LG1891	2966	You light that wraps me and all things in delicate equable showers!
LG1891	2967	You paths worn in the irregular hollows by the roadsides!
LG1891	2968	I believe you are latent with unseen existences, you are so dear to me.
LG1891	2969	You flagg'd walks of the cities! you strong curbs at the edges!
LG1891	2970	You ferries! you planks and posts of wharves! you timber-lined sides! you distant ships!

LG1891	2977	From the living and the dead you have peopled your impassive surfaces, and the spirits thereof would be evident and amicable with me.
LG1891	2978	The earth expanding right hand and left hand,
LG1891	2979	The picture alive, every part in its best light,
LG1891	2980	The music falling in where it is wanted, and stopping where it is not wanted,

LG1891	2982	O highway I travel, do you say to me Do not leave me?
LG1891	2983	Do you say Venture not—if you leave me you are lost?
LG1891	2984	Do you say I am already prepared, I am well-beaten and un-deniedundenied, adhere to me?

LG1891	2986	You express me better than I can express myself,
LG1891	2987	You shall be more to me than my poem.
LG1891	2988	I think heroic deeds were all conceiv'd in the open air, and all free poems also,
LG1891	2989	I think I could stop here myself and do miracles,
LG1891	2990	I think whatever I shall meet on the road I shall like, and who-everwhoever beholds me shall like me,
LG1891	2991	I think whoever I see must be happy.
LG1891	2992	From this hour I ordain myself loos'd of limits and imaginary lines,
LG1891	2993	Going where I list, my own master total and absolute,
LG1891	2994	Listening to others, considering well what they say,
LG1891	2995	Pausing, searching, receiving, contemplating,
LG1891	2996	Gently, but with undeniable will, divesting myself of the holds that would hold me.
LG1891	2997	I inhale great draughts of space,
LG1891	2998	The east and the west are mine, and the north and the south are mine.
LG1891	2999	I am larger, better than I thought,
LG1891	3000	I did not know I held so much goodness.
LG1891	3001	All seems beautiful to me,
LG1891	3002	I can repeat over to men and women You have done such good to me I would do the same to you,
LG1891	3003	I will recruit for myself and you as I go,
LG1891	3004	I will scatter myself among men and women as I go,
LG1891	3005	I will toss a new gladness and roughness among them,
LG1891	3006	Whoever denies me it shall not trouble me,
LG1891	3007	Whoever accepts me he or she shall be blessed and shall bless me.
LG1891	3008	Now if a thousand perfect men were to appear it would not amaze me,
LG1891	3009	Now if a thousand beautiful forms of women appear'd it would not astonish me.
LG1891	3010	Now I see the secret of the making of the best persons,
LG1891	3011	It is to grow in the open air and to eat and sleep with the earth.
LG1891	3012	Here a great personal deed has room,
LG1891	3013	(Such a deed seizes upon the hearts of the whole race of men,
LG1891	3014	Its effusion of strength and will overwhelms law and mocks all authority and all argument against it.)
LG1891	3015	Here is the test of wisdom,
LG1891	3016	Wisdom is not finally tested in schools,
LG1891	3017	Wisdom cannot be pass'd from one having it to another not having it,
LG1891	3018	Wisdom is of the soul, is not susceptible of proof, is its own proof,
LG1891	3019	Applies to all stages and objects and qualities and is content,
LG1891	3020	Is the certainty of the reality and immortality of things, and the excellence of things;
LG1891	3021	Something there is in the float of the sight of things that provokes it out of the soul.
LG1891	3022	Now I re-examine philosophies and religions,
LG1891	3023	They may prove well in lecture-rooms, yet not prove at all under the spacious clouds and along the landscape and flowing currents.
LG1891	3024	Here is realization,
LG1891	3025	Here is a man tallied—he realizes here what he has in him,
LG1891	3026	The past, the future, majesty, love—if they are vacant of you,you are vacant of them.
LG1891	3027	Only the kernel of every object nourishes;
LG1891	3028	Where is he who tears off the husks for you and me?
LG1891	3029	Where is he that undoes stratagems and envelopes for you and me?
LG1891	3030	Here is adhesiveness, it is not previously fashion'd, it is apropos;
LG1891	3031	Do you know what it is as you pass to be loved by strangers?
LG1891	3032	Do you know the talk of those turning eye-balls?
LG1891	3033	Here is the efflux of the soul,
LG1891	3034	The efflux of the soul comes from within through embower'd gates, ever provoking questions,
LG1891	3035	These yearnings why are they? these thoughts in the darkness why are they?
LG1891	3036	Why are there men and women that while they are nigh me the sunlight expands my blood?
LG1891	3037	Why when they leave me do my pennants of joy sink flat and lank?
LG1891	3038	Why are there trees I never walk under but large and melodious thoughts descend upon me?
LG1891	3039	(I think they hang there winter and summer on those trees and always drop fruit as I pass;)
LG1891	3040	What is it I interchange so suddenly with strangers?
LG1891	3041	What with some driver as I ride on the seat by his side?
LG1891	3042	What with some fisherman drawing his seine by the shore as I walk by and pause?
LG1891	3043	What gives me to be free to a woman's and man's good-will?what gives them to be free to mine?
LG1891	3044	The efflux of the soul is happiness, here is happiness,
LG1891	3045	I think it pervades the open air, waiting at all times,
LG1891	3046	Now it flows unto us, we are rightly charged.
LG1891	3047	Here rises the fluid and attaching character,
LG1891	3048	The fluid and attaching character is the freshness and sweetness of man and woman,
LG1891	3049	(The herbs of the morning sprout no fresher and sweeter every day out of the roots of themselves, than it sprouts fresh and sweet continually out of itself.)
LG1891	3050	Toward the fluid and attaching character exudes the sweat of the love of young and old,
LG1891	3051	From it falls distill'd the charm that mocks beauty and attainments,
LG1891	3052	Toward it heaves the shuddering longing ache of contact.
LG1891	3053	Allons! whoever you are come travel with me!
LG1891	3054	Traveling with me you find what never tires.
LG1891	3055	The earth never tires,
LG1891	3056	The earth is rude, silent, incomprehensible at first, Nature is rude and incomprehensible at first,
LG1891	3057	Be not discouraged, keep on, there are divine things well envelop'd,
LG1891	3058	I swear to you there are divine things more beautiful than words can tell.

LG1891	3060	However sweet these laid-up stores, however convenient this dwell-ingdwelling we cannot remain here,

LG1891	3064	We will sail pathless and wild seas,
LG1891	3065	We will go where winds blow, waves dash, and the Yankee clipper speeds by under full sail.
LG1891	3066	Allons! with power, liberty, the earth, the elements,
LG1891	3067	Health, defiance, gayety, self-esteem, curiosity;
LG1891	3068	Allons! from all formules!
LG1891	3069	From your formules, O bat-eyed and materialistic priests.
LG1891	3070	The stale cadaver blocks up the passage—the burial waits no longer.
LG1891	3071	Allons! yet take warning!
LG1891	3072	He traveling with me needs the best blood, thews, endurance,
LG1891	3073	None may come to the trial till he or she bring courage and health,
LG1891	3074	Come not here if you have already spent the best of yourself,
LG1891	3075	Only those may come who come in sweet and determin'd bodies,
LG1891	3076	No diseas'd person, no rum-drinker or venereal taint is permitted here.
LG1891	3077	(I and mine do not convince by arguments, similes, rhymes,
LG1891	3078	We convince by our presence.)
LG1891	3079	Listen! I will be honest with you,
LG1891	3080	I do not offer the old smooth prizes, but offer rough new prizes,
LG1891	3081	These are the days that must happen to you:
LG1891	3082	You shall not heap up what is call'd riches,
LG1891	3083	You shall scatter with lavish hand all that you earn or achieve,

LG1891	3087	You shall not allow the hold of those who spread their reach'd hands toward you.
LG1891	3088	Allons! after the great Companions, and to belong to them!
LG1891	3089	They too are on the road—they are the swift and majestic men—they are the greatest women,
LG1891	3090	Enjoyers of calms of seas and storms of seas,
LG1891	3091	Sailors of many a ship, walkers of many a mile of land,
LG1891	3092	Habituès of many distant countries, habituès of far-distant dwellings,

LG1891	3105	Allons! to that which is endless as it was beginningless,
LG1891	3106	To undergo much, tramps of days, rests of nights,

LG1891	3120	All parts away for the progress of souls,

LG1891	3122	Of the progress of the souls of men and women along the grand roads of the universe, all other progress is the needed emblem and sustenance.
LG1891	3123	Forever alive, forever forward,
LG1891	3124	Stately, solemn, sad, withdrawn, baffled, mad, turbulent, feeble,dissatisfied,

LG1891	3128	Whoever you are, come forth! or man or woman come forth!

LG1891	3131	It is useless to protest, I know all and expose it.
LG1891	3132	Behold through you as bad as the rest,

LG1891	3283	Appearances, now or henceforth, indicate what you are,
LG1891	3284	You necessary film, continue to envelop the soul,
LG1891	3285	About my body for me, and your body for you, be hung our divinest aromas,
LG1891	3286	Thrive, cities—bring your freight, bring your shows, ample and sufficient rivers,
LG1891	3287	Expand, being than which none else is perhaps more spiritual,
LG1891	3288	Keep your places, objects than which none else is more lasting.
LG1891	3289	You have waited, you always wait, you dumb, beautiful ministers,
LG1891	3290	We receive you with free sense at last, and are insatiate hence-forwardhenceforward,
LG1891	3291	Not you any more shall be able to foil us, or withhold yourselves from us,
LG1891	3292	We use you, and do not cast you aside—we plant you perma-nentlypermanently within us,
LG1891	3293	We fathom you not—we love you—there is perfection in you also,
LG1891	3294	You furnish your parts toward eternity,
LG1891	3295	Great or small, you furnish your parts toward the soul.
LG1891	3296	NOW list to my morning's romanza, I tell the signs of the Answerer,
LG1891	3297	To the cities and farms I sing as they spread in the sunshine before me.
LG1891	3298	A young man comes to me bearing a message from his brother,
LG1891	3299	How shall the young man know the whether and when of his brother?
LG1891	3300	Tell him to send me the signs.
LG1891	3301	And I stand before the young man face to face, and take his right hand in my left hand and his left hand in my right hand,
LG1891	3302	And I answer for his brother and for men, and I answer for him that answers for all, and send these signs.
LG1891	3303	Him all wait for, him all yield up to, his word is decisive and final,
LG1891	3304	Him they accept, in him lave, in him perceive themselves as amid light,
LG1891	3305	Him they immerse and he immerses them.
LG1891	3306	Beautiful women, the haughtiest nations, laws, the landscape,people, animals,
LG1891	3307	The profound earth and its attributes and the unquiet ocean, (so tell I my morning's romanza,)
LG1891	3308	All enjoyments and properties and money, and whatever money will buy,
LG1891	3309	The best farms, others toiling and planting and he unavoidably reaps,
LG1891	3310	The noblest and costliest cities, others grading and building and he domiciles there,
LG1891	3311	Nothing for any one but what is for him, near and far are for him,the ships in the offing,
LG1891	3312	The perpetual shows and marches on land are for him if they are for anybody.
LG1891	3313	He puts things in their attitudes,
LG1891	3314	He puts to-day out of himself with plasticity and love,
LG1891	3315	He places his own times, reminiscences, parents, brothers and sisters, associations, employment, politics, so that the rest never shame them afterward, nor assume to command them.
LG1891	3316	He is the Answerer,
LG1891	3317	What can be answer'd he answers, and what cannot be answer'd he shows how it cannot be answer'd.
LG1891	3318	A man is a summons and challenge,
LG1891	3319	(It is vain to skulk—do you hear that mocking and laughter? do you hear the ironical echoes?)
LG1891	3320	Books, friendships, philosophers, priests, action, pleasure, pride,beat up and down seeking to give satisfaction,
LG1891	3321	He indicates the satisfaction, and indicates them that beat up and down also.
LG1891	3322	Whichever the sex, whatever the season or place, he may go freshly and gently and safely by day or by night,
LG1891	3323	He has the pass-key of hearts, to him the response of the prying of hands on the knobs.
LG1891	3324	His welcome is universal, the flow of beauty is not more welcome or universal than he is,
LG1891	3325	The person he favors by day or sleeps with at night is blessed.
LG1891	3326	Every existence has its idiom, every thing has an idiom and tongue,
LG1891	3327	He resolves all tongues into his own and bestows it upon men, and any man translates, and any man translates himself also,
LG1891	3328	One part does not counteract another part, he is the joiner, he sees how they join.
LG1891	3329	He says indifferently and alike How are you friend? to the President at his levee,
LG1891	3330	And he says Good-day my brother, to Cudge that hoes in the sugar-field,
LG1891	3331	And both understand him and know that his speech is right.
LG1891	3332	He walks with perfect ease in the capitol,
LG1891	3333	He walks among the Congress, and one Representative says to another, Here is our equal appearing and new.
LG1891	3334	Then the mechanics take him for a mechanic,
LG1891	3335	And the soldiers suppose him to be a soldier, and the sailors that he has follow'd the sea,
LG1891	3336	And the authors take him for an author, and the artists for an artist,
LG1891	3337	And the laborers perceive he could labor with them and love them,
LG1891	3338	No matter what the work is, that he is the one to follow it or has follow'd it,
LG1891	3339	No matter what the nation, that he might find his brothers and sisters there.
LG1891	3340	The English believe he comes of their English stock,
LG1891	3341	A Jew to the Jew he seems, a Russ to the Russ, usual and near,removed from none.
LG1891	3342	Whoever he looks at in the traveler's coffee-house claims him,
LG1891	3343	The Italian or Frenchman is sure, the German is sure, the Spaniard is sure, and the island Cuban is sure,
LG1891	3344	The engineer, the deck-hand on the great lakes, or on the Missis-sippi or St. Lawrence or Sacramento, or Hudson or Pau-manokPaumanok sound, claims him.
LG1891	3345	The gentleman of perfect blood acknowledges his perfect blood,
LG1891	3346	The insulter, the prostitute, the angry person, the beggar, see themselves in the ways of him, he strangely transmutes them,
LG1891	3347	They are not vile any more, they hardly know themselves they are so grown.
LG1891	3348	The indications and tally of time,
LG1891	3349	Perfect sanity shows the master among philosophs,
LG1891	3350	Time, always without break, indicates itself in parts,
LG1891	3351	What always indicates the poet is the crowd of the pleasant com-panycompany of singers, and their words,
LG1891	3352	The words of the singers are the hours or minutes of the light or dark, but the words of the maker of poems are the general light and dark,
LG1891	3353	The maker of poems settles justice, reality, immortality,
LG1891	3354	His insight and power encircle things and the human race,
LG1891	3355	He is the glory and extract thus far of things and of the human race.
LG1891	3356	The singers do not beget, only the Poet begets,
LG1891	3357	The singers are welcom'd, understood, appear often enough, but rare has the day been, likewise the spot, of the birth of the maker of poems, the Answerer,
LG1891	3358	(Not every century nor every five centuries has contain'd such a day, for all its names.)
LG1891	3359	The singers of successive hours of centuries may have ostensible names, but the name of each of them is one of the singers,
LG1891	3360	The name of each is, eye-singer, ear-singer, head-singer, sweet-singersweet-singer, night-singer, parlor-singer, love-singer, weird-singer,or something else.
LG1891	3361	All this time and at all times wait the words of true poems,
LG1891	3362	The words of true poems do not merely please,
LG1891	3363	The true poets are not followers of beauty but the august masters of beauty;
LG1891	3364	The greatness of sons is the exuding of the greatness of mothers and fathers,
LG1891	3365	The words of true poems are the tuft and final applause of science.
LG1891	3366	Divine instinct, breadth of vision, the law of reason, health, rudeness of body, withdrawnness,
LG1891	3367	Gayety, sun-tan, air-sweetness, such are some of the words of poems.
LG1891	3368	The sailor and traveler underlie the makers of poems, the Answerer,
LG1891	3369	The builder, geometer, chemist, anatomist, phrenologist, artist, all these underlie the maker of poems, the Answerer.
LG1891	3370	The words of the true poems give you more than poems,
LG1891	3371	They give you to form for yourself poems, religions, politics, war,peace, behavior, histories, essays, daily life, and every thing else,
LG1891	3372	They balance ranks, colors, races, creeds, and the sexes,
LG1891	3373	They do not seek beauty, they are sought,
LG1891	3374	Forever touching them or close upon them follows beauty, longing,fain, love-sick.
LG1891	3375	They prepare for death, yet are they not the finish, but rather the outset,
LG1891	3376	They bring none to his or her terminus or to be content and full,
LG1891	3377	Whom they take they take into space to behold the birth of stars,to learn one of the meanings,
LG1891	3378	To launch off with absolute faith, to sweep through the ceaseless rings and never be quiet again.
LG1891	3379	ALWAYS our old feuillage!
LG1891	3380	Always Florida's green peninsula—always the priceless delta of Louisiana—always the cotton-fields of Alabama and Texas,
LG1891	3381	Always California's golden hills and hollows, and the silver moun-tainsmountains of New Mexico—always soft-breath'd Cuba,
LG1891	3382	Always the vast slope drain'd by the Southern sea, inseparable with the slopes drain'd by the Eastern and Western seas,
LG1891	3383	The area the eighty-third year of these States, the three and a half millions of square miles,
LG1891	3384	The eighteen thousand miles of sea-coast and bay-coast on the main, the thirty thousand miles of river navigation,
LG1891	3385	The seven millions of distinct families and the same number of dwellings—always these, and more, branching forth into numberless branches,
LG1891	3386	Always the free range and diversity—always the continent of Democracy;
LG1891	3387	Always the prairies, pastures, forests, vast cities, travelers, KanadaCanada, the snows;
LG1891	3388	Always these compact lands tied at the hips with the belt stringing the huge oval lakes;
LG1891	3389	Always the West with strong native persons, the increasing density there, the habitans, friendly, threatening, ironical, scorning invaders;
LG1891	3390	All sights, South, North, East—all deeds, promiscuously done at all times,
LG1891	3391	All characters, movements, growths, a few noticed, myriads unno-ticedunnoticed,
LG1891	3392	Through Mannahatta's streets I walking, these things gathering,
LG1891	3393	On interior rivers by night in the glare of pine knots, steamboats wooding up,
LG1891	3394	Sunlight by day on the valley of the Susquehanna, and on the valleys of the Potomac and Rappahannock, and the valleys of the Roanoke and Delaware,
LG1891	3395	In their northerly wilds beasts of prey haunting the Adirondacks the hills, or lapping the Saginaw waters to drink,
LG1891	3396	In a lonesome inlet a sheldrake lost from the flock, sitting on the water rocking silently,
LG1891	3397	In farmers' barns oxen in the stable, their harvest labor done, they rest standing, they are too tired,
LG1891	3398	Afar on arctic ice the she-walrus lying drowsily while her cubs play around,
LG1891	3399	The hawk sailing where men have not yet sail'd, the farthest polar sea, ripply, crystalline, open, beyond the floes,
LG1891	3400	White drift spooning ahead where the ship in the tempest dashes,
LG1891	3401	On solid land what is done in cities as the bells strike midnight together,
LG1891	3402	In primitive woods the sounds there also sounding, the howl of the wolf, the scream of the panther, and the hoarse bellow of the elk,
LG1891	3403	In winter beneath the hard blue ice of Moosehead lake, in summer visible through the clear waters, the great trout swimming,
LG1891	3404	In lower latitudes in warmer air in the Carolinas the large black buzzard floating slowly high beyond the tree tops,
LG1891	3405	Below, the red cedar festoon'd with tylandria, the pines and cypresses growing out of the white sand that spreads far and flat,
LG1891	3406	Rude boats descending the big Pedee, climbing plants, parasites with color'd flowers and berries enveloping huge trees,
LG1891	3407	The waving drapery on the live-oak trailing long and low, noise-lesslynoiselessly waved by the wind,
LG1891	3408	The camp of Georgia wagoners just after dark, the supper-fires and the cooking and eating by whites and negroes,
LG1891	3409	Thirty or forty great wagons, the mules, cattle, horses, feeding from troughs,
LG1891	3410	The shadows, gleams, up under the leaves of the old sycamore-treessycamore-trees, the flames with the black smoke from the pitch-pine curling and rising;
LG1891	3411	Southern fishermen fishing, the sounds and inlets of North Caro-lina'sCarolina's coast, the shad-fishery and the herring-fishery, the large sweep-seines, the windlasses on shore work'd by horses, the clearing, curing, and packing-houses;
LG1891	3412	Deep in the forest in piney woods turpentine dropping from the incisions in the trees, there are the turpentine works,
LG1891	3413	There are the negroes at work in good health, the ground in all directions is cover'd with pine straw;
LG1891	3414	In Tennessee and Kentucky slaves busy in the coalings, at the forge, by the furnace-blaze, or at the corn-shucking,
LG1891	3415	In Virginia, the planter's son returning after a long absence, joy-fullyjoyfully welcom'd and kiss'd by the aged mulatto nurse,
LG1891	3416	On rivers boatmen safely moor'd at nightfall in their boats under shelter of high banks,
LG1891	3417	Some of the younger men dance to the sound of the banjo or fiddle, others sit on the gunwale smoking and talking;
LG1891	3418	Late in the afternoon the mocking-bird, the American mimic, singing in the Great Dismal Swamp,
LG1891	3419	There are the greenish waters, the resinous odor, the plenteous moss, the cypress-tree, and the juniper-tree;
LG1891	3420	Northward, young men of Mannahatta, the target company from an excursion returning home at evening, the musket-muz-zlesmusket-muzzles all bear bunches of flowers presented by women;
LG1891	3421	Children at play, or on his father's lap a young boy fallen asleep, (how his lips move! how he smiles in his sleep!)
LG1891	3422	The scout riding on horseback over the plains west of the Missis-sippiMississippi, he ascends a knoll and sweeps his eyes around;
LG1891	3423	California life, the miner, bearded, dress'd in his rude costume, the stanch California friendship, the sweet air, the graves one in passing meets solitary just aside the horse-path;
LG1891	3424	Down in Texas the cotton-field, the negro-cabins, drivers driving mules or oxen before rude carts, cotton bales piled on banks and wharves;
LG1891	3425	Encircling all, vast-darting up and wide, the American Soul, with equal hemispheres, one Love, one Dilation or Pride;
LG1891	3426	In arriere the peace-talk with the Iroquois the aborigines, the calumet, the pipe of good-will, arbitration, and indorse-mentindorsement,
LG1891	3427	The sachem blowing the smoke first toward the sun and then toward the earth,
LG1891	3428	The drama of the scalp-dance enacted with painted faces and guttural exclamations,
LG1891	3429	The setting out of the war-party, the long and stealthy march,
LG1891	3430	The single file, the swinging hatchets, the surprise and slaughter of enemies;
LG1891	3431	All the acts, scenes, ways, persons, attitudes of these States, reminiscences, institutions,
LG1891	3432	All these States compact, every square mile of these States without excepting a particle;
LG1891	3433	Me pleas'd, rambling in lanes and country fields, Paumanok's fields,
LG1891	3434	Observing the spiral flight of two little yellow butterflies shuffling between each other, ascending high in the air,
LG1891	3435	The darting swallow, the destroyer of insects, the fall traveler southward but returning northward early in the spring,
LG1891	3436	The country boy at the close of the day driving the herd of cows and shouting to them as they loiter to browse by the road-sideroadside,
LG1891	3437	The city wharf, Boston, Philadelphia, Baltimore, Charleston, New Orleans, San Francisco,
LG1891	3438	The departing ships when the sailors heave at the capstan;
LG1891	3439	Evening—me in my room—the setting sun,
LG1891	3440	The setting summer sun shining in my open window, showing the swarm of flies, suspended, balancing in the air in the centre of the room, darting athwart, up and down, casting swift shadows in specks on the opposite wall where the shine is;
LG1891	3441	The athletic American matron speaking in public to crowds of listeners,
LG1891	3442	Males, females, immigrants, combinations, the copiousness, the individuality of the States, each for itself—the money-makersmoneymakers,
LG1891	3443	Factories, machinery, the mechanical forces, the windlass, lever, pulley, all certainties,
LG1891	3444	The certainty of space, increase, freedom, futurity,
LG1891	3445	In space the sporades, the scatter'd islands, the stars—on the firm earth, the lands, my lands,
LG1891	3446	O lands! all so dear to me—what you are, (whatever it is,) I putting it at random in these songs, become a part of that, whatever it is,
LG1891	3447	Southward there, I screaming, with wings slow flapping, with the myriads of gulls wintering along the coasts of Florida,
LG1891	3448	Otherways there atwixt the banks of the Arkansaw, the Rio Grande, the Nueces, the Brazos, the Tombigbee, the Red River, the Saskatchawan or the Osage, I with the spring waters laughing and skipping and running,
LG1891	3449	Northward, on the sands, on some shallow bay of Paumanok, I with parties of snowy herons wading in the wet to seek worms and aquatic plants,
LG1891	3450	Retreating, triumphantly twittering, the king-bird, from piercing the crow with its bill, for amusement—and I triumphantly twittering,
LG1891	3451	The migrating flock of wild geese alighting in autumn to refresh themselves, the body of the flock feed, the sentinels out-sideoutside move around with erect heads watching, and are from time to time reliev'd by other sentinels—and I feeding and taking turns with the rest,
LG1891	3452	In KanadianCanadian forests the moose, large as an ox, corner'd by hunters, rising desperately on his hind-feet, and plunging with his fore-feet, the hoofs as sharp as knives—and I, plunging at the hunters, corner'd and desperate,
LG1891	3453	In the Mannahatta, streets, piers, shipping, store-houses, and the countless workmen working in the shops,
LG1891	3454	And I too of the Mannahatta, singing thereof—and no less in myself than the whole of the Mannahatta in itself,
LG1891	3455	Singing the song of These, my ever-united lands—my body no more inevitably united, part to part, and made out of a thousand diverse contributions one identity, any more than my lands are inevitably united and made ONE IDENTITY;
LG1891	3456	Nativities, climates, the grass of the great pastoral Plains,
LG1891	3457	Cities, labors, death, animals, products, war, good and evil—these me,
LG1891	3458	These affording, in all their particulars, the old feuillage to me and to America, how can I do less than pass the clew of the union of them, to afford the like to you?
LG1891	3459	Whoever you are! how can I but offer you divine leaves, that you also be eligible as I am?
LG1891	3460	How can I but as here chanting, invite you for yourself to collect bouquets of the incomparable feuillage of these States?
LG1891	3461	O TO make the most jubilant song!
LG1891	3462	Full of music—full of manhood, womanhood, infancy!
LG1891	3463	Full of common employments—full of grain and trees.
LG1891	3464	O for the voices of animals—O for the swiftness and balance of fishes!
LG1891	3465	O for the dropping of raindrops in a song!
LG1891	3466	O for the sunshine and motion of waves in a song!
LG1891	3467	O the joy of my spirit—it is uncaged—it darts like lightning!
LG1891	3468	It is not enough to have this globe or a certain time,
LG1891	3469	I will have thousands of globes and all time.
LG1891	3470	O the engineer's joys! to go with a locomotive!
LG1891	3471	To hear the hiss of steam, the merry shriek, the steam-whistle, the laughing locomotive!
LG1891	3472	To push with resistless way and speed off in the distance.
LG1891	3473	O the gleesome saunter over fields and hillsides!
LG1891	3474	The leaves and flowers of the commonest weeds, the moist fresh stillness of the woods,
LG1891	3475	The exquisite smell of the earth at daybreak, and all through the forenoon.
LG1891	3476	O the horseman's and horsewoman's joys!
LG1891	3477	The saddle, the gallop, the pressure upon the seat, the cool gurgling by the ears and hair.
LG1891	3478	O the fireman's joys!
LG1891	3479	I hear the alarm at dead of night,
LG1891	3480	I hear bells, shouts! I pass the crowd, I run!
LG1891	3481	The sight of the flames maddens me with pleasure.
LG1891	3482	O the joy of the strong-brawn'd fighter, towering in the arena in perfect condition, conscious of power, thirsting to meet his opponent.
LG1891	3483	O the joy of that vast elemental sympathy which only the human soul is capable of generating and emitting in steady and limitless floods.
LG1891	3484	O the mother's joys!
LG1891	3485	The watching, the endurance, the precious love, the anguish, the patiently yielded life.
LG1891	3486	O the joy of increase, growth, recuperation,
LG1891	3487	The joy of soothing and pacifying, the joy of concord and harmony.
LG1891	3488	O to go back to the place where I was born,
LG1891	3489	To hear the birds sing once more,
LG1891	3490	To ramble about the house and barn and over the fields once more,
LG1891	3491	And through the orchard and along the old lanes once more.
LG1891	3492	O to have been brought up on bays, lagoons, creeks, or along the coast,
LG1891	3493	To continue and be employ'd there all my life,
LG1891	3494	The briny and damp smell, the shore, the salt weeds exposed at low water,
LG1891	3495	The work of fishermen, the work of the eel-fisher and clam-fisher;
LG1891	3496	I come with my clam-rake and spade, I come with my eel-spear,
LG1891	3497	Is the tide out? I join the group of clam-diggers on the flats,
LG1891	3498	I laugh and work with them, I joke at my work like a mettlesome young man;
LG1891	3499	In winter I take my eel-basket and eel-spear and travel out on foot on the ice—I have a small axe to cut holes in the ice,
LG1891	3500	Behold me well-clothed going gayly or returning in the afternoon, my brood of tough boys accompanying me,
LG1891	3501	My brood of grown and part-grown boys, who love to be with no one else so well as they love to be with me,
LG1891	3502	By day to work with me, and by night to sleep with me.
LG1891	3503	Another time in warm weather out in a boat, to lift the lobster-pots where they are sunk with heavy stones, (I know the buoys,)
LG1891	3504	O the sweetness of the Fifth-month morning upon the water as I row just before sunrise toward the buoys,
LG1891	3505	I pull the wicker pots up slantingly, the dark green lobsters are desperate with their claws as I take them out, I insert wooden pegs in the joints of their pincers,
LG1891	3506	I go to all the places one after another, and then row back to the shore,
LG1891	3507	There in a huge kettle of boiling water the lobsters shall be boil'd till their color becomes scarlet.
LG1891	3508	Another time mackerel-taking,
LG1891	3509	Voracious, mad for the hook, near the surface, they seem to fill the water for miles;
LG1891	3510	Another time fishing for rock-fish in Chesapeake bay, I one of the brown-faced crew;
LG1891	3511	Another time trailing for blue-fish off Paumanok, I stand with braced body,
LG1891	3512	My left foot is on the gunwale, my right arm throws far out the coils of slender rope,
LG1891	3513	In sight around me the quick veering and darting of fifty skiffs, my companions.
LG1891	3514	O boating on the rivers,
LG1891	3515	The voyage down the St. Lawrence, the superb scenery, the steamers,
LG1891	3516	The ships sailing, the Thousand Islands, the occasional timber-raft and the raftsmen with long-reaching sweep-oars,
LG1891	3517	The little huts on the rafts, and the stream of smoke when they cook supper at evening.
LG1891	3518	(O something pernicious and dread!
LG1891	3519	Something far away from a puny and pious life!
LG1891	3520	Something unproved! something in a trance!
LG1891	3521	Something escaped from the anchorage and driving free.)
LG1891	3522	O to work in mines, or forging iron,
LG1891	3523	Foundry casting, the foundry itself, the rude high roof, the ample and shadow'd space,
LG1891	3524	The furnace, the hot liquid pour'd out and running.
LG1891	3525	O to resume the joys of the soldier!
LG1891	3526	To feel the presence of a brave commanding officer—to feel his sympathy!
LG1891	3527	To behold his calmness—to be warm'd in the rays of his smile!
LG1891	3528	To go to battle—to hear the bugles play and the drums beat!
LG1891	3529	To hear the crash of artillery—to see the glittering of the bayonets and musket-barrels in the sun!
LG1891	3530	To see men fall and die and not complain!
LG1891	3531	To taste the savage taste of blood—to be so devilish!
LG1891	3532	To gloat so over the wounds and deaths of the enemy.
LG1891	3533	O the whaleman's joys! O I cruise my old cruise again!
LG1891	3534	I feel the ship's motion under me, I feel the Atlantic breezes fan-ningfanning me,
LG1891	3535	I hear the cry again sent down from the mast-head, There—she blows!
LG1891	3536	Again I spring up the rigging to look with the rest—we descend, wild with excitement,
LG1891	3537	I leap in the lower'd boat, we row toward our prey where he lies,
LG1891	3538	We approach stealthy and silent, I see the mountainous mass, lethargic, basking,
LG1891	3539	I see the harpooneer standing up, I see the weapon dart from his vigorous arm;
LG1891	3540	O swift again far out in the ocean the wounded whale, settling, running to windward, tows me,
LG1891	3541	Again I see him rise to breathe, we row close again,
LG1891	3542	I see a lance driven through his side, press'd deep, turn'd in the wound,
LG1891	3543	Again we back off, I see him settle again, the life is leaving him fast,
LG1891	3544	As he rises he spouts blood, I see him swim in circles narrower and narrower, swiftly cutting the water—I see him die,
LG1891	3545	He gives one convulsive leap in the centre of the circle, and then falls flat and still in the bloody foam.
LG1891	3546	O the old manhood of me, my noblest joy of all!
LG1891	3547	My children and grand-children, my white hair and beard,
LG1891	3548	My largeness, calmness, majesty, out of the long stretch of my life.
LG1891	3549	O ripen'd joy of womanhood! O happiness at last!
LG1891	3550	I am more than eighty years of age, I am the most venerable mother,
LG1891	3551	How clear is my mind—how all people draw nigh to me!
LG1891	3552	What attractions are these beyond any before? what bloom more than the bloom of youth?
LG1891	3553	What beauty is this that descends upon me and rises out of me?
LG1891	3554	O the orator's joys!
LG1891	3555	To inflate the chest, to roll the thunder of the voice out from the ribs and throat,
LG1891	3556	To make the people rage, weep, hate, desire, with yourself,
LG1891	3557	To lead America—to quell America with a great tongue.
LG1891	3558	O the joy of my soul leaning pois'd on itself, receiving identity through materials and loving them, observing characters and absorbing them,
LG1891	3559	My soul vibrated back to me from them, from sight, hearing, touch, reason, articulation, comparison, memory, and the like,
LG1891	3560	The real life of my senses and flesh transcending my senses and flesh,
LG1891	3561	My body done with materials, my sight done with my material eyes,
LG1891	3562	Proved to me this day beyond cavil that it is not my material eyes which finally see,
LG1891	3563	Nor my material body which finally loves, walks, laughs, shouts, embraces, procreates.

LG1891	3594	O while I live to be the ruler of life, not a slave,
LG1891	3595	To meet life as a powerful conqueror,
LG1891	3596	No fumes, no ennui, no more complaints or scornful criticisms,

LG1891	3600	The beautiful touch of Death, soothing and benumbing a few moments, for reasons,
LG1891	3601	Myself discharging my excrementitious body to be burn'd, or render'd to powder, or buried,
LG1891	3602	My real body doubtless left to me for other spheres,
LG1891	3603	My voided body nothing more to me, returning to the purifications, further offices, eternal uses of the earth.
LG1891	3604	O to attract by more than attraction!

LG1891	3610	To mount the scaffold, to advance to the muzzles of guns with perfect nonchalance!

LG1891	3860	Her shape arises,
LG1891	3861	She less guarded than ever, yet more guarded than ever,
LG1891	3862	The gross and soil'd she moves among do not make her gross and soil'd,

LG1891	3865	She is the best belov'd, it is without exception, she has no reason to fear and she does not fear,

LG1891	3869	She too is a law of Nature—there is no law stronger than she is.

LG1891	3876	(AH little recks the laborer,

LG1891	3878	The loving Laborer through space and time.)
LG1891	3879	After all not to create only, or found only,
LG1891	3880	But to bring perhaps from afar what is already founded,
LG1891	3881	To give it our own identity, average, limitless, free,
LG1891	3882	To fill the gross the torpid bulk with vital religious fire,

LG1891	3884	To obey as well as command, to follow more than to lead,
LG1891	3885	These also are the lessons of our New World;
LG1891	3886	While how little the New after all, how much the Old, Old World!
LG1891	3887	Long and long has the grass been growing,
LG1891	3888	Long and long has the rain been falling,
LG1891	3889	Long has the globe been rolling round.
LG1891	3890	Come Muse migrate from Greece and Ionia,
LG1891	3891	Cross out please those immensely overpaid accounts,

LG1891	3897	Responsive to our summons,

LG1891	3899	Join'd with an irresistible, natural gravitation,

LG1891	3901	I scent the odor of her breath's delicious fragrance,
LG1891	3902	I mark her step divine, her curious eyes a-turning, rolling,
LG1891	3903	Upon this very scene.
LG1891	3904	The dame of dames! can I believe then,
LG1891	3905	Those ancient temples, sculptures classic, could none of them retain her?

LG1891	3908	Yes, if you will allow me to say so,
LG1891	3909	I, my friends, if you do not, can plainly see her,
LG1891	3910	The same undying soul of earth's, activity's, beauty's, heroism's expression,

LG1891	3936	To introduce the stranger, (what else indeed do I live to chant for?) to thee Columbia;

LG1891	3939	Fear not O Muse! truly new ways and days receive, surround you,

LG1891	3941	And yet the same old human race, the same within, without,

LG1891	3948	Mightier than Egypt's tombs,
LG1891	3949	Fairer than Grecia's, Roma's temples,

LG1891	3958	Around a palace, loftier, fairer, ampler than any yet,

LG1891	3991	With precious benefits to both, glory to all,

LG1891	4220	In the labor of engines and trades and the labor of fields I find the developments,
LG1891	4221	And find the eternal meanings.
LG1891	4222	Workmen and Workwomen!
LG1891	4223	Were all educations practical and ornamental well display'd out of me, what would it amount to?
LG1891	4224	Were I as the head teacher, charitable proprietor, wise statesman,what would it amount to?
LG1891	4225	Were I to you as the boss employing and paying you, would that satisfy you?
LG1891	4226	The learn'd, virtuous, benevolent, and the usual terms,
LG1891	4227	A man like me and never the usual terms.
LG1891	4228	Neither a servant nor a master I,
LG1891	4229	I take no sooner a large price than a small price, I will have my own whoever enjoys me,
LG1891	4230	I will be even with you and you shall be even with me.
LG1891	4231	If you stand at work in a shop I stand as nigh as the nighest in the same shop,
LG1891	4232	If you bestow gifts on your brother or dearest friend I demand as good as your brother or dearest friend,
LG1891	4233	If your lover, husband, wife, is welcome by day or night, I must be personally as welcome,
LG1891	4234	If you become degraded, criminal, ill, then I become so for your sake,
LG1891	4235	If you remember your foolish and outlaw'd deeds, do you think I cannot remember my own foolish and outlaw'd deeds?
LG1891	4236	If you carouse at the table I carouse at the opposite side of the table,
LG1891	4237	If you meet some stranger in the streets and love him or her, why I often meet strangers in the street and love them.
LG1891	4238	Why what have you thought of yourself?
LG1891	4239	Is it you then that thought yourself less?
LG1891	4240	Is it you that thought the President greater than you?
LG1891	4241	Or the rich better off than you? or the educated wiser than you?
LG1891	4242	(Because you are greasy or pimpled, or were once drunk, or a thief,
LG1891	4243	Or that you are diseas'd, or rheumatic, or a prostitute,
LG1891	4244	Or from frivolity or impotence, or that you are no scholar and never saw your name in print,
LG1891	4245	Do you give in that you are any less immortal?)
LG1891	4246	Souls of men and women! it is not you I call unseen, unheard,untouchable and untouching,
LG1891	4247	It is not you I go argue pro and con about, and to settle whether you are alive or no,
LG1891	4248	I own publicly who you are, if nobody else owns.
LG1891	4249	Grown, half-grown and babe, of this country and every country, in-doorsindoors and out-doors, one just as much as the other, I see,
LG1891	4250	And all else behind or through them.
LG1891	4251	The wife, and she is not one jot less than the husband,
LG1891	4252	The daughter, and she is just as good as the son,
LG1891	4253	The mother, and she is every bit as much as the father.
LG1891	4254	Offspring of ignorant and poor, boys apprenticed to trades,
LG1891	4255	Young fellows working on farms and old fellows working on farms,
LG1891	4256	Sailor-men, merchant-men, coasters, immigrants,
LG1891	4257	All these I see, but nigher and farther the same I see,
LG1891	4258	None shall escape me and none shall wish to escape me.
LG1891	4259	I bring what you much need yet always have,
LG1891	4260	Not money, amours, dress, eating, erudition, but as good,
LG1891	4261	I send no agent or medium, offer no representative of value, but offer the value itself.
LG1891	4262	There is something that comes to one now and perpetually,
LG1891	4263	It is not what is printed, preach'd, discussed, it eludes discussion and print,
LG1891	4264	It is not to be put in a book, it is not in this book,
LG1891	4265	It is for you whoever you are, it is no farther from you than your hearing and sight are from you,
LG1891	4266	It is hinted by nearest, commonest, readiest, it is ever provoked by them.
LG1891	4267	You may read in many languages, yet read nothing about it,
LG1891	4268	You may read the President's message and read nothing about it there,
LG1891	4269	Nothing in the reports from the State department or Treasury department, or in the daily papers or weekly papers,
LG1891	4270	Or in the census or revenue returns, prices current, or any accounts of stock.
LG1891	4271	The sun and stars that float in the open air,
LG1891	4272	The apple-shaped earth and we upon it, surely the drift of them is something grand,
LG1891	4273	I do not know what it is except that it is grand, and that it is happiness,
LG1891	4274	And that the enclosing purport of us here is not a speculation or bon-mot or reconnoissance,
LG1891	4275	And that it is not something which by luck may turn out well for us, and without luck must be a failure for us,
LG1891	4276	And not something which may yet be retracted in a certain contingency.
LG1891	4277	The light and shade, the curious sense of body and identity, the greed that with perfect complaisance devours all things,
LG1891	4278	The endless pride and outstretching of man, unspeakable joys and sorrows,
LG1891	4279	The wonder every one sees in every one else he sees, and the wonders that fill each minute of time forever,
LG1891	4280	What have you reckon'd them for, camerado?
LG1891	4281	Have you reckon'd them for your trade or farm-work? or for the profits of your store?
LG1891	4282	Or to achieve yourself a position? or to fill a gentleman's leisure,or a lady's leisure?
LG1891	4283	Have you reckon'd that the landscape took substance and form that it might be painted in a picture?
LG1891	4284	Or men and women that they might be written of, and songs sung?
LG1891	4285	Or the attraction of gravity, and the great laws and harmonious combinations and the fluids of the air, as subjects for the savans?
LG1891	4286	Or the brown land and the blue sea for maps and charts?
LG1891	4287	Or the stars to be put in constellations and named fancy names?
LG1891	4288	Or that the growth of seeds is for agricultural tables, or agricul-tureagriculture itself?
LG1891	4289	Old institutions, these arts, libraries, legends, collections, and the practice handed along in manufactures, will we rate them so high?
LG1891	4290	Will we rate our cash and business high? I have no objection,
LG1891	4291	I rate them as high as the highest—then a child born of a woman and man I rate beyond all rate.
LG1891	4292	We thought our Union grand, and our Constitution grand,
LG1891	4293	I do not say they are not grand and good, for they are,
LG1891	4294	I am this day just as much in love with them as you,
LG1891	4295	Then I am in love with You, and with all my fellows upon the earth.
LG1891	4296	We consider bibles and religions divine—I do not say they are not divine,
LG1891	4297	I say they have all grown out of you, and may grow out of you still,
LG1891	4298	It is not they who give the life, it is you who give the life,
LG1891	4299	Leaves are not more shed from the trees, or trees from the earth,than they are shed out of you.
LG1891	4300	The sum of all known reverence I add up in you whoever you are,
LG1891	4301	The President is there in the White House for you, it is not you who are here for him,
LG1891	4302	The Secretaries act in their bureaus for you, not you here for them,
LG1891	4303	The Congress convenes every Twelfth-month for you,
LG1891	4304	Laws, courts, the forming of States, the charters of cities, the going and coming of commerce and mails, are all for you.
LG1891	4305	List close my scholars dear,
LG1891	4306	Doctrines, politics and civilization exurge from you,
LG1891	4307	Sculpture and monuments and any thing inscribed anywhere are tallied in you,
LG1891	4308	The gist of histories and statistics as far back as the records reach is in you this hour, and myths and tales the same,
LG1891	4309	If you were not breathing and walking here, where would they all be?
LG1891	4310	The most renown'd poems would be ashes, orations and plays would be vacuums.
LG1891	4311	All architecture is what you do to it when you look upon it,
LG1891	4312	(Did you think it was in the white or gray stone? or the lines of the arches and cornices?)
LG1891	4313	All music is what awakes from you when you are reminded by the instruments,
LG1891	4314	It is not the violins and the cornets, it is not the oboe nor the beating drums, nor the score of the baritone singer singing his sweet romanza, nor that of the men's chorus, nor that of the women's chorus,
LG1891	4315	It is nearer and farther than they.
LG1891	4316	Will the whole come back then?
LG1891	4317	Can each see signs of the best by a look in the looking-glass? is there nothing greater or more?
LG1891	4318	Does all sit there with you, with the mystic unseen soul?
LG1891	4319	Strange and hard that paradox true I give,
LG1891	4320	Objects gross and the unseen soul are one.
LG1891	4321	House-building, measuring, sawing the boards,
