LG1891	4345	The hourly routine of your own or any man's life, the shop, yard,store, or factory,
LG1891	4346	These shows all near you by day and night—workman! whoever you are, your daily life!
LG1891	4347	In that and them the heft of the heaviest—in that and them far more than you estimated, (and far less also,)
LG1891	4348	In them realities for you and me, in them poems for you and me,
LG1891	4349	In them, not yourself—you and your soul enclose all things, re-gardlessregardless of estimation,
LG1891	4350	In them the development good—in them all themes, hints, possi-bilitiespossibilities.
LG1891	4351	I do not affirm that what you see beyond is futile, I do not advise you to stop,
LG1891	4352	I do not say leadings you thought great are not great,
LG1891	4353	But I say that none lead to greater than these lead to.
LG1891	4354	Will you seek afar off? you surely come back at last,
LG1891	4355	In things best known to you finding the best, or as good as the best,
LG1891	4356	In folks nearest to you finding the sweetest, strongest, lovingest,
LG1891	4357	Happiness, knowledge, not in another place but this place, not for another hour but this hour,
LG1891	4358	Man in the first you see or touch, always in friend, brother,nighest neighbor—woman in mother, sister, wife,
LG1891	4359	The popular tastes and employments taking precedence in poems or anywhere,
LG1891	4360	You workwomen and workmen of these States having your own divine and strong life,
LG1891	4361	And all else giving place to men and women like you.
LG1891	4362	When the psalm sings instead of the singer,
LG1891	4363	When the script preaches instead of the preacher,
LG1891	4364	When the pulpit descends and goes instead of the carver that carved the supporting desk,
LG1891	4365	When I can touch the body of books by night or by day, and when they touch my body back again,
LG1891	4366	When a university course convinces like a slumbering woman and child convince,
LG1891	4367	When the minted gold in the vault smiles like the night-watchman's daughter,
LG1891	4368	When warrantee deeds loafe in chairs opposite and are my friendly companions,
LG1891	4369	I intend to reach them my hand, and make as much of them as I do of men and women like you.
LG1891	4370	A SONG of the rolling earth, and of words according,
LG1891	4371	Were you thinking that those were the words, those upright lines?those curves, angles, dots?
LG1891	4372	No, those are not the words, the substantial words are in the ground and sea,
LG1891	4373	They are in the air, they are in you.
LG1891	4374	Were you thinking that those were the words, those delicious sounds out of your friends' mouths?
LG1891	4375	No, the real words are more delicious than they.
LG1891	4376	Human bodies are words, myriads of words,
LG1891	4377	(In the best poems re-appears the body, man's or woman's, well-shapedwell-shaped, natural, gay,
LG1891	4378	Every part able, active, receptive, without shame or the need of shame.)
LG1891	4379	Air, soil, water, fire—those are words,
LG1891	4380	I myself am a word with them—my qualities interpenetrate with theirs—my name is nothing to them,
LG1891	4381	Though it were told in the three thousand languages, what would air, soil, water, fire, know of my name?
LG1891	4382	A healthy presence, a friendly or commanding gesture, are words,sayings, meanings,
LG1891	4383	The charms that go with the mere looks of some men and women,are sayings and meanings also.
LG1891	4384	The workmanship of souls is by those inaudible words of the earth,
LG1891	4385	The masters know the earth's words and use them more than audible words.
LG1891	4386	Amelioration is one of the earth's words,
LG1891	4387	The earth neither lags nor hastens,
LG1891	4388	It has all attributes, growths, effects, latent in itself from the jump,
LG1891	4389	It is not half beautiful only, defects and excrescences show just as much as perfections show.
LG1891	4390	The earth does not withhold, it is generous enough,
LG1891	4391	The truths of the earth continually wait, they are not so conceal'd either,
LG1891	4392	They are calm, subtle, untransmissible by print,
LG1891	4393	They are imbued through all things conveying themselves willingly,
LG1891	4394	Conveying a sentiment and invitation, I utter and utter,
LG1891	4395	I speak not, yet if you hear me not of what avail am I to you?
LG1891	4396	To bear, to better, lacking these of what avail am I?
LG1891	4397	(Accouche! accouchez!
LG1891	4398	Will you rot your own fruit in yourself there?
LG1891	4399	Will you squat and stifle there?)
LG1891	4400	The earth does not argue,
LG1891	4401	Is not pathetic, has no arrangements,
LG1891	4402	Does not scream, haste, persuade, threaten, promise,
LG1891	4403	Makes no discriminations, has no conceivable failures,
LG1891	4404	Closes nothing, refuses nothing, shuts none out,
LG1891	4405	Of all the powers, objects, states, it notifies, shuts none out.
LG1891	4406	The earth does not exhibit itself nor refuse to exhibit itself, pos-sessespossesses still underneath,
LG1891	4407	Underneath the ostensible sounds, the august chorus of heroes, the wail of slaves,
LG1891	4408	Persuasions of lovers, curses, gasps of the dying, laughter of young people, accents of bargainers,
LG1891	4409	Underneath these possessing words that never fail.
LG1891	4410	To her children the words of the eloquent dumb great mother never fail,
LG1891	4411	The true words do not fail, for motion does not fail and reflection does not fail,
LG1891	4412	Also the day and night do not fail, and the voyage we pursue does not fail.
LG1891	4413	Of the interminable sisters,
LG1891	4414	Of the ceaseless cotillons of sisters,
LG1891	4415	Of the centripetal and centrifugal sisters, the elder and younger sisters,
LG1891	4416	The beautiful sister we know dances on with the rest.
LG1891	4417	With her ample back towards every beholder,
LG1891	4418	With the fascinations of youth and the equal fascinations of age,
LG1891	4419	Sits she whom I too love like the rest, sits undisturb'd,
LG1891	4420	Holding up in her hand what has the character of a mirror, while her eyes glance back from it,
LG1891	4421	Glance as she sits, inviting none, denying none,
LG1891	4422	Holding a mirror day and night tirelessly before her own face.
LG1891	4423	Seen at hand or seen at a distance,
LG1891	4424	Duly the twenty-four appear in public every day,
LG1891	4425	Duly approach and pass with their companions or a companion,
LG1891	4426	Looking from no countenances of their own, but from the counte-nancescountenances of those who are with them,
LG1891	4427	From the countenances of children or women or the manly coun-tenancecountenance,
LG1891	4428	From the open countenances of animals or from inanimate things,
LG1891	4429	From the landscape or waters or from the exquisite apparition of the sky,
LG1891	4430	From our countenances, mine and yours, faithfully returning them,
LG1891	4431	Every day in public appearing without fail, but never twice with the same companions.
LG1891	4432	Embracing man, embracing all, proceed the three hundred and sixty-five resistlessly round the sun;
LG1891	4433	Embracing all, soothing, supporting, follow close three hundred and sixty-five offsets of the first, sure and necessary as they.
LG1891	4434	Tumbling on steadily, nothing dreading,
LG1891	4435	Sunshine, storm, cold, heat, forever withstanding, passing, carrying,
LG1891	4436	The soul's realization and determination still inheriting,
LG1891	4437	The fluid vacuum around and ahead still entering and dividing,
LG1891	4438	No balk retarding, no anchor anchoring, on no rock striking,
LG1891	4439	Swift, glad, content, unbereav'd, nothing losing,
LG1891	4440	Of all able and ready at any time to give strict account,
LG1891	4441	The divine ship sails the divine sea.
LG1891	4442	Whoever you are! motion and reflection are especially for you,
LG1891	4443	The divine ship sails the divine sea for you.
LG1891	4444	Whoever you are! you are he or she for whom the earth is solid and liquid,
LG1891	4445	You are he or she for whom the sun and moon hang in the sky,
LG1891	4446	For none more than you are the present and the past,
LG1891	4447	For none more than you is immortality.
LG1891	4448	Each man to himself and each woman to herself, is the word of the past and present, and the true word of immortality;
LG1891	4449	No one can acquire for another—not one,
LG1891	4450	Not one can grow for another—not one.
LG1891	4451	The song is to the singer, and comes back most to him,
LG1891	4452	The teaching is to the teacher, and comes back most to him,
LG1891	4453	The murder is to the murderer, and comes back most to him,
LG1891	4454	The theft is to the thief, and comes back most to him,
LG1891	4455	The love is to the lover, and comes back most to him,
LG1891	4456	The gift is to the giver, and comes back most to him—it cannot fail,
LG1891	4457	The oration is to the orator, the acting is to the actor and actress not to the audience,
LG1891	4458	And no man understands any greatness or goodness but his own,or the indication of his own.
LG1891	4459	I swear the earth shall surely be complete to him or her who shall be complete,
LG1891	4460	The earth remains jagged and broken only to him or her who remains jagged and broken.
LG1891	4461	I swear there is no greatness or power that does not emulate those of the earth,
LG1891	4462	There can be no theory of any account unless it corroborate the theory of the earth,
LG1891	4463	No politics, song, religion, behavior, or what not, is of account,unless it compare with the amplitude of the earth,
LG1891	4464	Unless it face the exactness, vitality, impartiality, rectitude of the earth.
LG1891	4465	I swear I begin to see love with sweeter spasms than that which responds love,
LG1891	4466	It is that which contains itself, which never invites and never refuses.
LG1891	4467	I swear I begin to see little or nothing in audible words,
LG1891	4468	All merges toward the presentation of the unspoken meanings of the earth,
LG1891	4469	Toward him who sings the songs of the body and of the truths of the earth,
LG1891	4470	Toward him who makes the dictionaries of words that print can-notcannot touch.
LG1891	4471	I swear I see what is better than to tell the best,
LG1891	4472	It is always to leave the best untold.
LG1891	4473	When I undertake to tell the best I find I cannot,
LG1891	4474	My tongue is ineffectual on its pivots,
LG1891	4475	My breath will not be obedient to its organs,
LG1891	4476	I become a dumb man.
LG1891	4477	The best of the earth cannot be told anyhow, all or any is best,
LG1891	4478	It is not what you anticipated, it is cheaper, easier, nearer,
LG1891	4479	Things are not dismiss'd from the places they held before,
LG1891	4480	The earth is just as positive and direct as it was before,
LG1891	4481	Facts, religions, improvements, politics, trades, are as real as before,
LG1891	4482	But the soul is also real, it too is positive and direct,
LG1891	4483	No reasoning, no proof has establish'd it,
LG1891	4484	Undeniable growth has establish'd it.
LG1891	4485	These to echo the tones of souls and the phrases of souls,
LG1891	4486	(If they did not echo the phrases of souls what were they then?
LG1891	4487	If they had not reference to you in especial what were they then?)
LG1891	4488	I swear I will never henceforth have to do with the faith that tells the best,
LG1891	4489	I will have to do only with that faith that leaves the best untold.
LG1891	4490	Say on, sayers! sing on, singers!
LG1891	4491	Delve! mould! pile the words of the earth!
LG1891	4492	Work on, age after age, nothing is to be lost,
LG1891	4493	It may have to wait long, but it will certainly come in use,
LG1891	4494	When the materials are all prepared and ready, the architects shall appear.
LG1891	4495	I swear to you the architects shall appear without fail,
LG1891	4496	I swear to you they will understand you and justify you,
LG1891	4497	The greatest among them shall be he who best knows you, and encloses all and is faithful to all,
LG1891	4498	He and the rest shall not forget you, they shall perceive that you are not an iota less than they,
LG1891	4499	You shall be fully glorified in them.
LG1891	4500	YOUTH, large, lusty, loving—youth full of grace, force, fascination,
LG1891	4501	Do you know that Old Age may come after you with equal grace, force, fascination?
LG1891	4502	Day full-blown and splendid—day of the immense sun, action, ambition, laughter,
LG1891	4503	The Night follows close with millions of suns, and sleep and restoring darkness.
LG1891	4504	COME said the Muse,
LG1891	4505	Sing me a song no poet yet has chanted,
LG1891	4506	Sing me the universal.
LG1891	4507	In this broad earth of ours,
LG1891	4508	Amid the measureless grossness and the slag,
LG1891	4509	Enclosed and safe within its central heart,
LG1891	4510	Nestles the seed perfection.
LG1891	4511	By every life a share or more or less,
LG1891	4512	None born but it is born, conceal'd or unconceal'd the seed is waiting.
LG1891	4513	Lo! keen-eyed towering science,
LG1891	4514	As from tall peaks the modern overlooking,
LG1891	4515	Successive absolute fiats issuing.
LG1891	4516	Yet again, lo! the soul, above all science,
LG1891	4517	For it has history gather'd like husks around the globe,
LG1891	4518	For it the entire star-myriads roll through the sky.
LG1891	4519	In spiral routes by long detours,
LG1891	4520	(As a much-tacking ship upon the sea,)
LG1891	4521	For it the partial to the permanent flowing,
LG1891	4522	For it the real to the ideal tends.
LG1891	4523	For it the mystic evolution,
LG1891	4524	Not the right only justified, what we call evil also justified.
LG1891	4525	Forth from their masks, no matter what,
LG1891	4526	From the huge festering trunk, from craft and guile and tears,
LG1891	4527	Health to emerge and joy, joy universal.
LG1891	4528	Out of the bulk, the morbid and the shallow,
LG1891	4529	Out of the bad majority, the varied countless frauds of men and states,
LG1891	4530	Electric, antiseptic yet, cleaving, suffusing all,
LG1891	4531	Only the good is universal.
LG1891	4532	Over the mountain-growths disease and sorrow,
LG1891	4533	An uncaught bird is ever hovering, hovering,

LG1891	4678	I whisper with my lips close to your ear,
LG1891	4679	I have loved many women and men, but I love none better than you.
LG1891	4680	O I have been dilatory and dumb,
LG1891	4681	I should have made my way straight to you long ago,
LG1891	4682	I should have blabb'd nothing but you, I should have chanted nothing but you.

LG1891	4689	Painters have painted their swarming groups and the centre-figure of all,

LG1891	4693	O I could sing such grandeurs and glories about you!

LG1891	4696	What you have done returns already in mockeries,

LG1891	4698	The mockeries are not you,
LG1891	4699	Underneath them and within them I see you lurk,
LG1891	4700	I pursue you where none else has pursued you,
LG1891	4701	Silence, the desk, the flippant expression, the night, the accustom'd routine, if these conceal you from others or from yourself,they do not conceal you from me,
LG1891	4702	The shaved face, the unsteady eye, the impure complexion, if these balk others they do not balk me,
LG1891	4703	The pert apparel, the deform'd attitude, drunkenness, greed, pre-maturepremature death, all these I part aside.
LG1891	4704	There is no endowment in man or woman that is not tallied in you,
LG1891	4705	There is no virtue, no beauty in man or woman, but as good is in you,
LG1891	4706	No pluck, no endurance in others, but as good is in you,
LG1891	4707	No pleasure waiting for others, but an equal pleasure waits for you.
LG1891	4708	As for me, I give nothing to any one except I give the like care-fullycarefully to you,
LG1891	4709	I sing the songs of the glory of none, not God, sooner than I sing the songs of the glory of you.
LG1891	4710	Whoever you are! claim your own at any hazard!
LG1891	4711	These shows of the East and West are tame compared to you,

LG1891	4713	These furies, elements, storms, motions of Nature, throes of appar-entapparent dissolution, you are he or she who is master or mistress over them,
LG1891	4714	Master or mistress in your own right over Nature, elements, pain,passion, dissolution.
LG1891	4715	The hopples fall from your ankles, you find an unfailing sufficiency,
LG1891	4716	Old or young, male or female, rude, low, rejected by the rest,whatever you are promulges itself,
LG1891	4717	Through birth, life, death, burial, the means are provided, nothing is scanted,
LG1891	4718	Through angers, losses, ambition, ignorance, ennui, what you are picks its way.
LG1891	4719	A GREAT year and place,
LG1891	4720	A harsh discordant natal scream out-sounding, to touch the mother's heart closer than any yet.
LG1891	4721	I walk'd the shores of my Eastern sea,
LG1891	4722	Heard over the waves the little voice,
LG1891	4723	Saw the divine infant where she woke mournfully wailing, amid the roar of cannon, curses, shouts, crash of falling buildings,
LG1891	4724	Was not so sick from the blood in the gutters running, nor from the single corpses, nor those in heaps, nor those borne away in the tumbrils,
LG1891	4725	Was not so desperate at the battues of death—was not so shock'd at the repeated fusillades of the guns.
LG1891	4726	Pale, silent, stern, what could I say to that long-accrued retribu-tionretribution?
LG1891	4727	Could I wish humanity different?
LG1891	4728	Could I wish the people made of wood and stone?
LG1891	4729	Or that there be no justice in destiny or time?
LG1891	4730	O Liberty! O mate for me!
LG1891	4731	Here too the blaze, the grape-shot and the axe, in reserve, to fetch them out in case of need,
LG1891	4732	Here too, though long represt, can never be destroy'd,
LG1891	4733	Here too could rise at last murdering and ecstatic,
LG1891	4734	Here too demanding full arrears of vengeance.
LG1891	4735	Hence I sign this salute over the sea,
LG1891	4736	And I do not deny that terrible red birth and baptism,

LG1891	4746	I will yet sing a song for you ma femme.
LG1891	4747	MYSELF and mine gymnastic ever,
LG1891	4748	To stand the cold or heat, to take good aim with a gun, to sail a boat, to manage horses, to beget superb children,
LG1891	4749	To speak readily and clearly, to feel at home among common people,
LG1891	4750	And to hold our own in terrible positions on land and sea.
LG1891	4751	Not for an embroiderer,
LG1891	4752	(There will always be plenty of embroiderers, I welcome them also,)
LG1891	4753	But for the fibre of things and for inherent men and women.
LG1891	4754	Not to chisel ornaments,
LG1891	4755	But to chisel with free stroke the heads and limbs of plenteous supreme Gods, that the States may realize them walking and talking.
LG1891	4756	Let me have my own way,
LG1891	4757	Let others promulge the laws, I will make no account of the laws,
LG1891	4758	Let others praise eminent men and hold up peace, I hold up agitation and conflict,
LG1891	4759	I praise no eminent man, I rebuke to his face the one that was thought most worthy.
LG1891	4760	(Who are you? and what are you secretly guilty of all your life?
LG1891	4761	Will you turn aside all your life? will you grub and chatter all your life?
LG1891	4762	And who are you, blabbing by rote, years, pages, languages,reminiscences,

LG1891	4764	Let others finish specimens, I never finish specimens,
LG1891	4765	I start them by exhaustless laws as Nature does, fresh and modern continually.
LG1891	4766	I give nothing as duties,
LG1891	4767	What others give as duties I give as living impulses,
LG1891	4768	(Shall I give the heart's action as a duty?)
LG1891	4769	Let others dispose of questions, I dispose of nothing, I arouse unanswerable questions,
LG1891	4770	Who are they I see and touch, and what about them?
LG1891	4771	What about these likes of myself that draw me so close by tender directions and indirections?
LG1891	4772	I call to the world to distrust the accounts of my friends, but listen to my enemies, as I myself do,
LG1891	4773	I charge you forever reject those who would expound me, for I cannot expound myself,
LG1891	4774	I charge that there be no theory or school founded out of me,
LG1891	4775	I charge you to leave all free, as I have left all free.
LG1891	4776	After me, vista!
LG1891	4777	O I see life is not short, but immeasurably long,
LG1891	4778	I henceforth tread the world chaste, temperate, an early riser, a steady grower,
LG1891	4779	Every hour the semen of centuries, and still of centuries.
LG1891	4780	I must follow up these continual lessons of the air, water, earth,
LG1891	4781	I perceive I have no time to lose.
LG1891	4782	YEAR of meteors! brooding year!
LG1891	4783	I would bind in words retrospective some of your deeds and signs,

LG1891	4807	WITH antecedents,
LG1891	4808	With my fathers and mothers and the accumulations of past ages,

LG1891	4827	The very sun swings itself and its system of planets around us,
LG1891	4828	Its sun, and its again, all swing around us.
LG1891	4829	As for me, (torn, stormy, amid these vehement days,)
LG1891	4830	I have the idea of all, and am all and believe in all,
LG1891	4831	I believe materialism is true and spiritualism is true, I reject no part.
LG1891	4832	(Have I forgotten any part? any thing in the past?
LG1891	4833	Come to me whoever and whatever, till I give you recogni-tionrecognition.)
LG1891	4834	I respect Assyria, China, Teutonia, and the Hebrews,

LG1891	4836	I see that the old accounts, bibles, genealogies, are true, without exception,
LG1891	4837	I assert that all past days were what they must have been,
LG1891	4838	And that they could no-how have been better than they were,
LG1891	4839	And that to-day is what it must be, and that America is,
LG1891	4840	And that to-day and America could no-how be better than they are.
LG1891	4841	In the name of these States and in your and my name, the Past,

LG1891	4843	I know that the past was great and the future will be great,

LG1891	4847	And there is the meaning to us of all that has ever come of races and days, or ever will come.
LG1891	4848	OVER the Western sea hither from Niphon come,

LG1891	4852	Libertad! I do not know whether others behold what I behold,

LG1891	4868	Superb-faced Manhattan!
LG1891	4869	Comrade Americanos! to us, then at last the Orient comes.
LG1891	4870	To us, my city,
LG1891	4871	Where our tall-topt marble and iron beauties range on opposite sides, to walk in the space between,

LG1891	4873	The Originatress comes,
LG1891	4874	The nest of languages, the bequeather of poems, the race of eld,

LG1891	4877	With sunburnt visage, with intense soul and glittering eyes,

LG1891	4879	See my cantabile! these and more are flashing to us from the procession,
LG1891	4880	As it moves changing, a kaleidoscope divine it moves changing before us.
LG1891	4881	For not the envoys nor the tann'd Japanee from his island only,

LG1891	4914	You shall sit in the middle well-pois'd thousands and thousands of years,

LG1891	4918	The ring is circled, the journey is done,

LG1891	4929	OUT of the cradle endlessly rocking,

LG1891	4951	Once Paumanok,
LG1891	4952	When the lilac-scent was in the air and Fifth-month grass was growing,
LG1891	4953	Up this seashore in some briers,
LG1891	4954	Two feather'd guests from Alabama, two together,
LG1891	4955	And their nest, and four light-green eggs spotted with brown,
LG1891	4956	And every day the he-bird to and fro near at hand,
LG1891	4957	And every day the she-bird crouch'd on her nest, silent, with bright eyes,
LG1891	4958	And every day I, a curious boy, never too close, never disturbing them,
LG1891	4959	Cautiously peering, absorbing, translating.
LG1891	4960	Shine! shine! shine!
LG1891	4961	Pour down your warmth, great sun!
LG1891	4962	While we bask, we two together.
LG1891	4963	Two together!
LG1891	4964	Winds blow south, or winds blow north,
LG1891	4965	Day come white, or night come black,
LG1891	4966	Home, or rivers and mountains from home,
LG1891	4967	Singing all time, minding no time,
LG1891	4968	While we two keep together.
LG1891	4969	Till of a sudden,
LG1891	4970	May-be kill'd, unknown to her mate,
LG1891	4971	One forenoon the she-bird crouch'd not on the nest,
LG1891	4972	Nor return'd that afternoon, nor the next,
LG1891	4973	Nor ever appear'd again.
LG1891	4974	And thenceforward all summer in the sound of the sea,

LG1891	4979	The solitary guest from Alabama.
LG1891	4980	Blow! blow! blow!
LG1891	4981	Blow up sea-winds along Paumanok's shore;
LG1891	4982	I wait and I wait till you blow my mate to me.

LG1891	4988	He pour'd forth the meanings which I of all men know.
LG1891	4989	Yes my brother I know,
LG1891	4990	The rest might not, but I have treasur'd every note,

LG1891	5242	Weep not, my darling,
LG1891	5243	With these kisses let me remove your tears,
LG1891	5244	The ravening clouds shall not long be victorious,
LG1891	5245	They shall not long possess the sky, they devour the stars only in apparition,
LG1891	5246	Jupiter shall emerge, be patient, watch again another night, the Pleiades shall emerge,
LG1891	5247	They are immortal, all those stars both silvery and golden shall shine out again,
LG1891	5248	The great stars and the little ones shall shine out again, they endure,
LG1891	5249	The vast immortal suns and the long-enduring pensive moons shall again shine.
LG1891	5250	Then dearest child mournest thou only for Jupiter?
LG1891	5251	Considerest thou alone the burial of the stars?
LG1891	5252	Something there is,
LG1891	5253	(With my lips soothing thee, adding I whisper,
LG1891	5254	I give thee the first suggestion, the problem and indirection,)
LG1891	5255	Something there is more immortal even than the stars,
LG1891	5256	(Many the burials, many the days and nights, passing away,)
LG1891	5257	Something that shall endure longer even than lustrous Jupiter,
LG1891	5258	Longer than sun or any revolving satellite,
LG1891	5259	Or the radiant sisters the Pleiades.
LG1891	5260	THE world below the brine,
LG1891	5261	Forests at the bottom of the sea, the branches and leaves,
LG1891	5262	Sea-lettuce, vast lichens, strange flowers and seeds, the thick tangle,openings, and pink turf,
LG1891	5263	Different colors, pale gray and green, purple, white, and gold, the play of light through the water,
LG1891	5264	Dumb swimmers there among the rocks, coral, gluten, grass, rushes,and the aliment of the swimmers,
LG1891	5265	Sluggish existences grazing there suspended, or slowly crawling close to the bottom,
LG1891	5266	The sperm-whale at the surface blowing air and spray, or disporting with his flukes,
LG1891	5267	The leaden-eyed shark, the walrus, the turtle, the hairy sea-leopard,and the sting-ray,
LG1891	5268	Passions there, wars, pursuits, tribes, sight in those ocean-depths,breathing that thick-breathing air, as so many do,
LG1891	5269	The change thence to the sight here, and to the subtle air breathed by beings like us who walk this sphere,
LG1891	5270	The change onward from ours to that of beings who walk other spheres.
LG1891	5271	ON the beach at night alone,
LG1891	5272	As the old mother sways her to and fro singing her husky song,
LG1891	5273	As I watch the bright stars shining, I think a thought of the clef of the universes and of the future.
LG1891	5274	A vast similitude interlocks all,
LG1891	5275	All spheres, grown, ungrown, small, large, suns, moons, planets,
LG1891	5276	All distances of place however wide,
LG1891	5277	All distances of time, all inanimate forms,
LG1891	5278	All souls, all living bodies though they be ever so different, or in different worlds,
LG1891	5279	All gaseous, watery, vegetable, mineral processes, the fishes, the brutes,
LG1891	5280	All nations, colors, barbarisms, civilizations, languages,
LG1891	5281	All identities that have existed or may exist on this globe, or any globe,
LG1891	5282	All lives and deaths, all of the past, present, future,
LG1891	5283	This vast similitude spans them, and always has spann'd,
LG1891	5284	And shall forever span them and compactly hold and enclose them.
LG1891	5285	TO-DAY a rude brief recitative,
LG1891	5286	Of ships sailing the seas, each with its special flag or ship-signal,
LG1891	5287	Of unnamed heroes in the ships—of waves spreading and spread-ingspreading far as the eye can reach,
LG1891	5288	Of dashing spray, and the winds piping and blowing,
LG1891	5289	And out of these a chant for the sailors of all nations,
LG1891	5290	Fitful, like a surge.
LG1891	5291	Of sea-captains young or old, and the mates, and of all intrepid sailors,
LG1891	5292	Of the few, very choice, taciturn, whom fate can never surprise nor death dismay,
LG1891	5293	Pick'd sparingly without noise by thee old ocean, chosen by thee,
LG1891	5294	Thou sea that pickest and cullest the race in time, and unitest nations,
LG1891	5295	Suckled by thee, old husky nurse, embodying thee,
LG1891	5296	Indomitable, untamed as thee.
LG1891	5297	(Ever the heroes on water or on land, by ones or twos appearing,
LG1891	5298	Ever the stock preserv'd and never lost, though rare, enough for seed preserv'd.)
LG1891	5299	Flaunt out O sea your separate flags of nations!
LG1891	5300	Flaunt out visible as ever the various ship-signals!
LG1891	5301	But do you reserve especially for yourself and for the soul of man one flag above all the rest,
LG1891	5302	A spiritual woven signal for all nations, emblem of man elate above death,
LG1891	5303	Token of all brave captains and all intrepid sailors and mates,
LG1891	5304	And all that went down doing their duty,
LG1891	5305	Reminiscent of them, twined from all intrepid captains young or old,
LG1891	5306	A pennant universal, subtly waving all time, o'er all brave sailors,
LG1891	5307	All seas, all ships.
LG1891	5308	WILD, wild the storm, and the sea high running,
LG1891	5309	Steady the roar of the gale, with incessant undertone muttering,
LG1891	5310	Shouts of demoniac laughter fitfully piercing and pealing,
LG1891	5311	Waves, air, midnight, their savagest trinity lashing,

LG1891	5319	Along the midnight edge by those milk-white combs careering,
LG1891	5320	A group of dim, weird forms, struggling, the night confronting,
LG1891	5321	That savage trinity warily watching.
LG1891	5322	AFTER the sea-ship, after the whistling winds,
LG1891	5323	After the white-gray sails taut to their spars and ropes,

LG1891	5334	TO get betimes in Boston town I rose this morning early,
LG1891	5335	Here's a good place at the corner, I must stand and see the show.
LG1891	5336	Clear the way there Jonathan!
LG1891	5337	Way for the President's marshal—way for the government cannon!
LG1891	5338	Way for the Federal foot and dragoons, (and the apparitions copiously tumbling.)
LG1891	5339	I love to look on the Stars and Stripes, I hope the fifes will play Yankee Doodle.
LG1891	5340	How bright shine the cutlasses of the foremost troops!
LG1891	5341	Every man holds his revolver, marching stiff through Boston town.
LG1891	5342	A fog follows, antiques of the same come limping,
LG1891	5343	Some appear wooden-legged, and some appear bandaged and bloodless.
LG1891	5344	Why this is indeed a show—it has called the dead out of the earth!
LG1891	5345	The old graveyards of the hills have hurried to see!
LG1891	5346	Phantoms! phantoms countless by flank and rear!
LG1891	5347	Cock'd hats of mothy mould—crutches made of mist!
LG1891	5348	Arms in slings—old men leaning on young men's shoulders.
LG1891	5349	What troubles you Yankee phantoms? what is all this chattering of bare gums?
LG1891	5350	Does the ague convulse your limbs? do you mistake your crutches for firelocks and level them?
LG1891	5351	If you blind your eyes with tears you will not see the President's marshal,
LG1891	5352	If you groan such groans you might balk the government cannon.
LG1891	5353	For shame old maniacs—bring down those toss'd arms, and let your white hair be,
LG1891	5354	Here gape your great grandsons, their wives gaze at them from the windows,
LG1891	5355	See how well dress'd, see how orderly they conduct themselves.
LG1891	5356	Worse and worse—can't you stand it? are you retreating?
LG1891	5357	Is this hour with the living too dead for you?
LG1891	5358	Retreat then—pell-mell!
LG1891	5359	To your graves—back—back to the hills old limpers!
LG1891	5360	I do not think you belong here anyhow.
LG1891	5361	But there is one thing that belongs here—shall I tell you what it is, gentlemen of Boston?
LG1891	5362	I will whisper it to the Mayor, he shall send a committee to England,
LG1891	5363	They shall get a grant from the Parliament, go with a cart to the royal vault,
LG1891	5364	Dig out King George's coffin, unwrap him quick from the grave-clothesgraveclothes, box up his bones for a journey,
LG1891	5365	Find a swift Yankee clipper—here is freight for you, black-bellied clipper,
LG1891	5366	Up with your anchor—shake out your sails—steer straight toward Boston bay.
LG1891	5367	Now call for the President's marshal again, bring out the govern-mentgovernment cannon,
LG1891	5368	Fetch home the roarers from Congress, make another procession,guard it with foot and dragoons.
LG1891	5369	This centre-piece for them;
LG1891	5370	Look, all orderly citizens—look from the windows, women!
LG1891	5371	The committee open the box, set up the regal ribs, glue those that will not stay,
LG1891	5372	Clap the skull on top of the ribs, and clap a crown on top of the skull.
LG1891	5373	You have got your revenge, old buster—the crown is come to its own, and more than its own.
LG1891	5374	Stick your hands in your pockets, Jonathan—you are a made man from this day,
LG1891	5375	You are mighty cute—and here is one of your bargains.
LG1891	5376	SUDDENLY out of its stale and drowsy lair, the lair of slaves,
LG1891	5377	Like lightning it le'pt forth half startled at itself,
LG1891	5378	Its feet upon the ashes and the rags, its hands tight to the throats of kings.
LG1891	5379	O hope and faith!
LG1891	5380	O aching close of exiled patriots' lives!
LG1891	5381	O many a sicken'd heart!
LG1891	5382	Turn back unto this day and make yourselves afresh.
LG1891	5383	And you, paid to defile the People—you liars, mark!
LG1891	5384	Not for numberless agonies, murders, lusts,
LG1891	5385	For court thieving in its manifold mean forms, worming from his simplicity the poor man's wages,
LG1891	5386	For many a promise sworn by royal lips and broken and laugh'd at in the breaking,
LG1891	5387	Then in their power not for all these did the blows strike revenge,or the heads of the nobles fall;
LG1891	5388	The People scorn'd the ferocity of kings.
LG1891	5389	But the sweetness of mercy brew'd bitter destruction, and the frighten'd monarchs come back,
LG1891	5390	Each comes in state with his train, hangman, priest, tax-gatherer,
LG1891	5391	Soldier, lawyer, lord, jailer, and sycophant.
LG1891	5392	Yet behind all lowering stealing, lo, a shape,
LG1891	5393	Vague as the night, draped interminably, head, front and form, in scarlet folds,

LG1891	5395	Out of its robes only this, the red robes lifted by the arm,
LG1891	5396	One finger crook'd pointed high over the top, like the head of a snake appears.
LG1891	5397	Meanwhile corpses lie in new-made graves, bloody corpses of young men,
LG1891	5398	The rope of the gibbet hangs heavily, the bullets of princes are flying, the creatures of power laugh aloud,
LG1891	5399	And all these things bear fruits, and they are good.
LG1891	5400	Those corpses of young men,
LG1891	5401	Those martyrs that hang from the gibbets, those hearts pierc'd by the gray lead,
LG1891	5402	Cold and motionless as they seem live elsewhere with unslaugh-ter'dunslaughter'd vitality.
LG1891	5403	They live in other young men O kings!
LG1891	5404	They live in brothers again ready to defy you,
LG1891	5405	They were purified by death, they were taught and exalted.
LG1891	5406	Not a grave of the murder'd for freedom but grows seed for free-domfreedom, in its turn to bear seed,
LG1891	5407	Which the winds carry afar and re-sow, and the rains and the snows nourish.
LG1891	5408	Not a disembodied spirit can the weapons of tyrants let loose,
LG1891	5409	But it stalks invisibly over the earth, whispering, counseling,cautioning.
LG1891	5410	Liberty, let others despair of you—I never despair of you.
LG1891	5411	Is the house shut? is the master away?
LG1891	5412	Nevertheless, be ready, be not weary of watching,
LG1891	5413	He will soon return, his messengers come anon.
LG1891	5414	HOLD it up sternly—see this it sends back, (who is it? is it you?)
LG1891	5415	Outside fair costume, within ashes and filth,
LG1891	5416	No more a flashing eye, no more a sonorous voice or springy step,
LG1891	5417	Now some slave's eye, voice, hands, step,
LG1891	5418	A drunkard's breath, unwholesome eater's face, venerealee's flesh,

LG1891	5420	Joints rheumatic, bowels clogged with abomination,
LG1891	5421	Blood circulating dark and poisonous streams,
LG1891	5422	Words babble, hearing and touch callous,
LG1891	5423	No brain, no heart left, no magnetism of sex;
LG1891	5424	Such from one look in this looking-glass ere you go hence,
LG1891	5425	Such a result so soon—and from such a beginning!
LG1891	5426	LOVER divine and perfect Comrade,
LG1891	5427	Waiting content, invisible yet, but certain,
LG1891	5428	Be thou my God.
LG1891	5429	Thou, thou, the Ideal Man,
LG1891	5430	Fair, able, beautiful, content, and loving,
LG1891	5431	Complete in body and dilate in spirit,
LG1891	5432	Be thou my God.
LG1891	5433	O Death, (for Life has served its turn,)
LG1891	5434	Opener and usher to the heavenly mansion,
LG1891	5435	Be thou my God.
LG1891	5436	Aught, aught of mightiest, best I see, conceive, or know,

LG1891	5438	Be thou my God.
LG1891	5439	All great ideas, the races' aspirations,
LG1891	5440	All heroisms, deeds of rapt enthusiasts,

LG1891	5447	FORMS, qualities, lives, humanity, language, thoughts,
LG1891	5448	The ones known, and the ones unknown, the ones on the stars,

LG1891	5452	Such-like, and as good as such-like, visible here or anywhere,stand provided for in a handful of space, which I extend my arm and half enclose with my hand,
LG1891	5453	That containing the start of each and all, the virtue, the germs of all.
LG1891	5454	OF ownership—as if one fit to own things could not at pleasure enter upon all, and incorporate them into himself or herself;
LG1891	5455	Of vista—suppose some sight in arriere through the formative chaos, presuming the growth, fulness, life, now attain'd on the journey,

LG1891	5457	Of what was once lacking on earth, and in due time has become supplied—and of what will yet be supplied,
LG1891	5458	Because all I see and know I believe to have its main purport in what will yet be supplied.
LG1891	5459	WHEN I heard the learn'd astronomer,
LG1891	5460	When the proofs, the figures, were ranged in columns before me,
LG1891	5461	When I was shown the charts and diagrams, to add, divide, and measure them,
LG1891	5462	When I sitting heard the astronomer where he lectured with much applause in the lecture-room,
LG1891	5463	How soon unaccountable I became tired and sick,
LG1891	5464	Till rising and gliding out I wander'd off by myself,
LG1891	5465	In the mystical moist night-air, and from time to time,
LG1891	5466	Look'd up in perfect silence at the stars.
LG1891	5467	ONLY themselves understand themselves and the like of themselves,
LG1891	5468	As souls only understand souls.
LG1891	5469	O ME! O life! of the questions of these recurring,
LG1891	5470	Of the endless trains of the faithless, of cities fill'd with the foolish,
LG1891	5471	Of myself forever reproaching myself, (for who more foolish than I, and who more faithless?)
LG1891	5472	Of eyes that vainly crave the light, of the objects mean, of the struggle ever renew'd,
LG1891	5473	Of the poor results of all, of the plodding and sordid crowds I see around me,
LG1891	5474	Of the empty and useless years of the rest, with the rest me inter-twinedintertwined,
LG1891	5475	The question, O me! so sad, recurring—What good amid these,O me, O life?
LG1891	5476	That you are here—that life exists and identity,
LG1891	5477	That the powerful play goes on, and you may contribute a verse.
LG1891	5478	ALL you are doing and saying is to America dangled mirages,
LG1891	5479	You have not learn'd of Nature—of the politics of Nature you have not learn'd the great amplitude, rectitude, impartiality,
LG1891	5480	You have not seen that only such as they are for these States,
LG1891	5481	And that what is less than they must sooner or later lift off from these States.
LG1891	5482	I SIT and look out upon all the sorrows of the world, and upon all oppression and shame,
LG1891	5483	I hear secret convulsive sobs from young men at anguish with themselves, remorseful after deeds done,
LG1891	5484	I see in low life the mother misused by her children, dying,neglected, gaunt, desperate,
LG1891	5485	I see the wife misused by her husband, I see the treacherous seducer of young women,
LG1891	5486	I mark the ranklings of jealousy and unrequited love attempted to be hid, I see these sights on the earth,
LG1891	5487	I see the workings of battle, pestilence, tyranny, I see martyrs and prisoners,
LG1891	5488	I observe a famine at sea, I observe the sailors casting lots who shall be kill'd to preserve the lives of the rest,
LG1891	5489	I observe the slights and degradations cast by arrogant persons upon laborers, the poor, and upon negroes, and the like;
LG1891	5490	All these—all the meanness and agony without end I sitting look out upon,
LG1891	5491	See, hear, and am silent.
LG1891	5492	WHAT you give me I cheerfully accept,
LG1891	5493	A little sustenance, a hut and garden, a little money, as I rendez-vousrendezvous with my poems,
LG1891	5494	A traveler's lodging and breakfast as I journey through the States,—why should I be ashamed to own such gifts? why to advertise for them?
LG1891	5495	For I myself am not one who bestows nothing upon man and woman,
LG1891	5496	For I bestow upon any man or woman the entrance to all the gifts of the universe.
LG1891	5497	SKIRTING the river road, (my forenoon walk, my rest,)
LG1891	5498	Skyward in air a sudden muffled sound, the dalliance of the eagles,
LG1891	5499	The rushing amorous contact high in space together,
LG1891	5500	The clinching interlocking claws, a living, fierce, gyrating wheel,
LG1891	5501	Four beating wings, two beaks, a swirling mass tight grappling,
LG1891	5502	In tumbling turning clustering loops, straight downward falling,
LG1891	5503	Till o'er the river pois'd, the twain yet one, a moment's lull,
LG1891	5504	A motionless still balance in the air, then parting, talons loosing,
LG1891	5505	Upward again on slow-firm pinions slanting, their separate diverse flight,
LG1891	5506	She hers, he his, pursuing.
LG1891	5507	Roaming in thought over the Universe, I saw the little that is Good steadily hastening towards immortality,
LG1891	5508	And the vast all that is call'd Evil I saw hastening to merge itself and become lost and dead.
LG1891	5509	THROUGH the ample open door of the peaceful country barn,
LG1891	5510	A sunlit pasture field with cattle and horses feeding,
LG1891	5511	And haze and vista, and the far horizon fading away.
LG1891	5512	SILENT and amazed even when a little boy,
LG1891	5513	I remember I heard the preacher every Sunday put God in his statements,
LG1891	5514	As contending against some being or influence.
LG1891	5515	ON a flat road runs the well-train'd runner,
LG1891	5516	He is lean and sinewy with muscular legs,
LG1891	5517	He is thinly clothed, he leans forward as he runs,
LG1891	5518	With lightly closed fists and arms partially rais'd.
LG1891	5519	WOMEN sit or move to and fro, some old, some young,
LG1891	5520	The young are beautiful—but the old are more beautiful than the young.
LG1891	5521	I SEE the sleeping babe nestling the breast of its mother,
LG1891	5522	The sleeping mother and babe—hush'd, I study them long and long.
LG1891	5523	OF obedience, faith, adhesiveness;
LG1891	5524	As I stand aloof and look there is to me something profoundly affecting in large masses of men following the lead of those who do not believe in men.
LG1891	5525	A MASK, a perpetual natural disguiser of herself,
LG1891	5526	Concealing her face, concealing her form,
LG1891	5527	Changes and transformations every hour, every moment,
LG1891	5528	Falling upon her even when she sleeps.
LG1891	5529	OF Justice—as if Justice could be any thing but the same ample law, expounded by natural judges and saviors,
LG1891	5530	As if it might be this thing or that thing, according to decisions.
LG1891	5531	GLIDING o'er all, through all,

LG1891	5533	As a ship on the waters advancing,
LG1891	5534	The voyage of the soul—not life alone,
LG1891	5535	Death, many deaths I'll sing.
LG1891	5536	HAST never come to thee an hour,
LG1891	5537	A sudden gleam divine, precipitating, bursting all these bubbles,fashions, wealth?
LG1891	5538	These eager business aims—books, politics, art, amours,
LG1891	5539	To utter nothingness?
LG1891	5540	OF Equality—as if it harm'd me, giving others the same chances and rights as myself—as if it were not indispensable to my own rights that others possess the same.
LG1891	5541	I SEE in you the estuary that enlarges and spreads itself grandly as it pours in the great sea.
LG1891	5542	LOCATIONS and times—what is it in me that meets them all, when-everwhenever and wherever, and makes me at home?
LG1891	5543	Forms, colors, densities, odors—what is it in me that corresponds with them?
LG1891	5544	A THOUSAND perfect men and women appear,
LG1891	5545	Around each gathers a cluster of friends, and gay children and youths, with offerings.
LG1891	5546	WHY reclining, interrogating? why myself and all drowsing?
LG1891	5547	What deepening twilight—scum floating atop of the waters,

LG1891	5551	Then I will sleep awhile yet, for I see that these States sleep, for reasons;
LG1891	5552	(With gathering murk, with muttering thunder and lambent shoots we all duly awake,
LG1891	5553	South, North, East, West, inland and seaboard, we will surely awake.)
LG1891	5554	FIRST O songs for a prelude,
LG1891	5555	Lightly strike on the stretch'd tympanum pride and joy in my city,
LG1891	5556	How she led the rest to arms, how she gave the cue,
LG1891	5557	How at once with lithe limbs unwaiting a moment she sprang,
LG1891	5558	(O superb! O Manhattan, my own, my peerless!
LG1891	5559	O strongest you in the hour of danger, in crisis! O truer than steel!)
LG1891	5560	How you sprang—how you threw off the costumes of peace with indifferent hand,

LG1891	5562	How you led to the war, (that shall serve for our prelude, songs of soldiers,)
LG1891	5563	How Manhattan drum-taps led.
LG1891	5564	Forty years had I in my city seen soldiers parading,
LG1891	5565	Forty years as a pageant, till unawares the lady of this teeming and turbulent city,
LG1891	5566	Sleepless amid her ships, her houses, her incalculable wealth,
LG1891	5567	With her million children around her, suddenly,
LG1891	5568	At dead of night, at news from the south,
LG1891	5569	Incens'd struck with clinch'd hand the pavement.
LG1891	5570	A shock electric, the night sustain'd it,
LG1891	5571	Till with ominous hum our hive at daybreak pour'd out its myriads.
LG1891	5572	From the houses then and the workshops, and through all the doorways,
LG1891	5573	Leapt they tumultuous, and lo! Manhattan arming.
LG1891	5574	To the drum-taps prompt,
LG1891	5575	The young men falling in and arming,
LG1891	5576	The mechanics arming, (the trowel, the jack-plane, the black-smith'sblacksmith's hammer, tost aside with precipitation,)
LG1891	5577	The lawyer leaving his office and arming, the judge leaving the court,
LG1891	5578	The driver deserting his wagon in the street, jumping down,throwing the reins abruptly down on the horses' backs,
LG1891	5579	The salesman leaving the store, the boss, book-keeper, porter, all leaving;
LG1891	5580	Squads gather everywhere by common consent and arm,
LG1891	5581	The new recruits, even boys, the old men show them how to wear their accoutrements, they buckle the straps carefully,
LG1891	5582	Outdoors arming, indoors arming, the flash of the musket-barrels,
LG1891	5583	The white tents cluster in camps, the arm'd sentries around, the sunrise cannon and again at sunset,
LG1891	5584	Arm'd regiments arrive every day, pass through the city, and embark from the wharves,
LG1891	5585	(How good they look as they tramp down to the river, sweaty,with their guns on their shoulders!
LG1891	5586	How I love them! how I could hug them, with their brown faces and their clothes and knapsacks cover'd with dust!)
LG1891	5587	The blood of the city up—arm'd! arm'd! the cry everywhere,
LG1891	5588	The flags flung out from the steeples of churches and from all the public buildings and stores,
LG1891	5589	The tearful parting, the mother kisses her son, the son kisses his mother,
LG1891	5590	(Loth is the mother to part, yet not a word does she speak to detain him,)
LG1891	5591	The tumultuous escort, the ranks of policemen preceding, clearing the way,
LG1891	5592	The unpent enthusiasm, the wild cheers of the crowd for their favorites,
LG1891	5593	The artillery, the silent cannons bright as gold, drawn along,rumble lightly over the stones,
LG1891	5594	(Silent cannons, soon to cease your silence,
LG1891	5595	Soon unlimber'd to begin the red business;)
LG1891	5596	All the mutter of preparation, all the determin'd arming,
LG1891	5597	The hospital service, the lint, bandages and medicines,
LG1891	5598	The women volunteering for nurses, the work begun for in earnest,no mere parade now;
LG1891	5599	War! an arm'd race is advancing! the welcome for battle, no turning away;
LG1891	5600	War! be it weeks, months, or years, an arm'd race is advancing to welcome it.
LG1891	5601	Mannahatta a-march—and it's O to sing it well!
LG1891	5602	It's O for a manly life in the camp.
LG1891	5603	And the sturdy artillery,
LG1891	5604	The guns bright as gold, the work for giants, to serve well the guns,
LG1891	5605	Unlimber them! (no more as the past forty years for salutes for courtesies merely,
LG1891	5606	Put in something now besides powder and wadding.)
LG1891	5607	And you lady of ships, you Mannahatta,
LG1891	5608	Old matron of this proud, friendly, turbulent city,
LG1891	5609	Often in peace and wealth you were pensive or covertly frown'd amid all your children,
LG1891	5610	But now you smile with joy exulting old Mannahatta.
LG1891	5611	ARM'D year—year of the struggle,
LG1891	5612	No dainty rhymes or sentimental love verses for you terrible year,
LG1891	5613	Not you as some pale poetling seated at a desk lisping cadenzas piano,
LG1891	5614	But as a strong man erect, clothed in blue clothes, advancing,carrying a rifle on your shoulder,
LG1891	5615	With well-gristled body and sunburnt face and hands, with a knife in the belt at your side,
LG1891	5616	As I heard you shouting loud, your sonorous voice ringing across the continent,
LG1891	5617	Your masculine voice O year, as rising amid the great cities,
LG1891	5618	Amid the men of Manhattan I saw you as one of the workmen,the dwellers in Manhattan,
LG1891	5619	Or with large steps crossing the prairies out of Illinois and Indiana,
LG1891	5620	Rapidly crossing the West with springy gait and descending the Alleghanies,
LG1891	5621	Or down from the great lakes or in Pennsylvania, or on deck along the Ohio river,
LG1891	5622	Or southward along the Tennessee or Cumberland rivers, or at Chattanooga on the mountain top,
LG1891	5623	Saw I your gait and saw I your sinewy limbs clothed in blue,bearing weapons, robust year,
LG1891	5624	Heard your determin'd voice launch'd forth again and again,
LG1891	5625	Year that suddenly sang by the mouths of the round-lipp'd cannon,
LG1891	5626	I repeat you, hurrying, crashing, sad, distracted year.
LG1891	5627	BEAT! beat! drums!—blow! bugles! blow!
LG1891	5628	Through the windows—through doors—burst like a ruthless force,
LG1891	5629	Into the solemn church, and scatter the congregation,
LG1891	5630	Into the school where the scholar is studying;
LG1891	5631	Leave not the bridegroom quiet—no happiness must he have now with his bride,
LG1891	5632	Nor the peaceful farmer any peace, ploughing his field or gathering his grain,
LG1891	5633	So fierce you whirr and pound you drums—so shrill you bugles blow.
LG1891	5634	Beat! beat! drums!—blow! bugles! blow!
LG1891	5635	Over the traffic of cities—over the rumble of wheels in the streets;
LG1891	5636	Are beds prepared for sleepers at night in the houses? no sleepers must sleep in those beds,
LG1891	5637	No bargainers' bargains by day—no brokers or speculators—would they continue?
LG1891	5638	Would the talkers be talking? would the singer attempt to sing?
LG1891	5639	Would the lawyer rise in the court to state his case before the judge?
LG1891	5640	Then rattle quicker, heavier drums—you bugles wilder blow.
LG1891	5641	Beat! beat! drums!—blow! bugles! blow!
LG1891	5642	Make no parley—stop for no expostulation,
LG1891	5643	Mind not the timid—mind not the weeper or prayer,
LG1891	5644	Mind not the old man beseeching the young man,
LG1891	5645	Let not the child's voice be heard, nor the mother's entreaties,
LG1891	5646	Make even the trestles to shake the dead where they lie awaiting the hearses,
LG1891	5647	So strong you thump O terrible drums—so loud you bugles blow.
LG1891	5648	FROM Paumanok starting I fly like a bird,
LG1891	5649	Around and around to soar to sing the idea of all,
LG1891	5650	To the north betaking myself to sing there arctic songs,
LG1891	5651	To KanadaCanada till I absorb KanadaCanada in myself, to Michigan then,
LG1891	5652	To Wisconsin, Iowa, Minnesota, to sing their songs, (they are inimitable;)
LG1891	5653	Then to Ohio and Indiana to sing theirs, to Missouri and Kansas and Arkansas to sing theirs,
LG1891	5654	To Tennessee and Kentucky, to the Carolinas and Georgia to sing theirs,
LG1891	5655	To Texas and so along up toward California, to roam accepted everywhere;
LG1891	5656	To sing first, (to the tap of the war-drum if need be,)
LG1891	5657	The idea of all, of the Western world one and inseparable,
LG1891	5658	And then the song of each member of these States.
LG1891	5659	O A new song, a free song,
LG1891	5660	Flapping, flapping, flapping, flapping, by sounds, by voices clearer,
LG1891	5661	By the wind's voice and that of the drum,
LG1891	5662	By the banner's voice and child's voice and sea's voice and father's voice,
LG1891	5663	Low on the ground and high in the air,
LG1891	5664	On the ground where father and child stand,
LG1891	5665	In the upward air where their eyes turn,
LG1891	5666	Where the banner at daybreak is flapping.
LG1891	5667	Words! book-words! what are you?
LG1891	5668	Words no more, for hearken and see,
LG1891	5669	My song is there in the open air, and I must sing,
LG1891	5670	With the banner and pennant a-flapping.
LG1891	5671	I'll weave the chord and twine in,
LG1891	5672	Man's desire and babe's desire, I'll twine them in, I'll put in life,
LG1891	5673	I'll put the bayonet's flashing point, I'll let bullets and slugs whizz,
LG1891	5674	(As one carrying a symbol and menace far into the future,
LG1891	5675	Crying with trumpet voice, Arouse and beware! Beware and arouse!)
LG1891	5676	I'll pour the verse with streams of blood, full of volition, full of joy,
LG1891	5677	Then loosen, launch forth, to go and compete,
LG1891	5678	With the banner and pennant a-flapping.
LG1891	5679	Come up here, bard, bard,
LG1891	5680	Come up here, soul, soul,
LG1891	5681	Come up here, dear little child,
LG1891	5682	To fly in the clouds and winds with me, and play with the measure-lessmeasureless light.
LG1891	5683	Father what is that in the sky beckoning to me with long finger?
LG1891	5684	And what does it say to me all the while?
LG1891	5685	Nothing my babe you see in the sky,
LG1891	5686	And nothing at all to you it says—but look you my babe,
LG1891	5687	Look at these dazzling things in the houses, and see you the money-shops opening,
LG1891	5688	And see you the vehicles preparing to crawl along the streets with goods;
LG1891	5689	These, ah these, how valued and toil'd for these!
LG1891	5690	How envied by all the earth.
LG1891	5691	Fresh and rosy red the sun is mounting high,
LG1891	5692	On floats the sea in distant blue careering through its channels,
LG1891	5693	On floats the wind over the breast of the sea setting in toward land,
LG1891	5694	The great steady wind from west or west-by-south,
LG1891	5695	Floating so buoyant with milk-white foam on the waters.
LG1891	5696	But I am not the sea nor the red sun,
LG1891	5697	I am not the wind with girlish laughter,
LG1891	5698	Not the immense wind which strengthens, not the wind which lashes,
LG1891	5699	Not the spirit that ever lashes its own body to terror and death,
LG1891	5700	But I am that which unseen comes and sings, sings, sings,
LG1891	5701	Which babbles in brooks and scoots in showers on the land,
LG1891	5702	Which the birds know in the woods mornings and evenings,
LG1891	5703	And the shore-sands know and the hissing wave, and that banner and pennant,
LG1891	5704	Aloft there flapping and flapping.
LG1891	5705	O father it is alive—it is full of people—it has children,
LG1891	5706	O now it seems to me it is talking to its children,
LG1891	5707	I hear it—it talks to me—O it is wonderful!
LG1891	5708	O it stretches—it spreads and runs so fast—O my father,
LG1891	5709	It is so broad it covers the whole sky.
LG1891	5710	Cease, cease, my foolish babe,
LG1891	5711	What you are saying is sorrowful to me, much it displeases me;
LG1891	5712	Behold with the rest again I say, behold not banners and pennants aloft,
LG1891	5713	But the well-prepared pavements behold, and mark the solid-wall'd houses.
LG1891	5714	Speak to the child O bard out of Manhattan,
LG1891	5715	To our children all, or north or south of Manhattan,
LG1891	5716	Point this day, leaving all the rest, to us over all—and yet we know not why,
LG1891	5717	For what are we, mere strips of cloth profiting nothing,
LG1891	5718	Only flapping in the wind?
LG1891	5719	I hear and see not strips of cloth alone,
LG1891	5720	I hear the tramp of armies, I hear the challenging sentry,
LG1891	5721	I hear the jubilant shouts of millions of men, I hear Liberty!
LG1891	5722	I hear the drums beat and the trumpets blowing,
LG1891	5723	I myself move abroad swift-rising flying then,
LG1891	5724	I use the wings of the land-bird and use the wings of the sea-bird, and look down as from a height,
LG1891	5725	I do not deny the precious results of peace, I see populous cities with wealth incalculable,
LG1891	5726	I see numberless farms, I see the farmers working in their fields or barns,
LG1891	5727	I see mechanics working, I see buildings everywhere founded, going up, or finish'd,
LG1891	5728	I see trains of cars swiftly speeding along railroad tracks drawn by the locomotives,
LG1891	5729	I see the stores, depots, of Boston, Baltimore, Charleston, New Orleans,
LG1891	5730	I see far in the West the immense area of grain, I dwell awhile hovering,
LG1891	5731	I pass to the lumber forests of the North, and again to the South-ernSouthern plantation, and again to California;
LG1891	5732	Sweeping the whole I see the countless profit, the busy gatherings, earn'd wages,
LG1891	5733	See the Identity formed out of thirty-eight spacious and haughty States, (and many more to come,)
LG1891	5734	See forts on the shores of harbors, see ships sailing in and out;
LG1891	5735	Then over all, (aye! aye!) my little and lengthen'd pennant shaped like a sword,
LG1891	5736	Runs swiftly up indicating war and defiance—and now the hal-yardshalyards have rais'd it,
LG1891	5737	Side of my banner broad and blue, side of my starry banner,
LG1891	5738	Discarding peace over all the sea and land.
LG1891	5739	Yet louder, higher, stronger, bard! yet farther, wider cleave!
LG1891	5740	No longer let our children deem us riches and peace alone,
LG1891	5741	We may be terror and carnage, and are so now,
LG1891	5742	Not now are we any one of these spacious and haughty States, (nor any five, nor ten,)
LG1891	5743	Nor market nor depot we, nor money-bank in the city,
LG1891	5744	But these and all, and the brown and spreading land, and the mines below, are ours,
LG1891	5745	And the shores of the sea are ours, and the rivers great and small,
LG1891	5746	And the fields they moisten, and the crops and the fruits are ours,
LG1891	5747	Bays and channels and ships sailing in and out are ours—while we over all,
LG1891	5748	Over the area spread below, the three or four millions of square miles, the capitals,
LG1891	5749	The forty millions of people,—O bard! in life and death supreme,
LG1891	5750	We, even we, henceforth flaunt out masterful, high up above,
LG1891	5751	Not for the present alone, for a thousand years chanting through you,
LG1891	5752	This song to the soul of one poor little child.
LG1891	5753	O my father I like not the houses,
LG1891	5754	They will never to me be any thing, nor do I like money,
LG1891	5755	But to mount up there I would like, O father dear, that banner I like,
LG1891	5756	That pennant I would be and must be.
LG1891	5757	Child of mine you fill me with anguish,
LG1891	5758	To be that pennant would be too fearful,
LG1891	5759	Little you know what it is this day, and after this day, forever,
LG1891	5760	It is to gain nothing, but risk and defy every thing,
LG1891	5761	Forward to stand in front of wars—and O, such wars!—what have you to do with them?
LG1891	5762	With passions of demons, slaughter, premature death?
LG1891	5763	Demons and death then I sing,
LG1891	5764	Put in all, aye all will I, sword-shaped pennant for war,
LG1891	5765	And a pleasure new and ecstatic, and the prattled yearning of children,
LG1891	5766	Blent with the sounds of the peaceful land and the liquid wash of the sea,
LG1891	5767	And the black ships fighting on the sea envelop'd in smoke,
LG1891	5768	And the icy cool of the far, far north, with rustling cedars and pines,
LG1891	5769	And the whirr of drums and the sound of soldiers marching, and the hot sun shining south,
LG1891	5770	And the beach-waves combing over the beach on my Eastern shore, and my Western shore the same,
LG1891	5771	And all between those shores, and my ever running Mississippi with bends and chutes,
LG1891	5772	And my Illinois fields, and my Kansas fields, and my fields of Missouri,
LG1891	5773	The Continent, devoting the whole identity without reserving an atom,
LG1891	5774	Pour in! whelm that which asks, which sings, with all and the yield of all,
LG1891	5775	Fusing and holding, claiming, devouring the whole,
LG1891	5776	No more with tender lip, nor musical labial sound,
LG1891	5777	But out of the night emerging for good, our voice persuasive no more,
LG1891	5778	Croaking like crows here in the wind.
LG1891	5779	My limbs, my veins dilate, my theme is clear at last,
LG1891	5780	Banner so broad advancing out of the night, I sing you haughty and resolute,
LG1891	5781	I burst through where I waited long, too long, deafen'd and blinded,
LG1891	5782	My hearing and tongue are come to me, (a little child taught me,)
LG1891	5783	I hear from above O pennant of war your ironical call and demand,
LG1891	5784	Insensate! insensate! (yet I at any rate chant you,) O banner!
LG1891	5785	Not houses of peace indeed are you, nor any nor all their pros-perityprosperity, (if need be, you shall again have every one of those houses to destroy them,
LG1891	5786	You thought not to destroy those valuable houses, standing fast, full of comfort, built with money,
LG1891	5787	May they stand fast, then? not an hour except you above them and all stand fast;)
LG1891	5788	O banner, not money so precious are you, not farm produce you, nor the material good nutriment,
LG1891	5789	Nor excellent stores, nor landed on wharves from the ships,
LG1891	5790	Not the superb ships with sail-power or steam-power, fetching and carrying cargoes,
LG1891	5791	Nor machinery, vehicles, trade, nor revenues—but you as hence-forthhenceforth I see you,
LG1891	5792	Running up out of the night, bringing your cluster of stars, (ever-enlargingever-enlarging stars,)
LG1891	5793	Divider of daybreak you, cutting the air, touch'd by the sun, measuring the sky,
LG1891	5794	(Passionately seen and yearn'd for by one poor little child,
LG1891	5795	While others remain busy or smartly talking, forever teaching thrift, thrift;)
LG1891	5796	O you up there! O pennant! where you undulate like a snake hissing so curious,
LG1891	5797	Out of reach, an idea only, yet furiously fought for, risking bloody death, loved by me,
LG1891	5798	So loved—O you banner leading the day with stars brought from the night!
LG1891	5799	Valueless, object of eyes, over all and demanding all—(absolute owner of all)—O banner and pennant!
LG1891	5800	I too leave the rest—great as it is, it is nothing—houses, machines are nothing—I see them not,
LG1891	5801	I see but you, O warlike pennant! O banner so broad, with stripes, I sing you only,
LG1891	5802	Flapping up there in the wind.
LG1891	5803	RISE O days from your fathomless deeps, till you loftier, fiercer sweep,
LG1891	5804	Long for my soul hungering gymnastic I devour'd what the earth gave me,
LG1891	5805	Long I roam'd the woods of the north, long I watch'd Niagara pouring,
LG1891	5806	I travel'd the prairies over and slept on their breast, I cross'd the Nevadas, I cross'd the plateaus,
LG1891	5807	I ascended the towering rocks along the Pacific, I sail'd out to sea,
LG1891	5808	I sail'd through the storm, I was refresh'd by the storm,
LG1891	5809	I watch'd with joy the threatening maws of the waves,
LG1891	5810	I mark'd the white combs where they career'd so high, curling over,
LG1891	5811	I heard the wind piping, I saw the black clouds,
LG1891	5812	Saw from below what arose and mounted, (O superb! O wild as my heart, and powerful!)
LG1891	5813	Heard the continuous thunder as it bellow'd after the lightning,
LG1891	5814	Noted the slender and jagged threads of lightning as sudden and fast amid the din they chased each other across the sky;
LG1891	5815	These, and such as these, I, elate, saw—saw with wonder, yet pensive and masterful,
LG1891	5816	All the menacing might of the globe uprisen around me,
LG1891	5817	Yet there with my soul I fed, I fed content, supercilious.
LG1891	5818	'Twas well, O soul—'twas a good preparation you gave me,
LG1891	5819	Now we advance our latent and ampler hunger to fill,
LG1891	5820	Now we go forth to receive what the earth and the sea never gave us,
LG1891	5821	Not through the mighty woods we go, but through the mightier cities,
LG1891	5822	Something for us is pouring now more than Niagara pouring,
LG1891	5823	Torrents of men, (sources and rills of the Northwest are you indeed inexhaustible?)
LG1891	5824	What, to pavements and homesteads here, what were those storms of the mountains and sea?
LG1891	5825	What, to passions I witness around me to-day? was the sea risen?
LG1891	5826	Was the wind piping the pipe of death under the black clouds?
LG1891	5827	Lo! from deeps more unfathomable, something more deadly and savage,
LG1891	5828	Manhattan rising, advancing with menacing front—Cincinnati, Chicago, unchain'd;
LG1891	5829	What was that swell I saw on the ocean? behold what comes here,
LG1891	5830	How it climbs with daring feet and hands—how it dashes!
LG1891	5831	How the true thunder bellows after the lightning—how bright the flashes of lightning!
LG1891	5832	How Democracy with desperate vengeful port strides on, shown through the dark by those flashes of lightning!
LG1891	5833	(Yet a mournful wail and low sob I fancied I heard through the dark,
LG1891	5834	In a lull of the deafening confusion.)
LG1891	5835	Thunder on! stride on, Democracy! strike with vengeful stroke!
LG1891	5836	And do you rise higher than ever yet O days, O cities!
LG1891	5837	Crash heavier, heavier yet O storms! you have done me good,
LG1891	5838	My soul prepared in the mountains absorbs your immortal strong nutriment,
LG1891	5839	Long had I walk'd my cities, my country roads through farms, only half satisfied,
LG1891	5840	One doubt nauseous undulating like a snake, crawl'd on the ground before me,
LG1891	5841	Continually preceding my steps, turning upon me oft, ironically hissing low;
LG1891	5842	The cities I loved so well I abandon'd and left, I sped to the certainties suitable to me,
LG1891	5843	Hungering, hungering, hungering, for primal energies and Nature's dauntlessness,
LG1891	5844	I refresh'd myself with it only, I could relish it only,
LG1891	5845	I waited the bursting forth of the pent fire—on the water and air I waited long;
LG1891	5846	But now I no longer wait, I am fully satisfied, I am glutted,
LG1891	5847	I have witness'd the true lightning, I have witness'd my cities electric,
LG1891	5848	I have lived to behold man burst forth and warlike America rise,
LG1891	5849	Hence I will seek no more the food of the northern solitary wilds,
LG1891	5850	No more the mountains roam or sail the stormy sea.
LG1891	5851	THE noble sire fallen on evil days,
LG1891	5852	I saw with hand uplifted, menacing, brandishing,
LG1891	5853	(Memories of old in abeyance, love and faith in abeyance,)
LG1891	5854	The insane knife toward the Mother of All.
LG1891	5855	The noble son on sinewy feet advancing,
LG1891	5856	I saw, out of the land of prairies, land of Ohio's waters and of Indiana,
LG1891	5857	To the rescue the stalwart giant hurry his plenteous offspring,
LG1891	5858	Drest in blue, bearing their trusty rifles on their shoulders.
LG1891	5859	Then the Mother of All with calm voice speaking,
LG1891	5860	As to you Rebellious, (I seemed to hear her say,) why strive against me, and why seek my life?
LG1891	5861	When you yourself forever provide to defend me?
LG1891	5862	For you provided me Washington—and now these also.
LG1891	5863	CITY of ships!
LG1891	5864	(O the black ships! O the fierce ships!
LG1891	5865	O the beautiful sharp-bow'd steam-ships and sail-ships!)
LG1891	5866	City of the world! (for all races are here,
LG1891	5867	All the lands of the earth make contributions here;)
LG1891	5868	City of the sea! city of hurried and glittering tides!
LG1891	5869	City whose gleeful tides continually rush or recede, whirling in and out with eddies and foam!
LG1891	5870	City of wharves and stores—city of tall façades of marble and iron!
LG1891	5871	Proud and passionate city—mettlesome, mad, extravagant city!
LG1891	5872	Spring up O city—not for peace alone, but be indeed yourself,warlike!
LG1891	5873	Fear not—submit to no models but your own O city!
LG1891	5874	Behold me—incarnate me as I have incarnated you!
LG1891	5875	I have rejected nothing you offer'd me—whom you adopted I have adopted,
LG1891	5876	Good or bad I never question you—I love all—I do not con-demncondemn any thing,
LG1891	5877	I chant and celebrate all that is yours—yet peace no more,
LG1891	5878	In peace I chanted peace, but now the drum of war is mine,
LG1891	5879	War, red war is my song through your streets, O city!
LG1891	5880	GIVE me your hand old Revolutionary,
LG1891	5881	The hill-top is nigh, but a few steps, (make room gentlemen,)
LG1891	5882	Up the path you have follow'd me well, spite of your hundred and extra years,
LG1891	5883	You can walk old man, though your eyes are almost done,
LG1891	5884	Your faculties serve you, and presently I must have them serve me.
LG1891	5885	Rest, while I tell what the crowd around us means,
LG1891	5886	On the plain below recruits are drilling and exercising,
LG1891	5887	There is the camp, one regiment departs to-morrow,
LG1891	5888	Do you hear the officers giving their orders?
LG1891	5889	Do you hear the clank of the muskets?
LG1891	5890	Why what comes over you now old man?
LG1891	5891	Why do you tremble and clutch my hand so convulsively?
LG1891	5892	The troops are but drilling, they are yet surrounded with smiles,
LG1891	5893	Around them at hand the well-drest friends and the women,
LG1891	5894	While splendid and warm the afternoon sun shines down,
LG1891	5895	Green the midsummer verdure and fresh blows the dallying breeze,
LG1891	5896	O'er proud and peaceful cities and arm of the sea between.
LG1891	5897	But drill and parade are over, they march back to quarters,
LG1891	5898	Only hear that approval of hands! hear what a clapping!
LG1891	5899	As wending the crowds now part and disperse—but we old man,
LG1891	5900	Not for nothing have I brought you hither—we must remain,
LG1891	5901	You to speak in your turn, and I to listen and tell.
LG1891	5902	When I clutch'd your hand it was not with terror,
LG1891	5903	But suddenly pouring about me here on every side,
LG1891	5904	And below there where the boys were drilling, and up the slopes they ran,
LG1891	5905	And where tents are pitch'd, and wherever you see south and south-east and south-west,
LG1891	5906	Over hills, across lowlands, and in the skirts of woods,
LG1891	5907	And along the shores, in mire (now fill'd over) came again and suddenly raged,
LG1891	5908	As eighty-five years a-gone no mere parade receiv'd with applause of friends,
LG1891	5909	But a battle which I took part in myself—aye, long ago as it is, I took part in it,
LG1891	5910	Walking then this hilltop, this same ground.
LG1891	5911	Aye, this is the ground,
LG1891	5912	My blind eyes even as I speak behold it re-peopled from graves,
LG1891	5913	The years recede, pavements and stately houses disappear,
LG1891	5914	Rude forts appear again, the old hoop'd guns are mounted,
LG1891	5915	I see the lines of rais'd earth stretching from river to bay,
LG1891	5916	I mark the vista of waters, I mark the uplands and slopes;
LG1891	5917	Here we lay encamp'd, it was this time in summer also.
LG1891	5918	As I talk I remember all, I remember the Declaration,
LG1891	5919	It was read here, the whole army paraded, it was read to us here,
LG1891	5920	By his staff surrounded the General stood in the middle, he held up his unsheath'd sword,
LG1891	5921	It glitter'd in the sun in full sight of the army.
LG1891	5922	'Twas a bold act then—the English war-ships had just arrived,
LG1891	5923	We could watch down the lower bay where they lay at anchor,
LG1891	5924	And the transports swarming with soldiers.
LG1891	5925	A few days more and they landed, and then the battle.
LG1891	5926	Twenty thousand were brought against us,
LG1891	5927	A veteran force furnish'd with good artillery.
LG1891	5928	I tell not now the whole of the battle,
LG1891	5929	But one brigade early in the forenoon order'd forward to engage the red-coats,
LG1891	5930	Of that brigade I tell, and how steadily it march'd,
LG1891	5931	And how long and well it stood confronting death.
LG1891	5932	Who do you think that was marching steadily sternly confronting death?
LG1891	5933	It was the brigade of the youngest men, two thousand strong,
LG1891	5934	Rais'd in Virginia and Maryland, and most of them known per-sonallypersonally to the General.
LG1891	5935	Jauntily forward they went with quick step toward Gowanus' waters,
LG1891	5936	Till of a sudden unlook'd for by defiles through the woods, gain'd at night,
LG1891	5937	The British advancing, rounding in from the east, fiercely playing their guns,
LG1891	5938	That brigade of the youngest was cut off and at the enemy's mercy.
LG1891	5939	The General watch'd them from this hill,
LG1891	5940	They made repeated desperate attempts to burst their environment,
LG1891	5941	Then drew close together, very compact, their flag flying in the middle,
LG1891	5942	But O from the hills how the cannon were thinning and thinning them!
LG1891	5943	It sickens me yet, that slaughter!
LG1891	5944	I saw the moisture gather in drops on the face of the General.
LG1891	5945	I saw how he wrung his hands in anguish.
LG1891	5946	Meanwhile the British manoeuvr'd to draw us out for a pitch'd battle,
LG1891	5947	But we dared not trust the chances of a pitch'd battle.
LG1891	5948	We fought the fight in detachments,
LG1891	5949	Sallying forth we fought at several points, but in each the luck was against us,
LG1891	5950	Our foe advancing, steadily getting the best of it, push'd us back to the works on this hill,
LG1891	5951	Till we turn'd menacing here, and then he left us.
LG1891	5952	That was the going out of the brigade of the youngest men, two thousand strong,
LG1891	5953	Few return'd, nearly all remain in Brooklyn.
LG1891	5954	That and here my General's first battle,
LG1891	5955	No women looking on nor sunshine to bask in, it did not conclude with applause,
LG1891	5956	Nobody clapp'd hands here then.
LG1891	5957	But in darkness in mist on the ground under a chill rain,
LG1891	5958	Wearied that night we lay foil'd and sullen,
LG1891	5959	While scornfully laugh'd many an arrogant lord off against us encamp'd,
LG1891	5960	Quite within hearing, feasting, clinking wineglasses together over their victory.
LG1891	5961	So dull and damp and another day,
LG1891	5962	But the night of that, mist lifting, rain ceasing,
LG1891	5963	Silent as a ghost while they thought they were sure of him, my General retreated.
LG1891	5964	I saw him at the river-side,
LG1891	5965	Down by the ferry lit by torches, hastening the embarcation;
LG1891	5966	My General waited till the soldiers and wounded were all pass'd over,
LG1891	5967	And then, (it was just ere sunrise,) these eyes rested on him for the last time.
LG1891	5968	Every one else seem'd fill'd with gloom,
LG1891	5969	Many no doubt thought of capitulation.
LG1891	5970	But when my General pass'd me,
LG1891	5971	As he stood in his boat and look'd toward the coming sun,
LG1891	5972	I saw something different from capitulation.
LG1891	5973	Enough, the Centenarian's story ends,
LG1891	5974	The two, the past and present, have interchanged,
LG1891	5975	I myself as connecter, as chansonnier of a great future, am now speaking.
LG1891	5976	And is this the ground Washington trod?
LG1891	5977	And these waters I listlessly daily cross, are these the waters he cross'd,
LG1891	5978	As resolute in defeat as other generals in their proudest triumphs?
LG1891	5979	I must copy the story, and send it eastward and westward,
LG1891	5980	I must preserve that look as it beam'd on you rivers of Brooklyn.
LG1891	5981	See—as the annual round returns the phantoms return,
LG1891	5982	It is the 27th of August and the British have landed,
LG1891	5983	The battle begins and goes against us, behold through the smoke Washington's face,
LG1891	5984	The brigade of Virginia and Maryland have march'd forth to inter-ceptintercept the enemy,

LG1891	5988	In death, defeat, and sisters', mothers' tears.
LG1891	5989	Ah, hills and slopes of Brooklyn! I perceive you are more valuable than your owners supposed;
LG1891	5990	In the midst of you stands an encampment very old,
LG1891	5991	Stands forever the camp of that dead brigade.
LG1891	5992	A LINE in long array where they wind betwixt green islands,

LG1891	5999	I SEE before me now a traveling army halting,
LG1891	6000	Below a fertile valley spread, with barns and the orchards of summer,

LG1891	6023	COME up from the fields father, here's a letter from our Pete,

LG1891	6060	VIGIL strange I kept on the field one night;

LG1891	6086	A MARCH in the ranks hard-prest, and the road unknown,

LG1891	6214	LONG, too long America,
LG1891	6215	Traveling roads all even and peaceful you learn'd from joys and prosperity only,
