LG1891	6232	While yet incessantly asking still I adhere to my city,
LG1891	6233	Day upon day and year upon year O city, walking your streets,
LG1891	6234	Where you hold me enchain'd a certain time refusing to give me up,
LG1891	6235	Yet giving to make me glutted, enrich'd of soul, you give me forever faces;
LG1891	6236	(O I see what I sought to escape, confronting, reversing my cries,
LG1891	6237	I see my own soul trampling down what it ask'd for.)
LG1891	6238	Keep your splendid silent sun,
LG1891	6239	Keep your woods O Nature, and the quiet places by the woods,
LG1891	6240	Keep your fields of clover and timothy, and your corn-fields and orchards,
LG1891	6241	Keep the blossoming buckwheat fields where the Ninth-month bees hum;
LG1891	6242	Give me faces and streets—give me these phantoms incessant and endless along the trottoirs!
LG1891	6243	Give me interminable eyes—give me women—give me comrades and lovers by the thousand!
LG1891	6244	Let me see new ones every day—let me hold new ones by the hand every day!
LG1891	6245	Give me such shows—give me the streets of Manhattan!
LG1891	6246	Give me Broadway, with the soldiers marching—give me the sound of the trumpets and drums!
LG1891	6247	(The soldiers in companies or regiments—some starting away, flush'd and reckless,
LG1891	6248	Some, their time up, returning with thinn'd ranks, young, yet very old, worn, marching, noticing nothing;)
LG1891	6249	Give me the shores and wharves heavy-fringed with black ships!
LG1891	6250	O such for me! O an intense life, full to repletion and varied!
LG1891	6251	The life of the theatre, bar-room, huge hotel, for me!
LG1891	6252	The saloon of the steamer! the crowded excursion for me! the torchlight procession!
LG1891	6253	The dense brigade bound for the war, with high piled military wagons following;
LG1891	6254	People, endless, streaming, with strong voices, passions, pageants,
LG1891	6255	Manhattan streets with their powerful throbs, with beating drums as now,
LG1891	6256	The endless and noisy chorus, the rustle and clank of muskets, (even the sight of the wounded,)
LG1891	6257	Manhattan crowds, with their turbulent musical chorus!
LG1891	6258	Manhattan faces and eyes forever for me.
LG1891	6259	THE last sunbeam
LG1891	6260	Lightly falls from the finish'd Sabbath,
LG1891	6261	On the pavement here, and there beyond it is looking,
LG1891	6262	Down a new-made double grave.
LG1891	6263	LO, the moon ascending,
LG1891	6264	Up from the east the silvery round moon,
LG1891	6265	Beautiful over the house-tops, ghastly, phantom moon,
LG1891	6266	Immense and silent moon.
LG1891	6267	I see a sad procession,
LG1891	6268	And I hear the sound of coming full-key'd bugles,
LG1891	6269	All the channels of the city streets they're flooding,
LG1891	6270	As with voices and with tears.
LG1891	6271	I hear the great drums pounding,
LG1891	6272	And the small drums steady whirring,
LG1891	6273	And every blow of the great convulsive drums,
LG1891	6274	Strikes me through and through.
LG1891	6275	For the son is brought with the father,
LG1891	6276	(In the foremost ranks of the fierce assault they fell,
LG1891	6277	Two veterans son and father dropt together,
LG1891	6278	And the double grave awaits them.)
LG1891	6279	Now nearer blow the bugles,
LG1891	6280	And the drums strike more convulsive,
LG1891	6281	And the daylight o'er the pavement quite has faded,
LG1891	6282	And the strong dead-march enwraps me.
LG1891	6283	In the eastern sky up-buoying,
LG1891	6284	The sorrowful vast phantom moves illumin'd,
LG1891	6285	('Tis some mother's large transparent face,
LG1891	6286	In heaven brighter growing.)
LG1891	6287	O strong dead-march you please me!
LG1891	6288	O moon immense with your silvery face you soothe me!
LG1891	6289	O my soldiers twain! O my veterans passing to burial!
LG1891	6290	What I have I also give you.
LG1891	6291	The moon gives you light,
LG1891	6292	And the bugles and the drums give you music,
LG1891	6293	And my heart, O my soldiers, my veterans,
LG1891	6294	My heart gives you love.
LG1891	6295	OVER the carnage rose prophetic a voice,
LG1891	6296	Be not dishearten'd, affection shall solve the problems of freedom yet,
LG1891	6297	Those who love each other shall become invincible,
LG1891	6298	They shall yet make Columbia victorious.
LG1891	6299	Sons of the Mother of All, you shall yet be victorious,
LG1891	6300	You shall yet laugh to scorn the attacks of all the remainder of the earth.
LG1891	6301	No danger shall balk Columbia's lovers,
LG1891	6302	If need be a thousand shall sternly immolate themselves for one.
LG1891	6303	One from Massachusetts shall be a Missourian's comrade,
LG1891	6304	From Maine and from hot Carolina, and another an Oregonese,shall be friends triune,
LG1891	6305	More precious to each other than all the riches of the earth.
LG1891	6306	To Michigan, Florida perfumes shall tenderly come,
LG1891	6307	Not the perfumes of flowers, but sweeter, and wafted beyond death.
LG1891	6308	It shall be customary in the houses and streets to see manly affection,
LG1891	6309	The most dauntless and rude shall touch face to face lightly,
LG1891	6310	The dependence of Liberty shall be lovers,
LG1891	6311	The continuance of Equality shall be comrades.
LG1891	6312	These shall tie you and band you stronger than hoops of iron,
LG1891	6313	I, ecstatic, O partners! O lands! with the love of lovers tie you.
LG1891	6314	(Were you looking to be held together by lawyers?
LG1891	6315	Or by an agreement on a paper? or by arms?
LG1891	6316	Nay, nor the world, nor any living thing, will so cohere.)
LG1891	6317	I SAW old General at bay,
LG1891	6318	(Old as he was, his gray eyes yet shone out in battle like stars,)
LG1891	6319	His small force was now completely hemm'd in, in his works,
LG1891	6320	He call'd for volunteers to run the enemy's lines, a desperate emergency,
LG1891	6321	I saw a hundred and more step forth from the ranks, but two or three were selected,
LG1891	6322	I saw them receive their orders aside, they listen'd with care, the adjutant was very grave,
LG1891	6323	I saw them depart with cheerfulness, freely risking their lives.
LG1891	6324	WHILE my wife at my side lies slumbering, and the wars are over long,
LG1891	6325	And my head on the pillow rests at home, and the vacant mid-nightmidnight passes,
LG1891	6326	And through the stillness, through the dark, I hear, just hear, the breath of my infant,
LG1891	6327	There in the room as I wake from sleep this vision presses upon me;
LG1891	6328	The engagement opens there and then in fantasy unreal,
LG1891	6329	The skirmishers begin, they crawl cautiously ahead, I hear the irregular snap! snap!
LG1891	6330	I hear the sounds of the different missiles, the short t-h-t! t-h-t! of the rifle-balls,
LG1891	6331	I see the shells exploding leaving small white clouds, I hear the great shells shrieking as they pass,
LG1891	6332	The grape like the hum and whirr of wind through the trees,(tumultuous now the contest rages,)
LG1891	6333	All the scenes at the batteries rise in detail before me again,
LG1891	6334	The crashing and smoking, the pride of the men in their pieces,
LG1891	6335	The chief-gunner ranges and sights his piece and selects a fuse of the right time,
LG1891	6336	After firing I see him lean aside and look eagerly off to note the effect;
LG1891	6337	Elsewhere I hear the cry of a regiment charging, (the young colonel leads himself this time with brandish'd sword,)
LG1891	6338	I see the gaps cut by the enemy's volleys, (quickly fill'd up, no delay,)
LG1891	6339	I breathe the suffocating smoke, then the flat clouds hover low concealing all;
LG1891	6340	Now a strange lull for a few seconds, not a shot fired on either side,
LG1891	6341	Then resumed the chaos louder than ever, with eager calls and orders of officers,
LG1891	6342	While from some distant part of the field the wind wafts to my ears a shout of applause, (some special success,)
LG1891	6343	And ever the sound of the cannon far or near, (rousing even in dreams a devilish exultation and all the old mad joy in the depths of my soul,)
LG1891	6344	And ever the hastening of infantry shifting positions, batteries,cavalry, moving hither and thither,
LG1891	6345	(The falling, dying, I heed not, the wounded dripping and red I heed not, some to the rear are hobbling,)
LG1891	6346	Grime, heat, rush, aide-de-camps galloping by or on a full run,
LG1891	6347	With the patter of small arms, the warning s-s-t of the rifles, (these in my vision I hear or see,)
LG1891	6348	And bombs bursting in air, and at night the vari-color'd rockets.
LG1891	6349	WHO are you dusky woman, so ancient hardly human,
LG1891	6350	With your woolly-white and turban'd head, and bare bony feet?
LG1891	6351	Why rising by the roadside here, do you the colors greet?
LG1891	6352	('Tis while our army lines Carolina's sands and pines,
LG1891	6353	Forth from thy hovel door thou Ethiopia com'st to me,
LG1891	6354	As under doughty Sherman I march toward the sea.)
LG1891	6355	Me master years a hundred since from my parents sunder'd,
LG1891	6356	A little child, they caught me as the savage beast is caught,
LG1891	6357	Then hither me across the sea the cruel slaver brought.
LG1891	6358	No further does she say, but lingering all the day,
LG1891	6359	Her high-borne turban'd head she wags, and rolls her darkling eye,
LG1891	6360	And courtesies to the regiments, the guidons moving by.
LG1891	6361	What is it fateful woman, so blear, hardly human?
LG1891	6362	Why wag your head with turban bound, yellow, red and green?
LG1891	6363	Are the things so strange and marvelous you see or have seen?
LG1891	6364	NOT youth pertains to me,
LG1891	6365	Nor delicatesse, I cannot beguile the time with talk,
LG1891	6366	Awkward in the parlor, neither a dancer nor elegant,
LG1891	6367	In the learn'd coterie sitting constrain'd and still, for learning inures not to me,
LG1891	6368	Beauty, knowledge, inure not to me—yet there are two or three things inure to me,
LG1891	6369	I have nourish'd the wounded and sooth'd many a dying soldier,
LG1891	6370	And at intervals waiting or in the midst of camp,
LG1891	6371	Composed these songs.
LG1891	6372	RACE of veterans—race of victors!
LG1891	6373	Race of the soil, ready for conflict—race of the conquering march!
LG1891	6374	(No more credulity's race, abiding-temper'd race,)
LG1891	6375	Race henceforth owning no law but the law of itself,
LG1891	6376	Race of passion and the storm.
LG1891	6377	WORLD take good notice, silver stars fading,
LG1891	6378	Milky hue ript, weft of white detaching,
LG1891	6379	Coals thirty-eight, baleful and burning,
LG1891	6380	Scarlet, significant, hands off warning,
LG1891	6381	Now and henceforth flaunt from these shores.
LG1891	6382	O TAN-FACED prairie-boy,
LG1891	6383	Before you came to camp came many a welcome gift,
LG1891	6384	Praises and presents came and nourishing food, till at last among the recruits,
LG1891	6385	You came, taciturn, with nothing to give—we but look'd on each other,
LG1891	6386	When lo! more than all the gifts of the world you gave me.
LG1891	6387	LOOK down fair moon and bathe this scene,
LG1891	6388	Pour softly down night's nimbus floods on faces ghastly, swollen,purple,
LG1891	6389	On the dead on their backs with arms toss'd wide,
LG1891	6390	Pour down your unstinted nimbus sacred moon.
LG1891	6391	WORD over all, beautiful as the sky,
LG1891	6392	Beautiful that war and all its deeds of carnage must in time be utterly lost,
LG1891	6393	That the hands of the sisters Death and Night incessantly softly wash again, and ever again, this soil'd world;
LG1891	6394	For my enemy is dead, a man divine as myself is dead,
LG1891	6395	I look where he lies white-faced and still in the coffin—I draw near,
LG1891	6396	Bend down and touch lightly with my lips the white face in the coffin.
LG1891	6397	HOW solemn as one by one,
LG1891	6398	As the ranks returning worn and sweaty, as the men file by where I stand,
LG1891	6399	As the faces the masks appear, as I glance at the faces studying the masks,
LG1891	6400	(As I glance upward out of this page studying you, dear friend,whoever you are,)
LG1891	6401	How solemn the thought of my whispering soul to each in the ranks, and to you,
LG1891	6402	I see behind each mask that wonder a kindred soul,
LG1891	6403	O the bullet could never kill what you really are, dear friend,
LG1891	6404	Nor the bayonet stab what you really are;
LG1891	6405	The soul! yourself I see, great as any, good as the best,
LG1891	6406	Waiting secure and content, which the bullet could never kill,
LG1891	6407	Nor the bayonet stab O friend.
LG1891	6408	AS I lay with my head in your lap camerado,
LG1891	6409	The confession I made I resume, what I said to you and the open air I resume,
LG1891	6410	I know I am restless and make others so,
LG1891	6411	I know my words are weapons full of danger, full of death,
LG1891	6412	For I confront peace, security, and all the settled laws, to unsettle them,
LG1891	6413	I am more resolute because all have denied me than I could ever have been had all accepted me,
LG1891	6414	I heed not and have never heeded either experience, cautions,majorities, nor ridicule,
LG1891	6415	And the threat of what is call'd hell is little or nothing to me,
LG1891	6416	And the lure of what is call'd heaven is little or nothing to me;
LG1891	6417	Dear camerado! I confess I have urged you onward with me, and still urge you, without the least idea what is our destination,
LG1891	6418	Or whether we shall be victorious, or utterly quell'd and defeated.
LG1891	6419	DELICATE cluster! flag of teeming life!
LG1891	6420	Covering all my lands—all my seashores lining!
LG1891	6421	Flag of death! (how I watch'd you through the smoke of battle pressing!
LG1891	6422	How I heard you flap and rustle, cloth defiant!)
LG1891	6423	Flag cerulean—sunny flag, with the orbs of night dappled!
LG1891	6424	Ah my silvery beauty—ah my woolly white and crimson!
LG1891	6425	Ah to sing the song of you, my matron mighty!
LG1891	6426	My sacred one, my mother.
LG1891	6427	DID you ask dulcet rhymes from me?
LG1891	6428	Did you seek the civilian's peaceful and languishing rhymes?
LG1891	6429	Did you find what I sang erewhile so hard to follow?
LG1891	6430	Why I was not singing erewhile for you to follow, to understand—nor am I now;
LG1891	6431	(I have been born of the same as the war was born,
LG1891	6432	The drum-corps' rattle is ever to me sweet music, I love well the martial dirge,
LG1891	6433	With slow wail and convulsive throb leading the officer's funeral;)
LG1891	6434	What to such as you anyhow such a poet as I? therefore leave my works,
LG1891	6435	And go lull yourself with what you can understand, and with piano-tunespiano-tunes,
LG1891	6436	For I lull nobody, and you will never understand me.
LG1891	6437	LO, Victress on the peaks,
LG1891	6438	Where thou with mighty brow regarding the world,
LG1891	6439	(The world O Libertad, that vainly conspired against thee,)
LG1891	6440	Out of its countless beleaguering toils, after thwarting them all,
LG1891	6441	Dominant, with the dazzling sun around thee,
LG1891	6442	Flauntest now unharm'd in immortal soundness and bloom—lo, in these hours supreme,
LG1891	6443	No poem proud, I chanting bring to thee, nor mastery's rapturous verse,
LG1891	6444	But a cluster containing night's darkness and blood-dripping wounds,
LG1891	6445	And psalms of the dead.
LG1891	6446	SPIRIT whose work is done—spirit of dreadful hours!
LG1891	6447	Ere departing fade from my eyes your forests of bayonets;
LG1891	6448	Spirit of gloomiest fears and doubts, (yet onward ever unfaltering pressing,)
LG1891	6449	Spirit of many a solemn day and many a savage scene—electric spirit,
LG1891	6450	That with muttering voice through the war now closed, like a tire-lesstireless phantom flitted,
LG1891	6451	Rousing the land with breath of flame, while you beat and beat the drum,
LG1891	6452	Now as the sound of the drum, hollow and harsh to the last,reverberates round me,
LG1891	6453	As your ranks, your immortal ranks, return, return from the battles,
LG1891	6454	As the muskets of the young men yet lean over their shoulders,
LG1891	6455	As I look on the bayonets bristling over their shoulders,
LG1891	6456	As those slanted bayonets, whole forests of them appearing in the distance, approach and pass on, returning homeward,
LG1891	6457	Moving with steady motion, swaying to and fro to the right and left,
LG1891	6458	Evenly lightly rising and falling while the steps keep time;
LG1891	6459	Spirit of hours I knew, all hectic red one day, but pale as death next day,
LG1891	6460	Touch my mouth ere you depart, press my lips close,
LG1891	6461	Leave me your pulses of rage—bequeath them to me—fill me with currents convulsive,
LG1891	6462	Let them scorch and blister out of my chants when you are gone,
LG1891	6463	Let them identify you to the future in these songs.
LG1891	6464	ADIEU O soldier,
LG1891	6465	You of the rude campaigning, (which we shared,)
LG1891	6466	The rapid march, the life of the camp,
LG1891	6467	The hot contention of opposing fronts, the long manoeuvre,
LG1891	6468	Red battles with their slaughter, the stimulus, the strong terrific game,
LG1891	6469	Spell of all brave and manly hearts, the trains of time through you and like of you all fill'd,
LG1891	6470	With war and war's expression.
LG1891	6471	Adieu dear comrade,
LG1891	6472	Your mission is fulfill'd—but I, more warlike,
LG1891	6473	Myself and this contentious soul of mine,
LG1891	6474	Still on our own campaigning bound,
LG1891	6475	Through untried roads with ambushes opponents lined,
LG1891	6476	Through many a sharp defeat and many a crisis, often baffled,
LG1891	6477	Here marching, ever marching on, a war fight out—aye here,
LG1891	6478	To fiercer, weightier battles give expression.
LG1891	6479	TURN O Libertad, for the war is over,
LG1891	6480	From it and all henceforth expanding, doubting no more, resolute,sweeping the world,
LG1891	6481	Turn from lands retrospective recording proofs of the past,
LG1891	6482	From the singers that sing the trailing glories of the past,
LG1891	6483	From the chants of the feudal world, the triumphs of kings, slavery,caste,
LG1891	6484	Turn to the world, the triumphs reserv'd and to come—give up that backward world,
LG1891	6485	Leave to the singers of hitherto, give them the trailing past,
LG1891	6486	But what remains remains for singers for you—wars to come are for you,
LG1891	6487	(Lo, how the wars of the past have duly inured to you, and the wars of the present also inure;)
LG1891	6488	Then turn, and be not alarm'd O Libertad—turn your undying face,
LG1891	6489	To where the future, greater than all the past,
LG1891	6490	Is swiftly, surely preparing for you.
LG1891	6491	TO the leaven'd soil they trod calling I sing for the last,
LG1891	6492	(Forth from my tent emerging for good, loosing, untying the tent-ropestent-ropes,)
LG1891	6493	In the freshness the forenoon air, in the far-stretching circuits and vistas again to peace restored,
LG1891	6494	To the fiery fields emanative and the endless vistas beyond, to the South and the North,
LG1891	6495	To the leaven'd soil of the general Western world to attest my songs,
LG1891	6496	To the Alleghanian hills and the tireless Mississippi,
LG1891	6497	To the rocks I calling sing, and all the trees in the woods,
LG1891	6498	To the plains of the poems of heroes, to the prairies spreading wide,
LG1891	6499	To the far-off sea and the unseen winds, and the sane impalpable air;
LG1891	6500	And responding they answer all, (but not in words,)
LG1891	6501	The average earth, the witness of war and peace, acknowledges mutely,
LG1891	6502	The prairie draws me close, as the father to bosom broad the son,
LG1891	6503	The Northern ice and rain that began me nourish me to the end,
LG1891	6504	But the hot sun of the South is to fully ripen my songs.
LG1891	6505	WHEN lilacs last in the dooryard bloom'd,
LG1891	6506	And the great star early droop'd in the western sky in the night,
LG1891	6507	I mourn'd, and yet shall mourn with ever-returning spring.
LG1891	6508	Ever-returning spring, trinity sure to me you bring,
LG1891	6509	Lilac blooming perennial and drooping star in the west,
LG1891	6510	And thought of him I love.
LG1891	6511	O powerful western fallen star!
LG1891	6512	O shades of night—O moody, tearful night!
LG1891	6513	O great star disappear'd—O the black murk that hides the star!
LG1891	6514	O cruel hands that hold me powerless—O helpless soul of me!
LG1891	6515	O harsh surrounding cloud that will not free my soul.
LG1891	6516	In the dooryard fronting an old farm-house near the white-wash'd palings,
LG1891	6517	Stands the lilac-bush tall-growing with heart-shaped leaves of rich green,
LG1891	6518	With many a pointed blossom rising delicate, with the perfume strong I love,
LG1891	6519	With every leaf a miracle—and from this bush in the dooryard,
LG1891	6520	With delicate-color'd blossoms and heart-shaped leaves of rich green,
LG1891	6521	A sprig with its flower I break.
LG1891	6522	In the swamp in secluded recesses,
LG1891	6523	A shy and hidden bird is warbling a song.
LG1891	6524	Solitary the thrush,
LG1891	6525	The hermit withdrawn to himself, avoiding the settlements,
LG1891	6526	Sings by himself a song.
LG1891	6527	Song of the bleeding throat,
LG1891	6528	Death's outlet song of life, (for well dear brother I know,
LG1891	6529	If thou wast not granted to sing thou would'st surely die.)
LG1891	6530	Over the breast of the spring, the land, amid cities,
LG1891	6531	Amid lanes and through old woods, where lately the violets peep'd from the ground, spotting the gray debris,
LG1891	6532	Amid the grass in the fields each side of the lanes, passing the endless grass,
LG1891	6533	Passing the yellow-spear'd wheat, every grain from its shroud in the dark-brown fields uprisen,
LG1891	6534	Passing the apple-tree blows of white and pink in the orchards,
LG1891	6535	Carrying a corpse to where it shall rest in the grave,
LG1891	6536	Night and day journeys a coffin.
LG1891	6537	Coffin that passes through lanes and streets,
LG1891	6538	Through day and night with the great cloud darkening the land,
LG1891	6539	With the pomp of the inloop'd flags with the cities draped in black,
LG1891	6540	With the show of the States themselves as of crape-veil'd women standing,
LG1891	6541	With processions long and winding and the flambeaus of the night,
LG1891	6542	With the countless torches lit, with the silent sea of faces and the unbared heads,
LG1891	6543	With the waiting depot, the arriving coffin, and the sombre faces,
LG1891	6544	With dirges through the night, with the thousand voices rising strong and solemn,
LG1891	6545	With all the mournful voices of the dirges pour'd around the coffin,
LG1891	6546	The dim-lit churches and the shuddering organs—where amid these you journey,
LG1891	6547	With the tolling tolling bells' perpetual clang,
LG1891	6548	Here, coffin that slowly passes,
LG1891	6549	I give you my sprig of lilac.
LG1891	6550	(Nor for you, for one alone,
LG1891	6551	Blossoms and branches green to coffins all I bring,
LG1891	6552	For fresh as the morning, thus would I chant a song for you O sane and sacred death.
LG1891	6553	All over bouquets of roses,
LG1891	6554	O death, I cover you over with roses and early lilies,
LG1891	6555	But mostly and now the lilac that blooms the first,
LG1891	6556	Copious I break, I break the sprigs from the bushes,
LG1891	6557	With loaded arms I come, pouring for you,
LG1891	6558	For you and the coffins all of you O death.)
LG1891	6559	O western orb sailing the heaven,
LG1891	6560	Now I know what you must have meant as a month since I walk'd,
LG1891	6561	As I walk'd in silence the transparent shadowy night,
LG1891	6562	As I saw you had something to tell as you bent to me night after night,
LG1891	6563	As you droop'd from the sky low down as if to my side, (while the other stars all look'd on,)
LG1891	6564	As we wander'd together the solemn night, (for something I know not what kept me from sleep,)
LG1891	6565	As the night advanced, and I saw on the rim of the west how full you were of woe,
LG1891	6566	As I stood on the rising ground in the breeze in the cool trans-parenttransparent night,
LG1891	6567	As I watch'd where you pass'd and was lost in the netherward black of the night,
LG1891	6568	As my soul in its trouble dissatisfied sank, as where you sad orb,
LG1891	6569	Concluded, dropt in the night, and was gone.
LG1891	6570	Sing on there in the swamp,
LG1891	6571	O singer bashful and tender, I hear your notes, I hear your call,
LG1891	6572	I hear, I come presently, I understand you,
LG1891	6573	But a moment I linger, for the lustrous star has detain'd me,
LG1891	6574	The star my departing comrade holds and detains me.
LG1891	6575	O how shall I warble myself for the dead one there I loved?
LG1891	6576	And how shall I deck my song for the large sweet soul that has gone?
LG1891	6577	And what shall my perfume be for the grave of him I love?
LG1891	6578	Sea-winds blown from east and west,
LG1891	6579	Blown from the Eastern sea and blown from the Western sea, till there on the prairies meeting,
LG1891	6580	These and with these and the breath of my chant,
LG1891	6581	I'll perfume the grave of him I love.
LG1891	6582	O what shall I hang on the chamber walls?
LG1891	6583	And what shall the pictures be that I hang on the walls,
LG1891	6584	To adorn the burial-house of him I love?
LG1891	6585	Pictures of growing spring and farms and homes,

LG1891	6601	The coming eve delicious, the welcome night and the stars,
LG1891	6602	Over my cities shining all, enveloping man and land.
LG1891	6603	Sing on, sing on you gray-brown bird,

LG1891	6607	Loud human song, with voice of uttermost woe.
LG1891	6608	O liquid and free and tender!
LG1891	6609	O wild and loose to my soul—O wondrous singer!
LG1891	6610	You only I hear—yet the star holds me, (but will soon depart,)
LG1891	6611	Yet the lilac with mastering odor holds me.
LG1891	6612	Now while I sat in the day and look'd forth,
LG1891	6613	In the close of the day with its light and the fields of spring, and the farmers preparing their crops,

LG1891	6623	And I knew death, its thought, and the sacred knowledge of death.
LG1891	6624	Then with the knowledge of death as walking one side of me,
LG1891	6625	And the thought of death close-walking the other side of me,

LG1891	6628	Down to the shores of the water, the path by the swamp in the dimness,

LG1891	6630	And the singer so shy to the rest receiv'd me,
LG1891	6631	The gray-brown bird I know receiv'd us comrades three,
LG1891	6632	And he sang the carol of death, and a verse for him I love.
LG1891	6633	From deep secluded recesses,
LG1891	6634	From the fragrant cedars and the ghostly pines so still,
LG1891	6635	Came the carol of the bird.
LG1891	6636	And the charm of the carol rapt me,
LG1891	6637	As I held as if by their hands my comrades in the night,
LG1891	6638	And the voice of my spirit tallied the song of the bird.
LG1891	6639	Come lovely and soothing death,
LG1891	6640	Undulate round the world, serenely arriving, arriving,
LG1891	6641	In the day, in the night, to all, to each,
LG1891	6642	Sooner or later delicate death.
LG1891	6643	Prais'd be the fathomless universe,
LG1891	6644	For life and joy, and for objects and knowledge curious,

LG1891	6647	Dark mother always gliding near with soft feet,

LG1891	6650	I bring thee a song that when thou must indeed come, come unfal-teringlyunfalteringly.

LG1891	6667	To the tally of my soul,

LG1891	6687	And the wife and the child and the musing comrade suffer'd,

LG1891	6711	O CAPTAIN! my Captain! our fearful trip is done,

LG1891	6736	And soldiers let us drape our war-worn weapons,

LG1891	6747	THIS dust was once the man,
LG1891	6748	Gentle, plain, just and resolute, under whose cautious hand,
LG1891	6749	Against the foulest crime in history known in any land or age,
LG1891	6750	Was saved the Union of these States.
LG1891	6751	BY blue Ontario's shore,
LG1891	6752	As I mused of these warlike days and of peace return'd, and the dead that return no more,
LG1891	6753	A Phantom gigantic superb, with stern visage accosted me,
LG1891	6754	Chant me the poem, it said, that comes from the soul of America, chant me the carol of victory,
LG1891	6755	And strike up the marches of Libertad, marches more powerful yet,
LG1891	6756	And sing me before you go the song of the throes of Democracy.
LG1891	6757	(Democracy, the destin'd conqueror, yet treacherous lip-smiles everywhere,
LG1891	6758	And death and infidelity at every step.)
LG1891	6759	A Nation announcing itself,
LG1891	6760	I myself make the only growth by which I can be appreciated,
LG1891	6761	I reject none, accept all, then reproduce all in my own forms.
LG1891	6762	A breed whose proof is in time and deeds,
LG1891	6763	What we are we are, nativity is answer enough to objections,
LG1891	6764	We wield ourselves as a weapon is wielded,
LG1891	6765	We are powerful and tremendous in ourselves,
LG1891	6766	We are executive in ourselves, we are sufficient in the variety of ourselves,
LG1891	6767	We are the most beautiful to ourselves and in ourselves,
LG1891	6768	We stand self-pois'd in the middle, branching thence over the world,
LG1891	6769	From Missouri, Nebraska, or Kansas, laughing attacks to scorn.
LG1891	6770	Nothing is sinful to us outside of ourselves,
LG1891	6771	Whatever appears, whatever does not appear, we are beautiful or sinful in ourselves only.
LG1891	6772	(O Mother—O Sisters dear!
LG1891	6773	If we are lost, no victor else has destroy'd us,
LG1891	6774	It is by ourselves we go down to eternal night.)
LG1891	6775	Have you thought there could be but a single supreme?
LG1891	6776	There can be any number of supremes—one does not counter-vailcountervail another any more than one eyesight countervails another, or one life countervails another.
LG1891	6777	All is eligible to all,
LG1891	6778	All is for individuals, all is for you,
LG1891	6779	No condition is prohibited, not God's or any.
LG1891	6780	All comes by the body, only health puts you rapport with the universe.

LG1891	6782	Piety and conformity to them that like,

LG1891	6789	(With pangs and cries as thine own O bearer of many children,

LG1891	6791	O lands, would you be freer than all that has ever been before?
LG1891	6792	If you would be freer than all that has been before, come listen to me.
LG1891	6793	Fear grace, elegance, civilization, delicatesse,

LG1891	6801	America, curious toward foreign characters, stands by its own at all hazards,
LG1891	6802	Stands removed, spacious, composite, sound, initiates the true use of precedents,
LG1891	6803	Does not repel them or the past or what they have produced under their forms,

LG1891	6807	And that he shall be fittest for his days.
LG1891	6808	Any period one nation must lead,
LG1891	6809	One land must be the promise and reliance of the future.
LG1891	6810	These States are the amplest poem,
LG1891	6811	Here is not merely a nation but a teeming Nation of nations,
LG1891	6812	Here the doings of men correspond with the broadcast doings of the day and night,

LG1891	6817	Of them standing among them, one lifts to the light a west-bred face,

LG1891	6888	Not in him but off from him things are grotesque, eccentric, fail of their full returns,
LG1891	6889	Nothing out of its place is good, nothing in its place is bad,
LG1891	6890	He bestows on every object or quality its fit proportion, neither more nor less,

LG1891	6904	For the great Idea, the idea of perfect and free individuals,
LG1891	6905	For that, the bard walks in advance, leader of leaders,
LG1891	6906	The attitude of him cheers up slaves and horrifies foreign despots.

LG1891	6916	(Angry cloth I saw there leaping!
LG1891	6917	I stand again in leaden rain your flapping folds saluting,
LG1891	6918	I sing you over all, flying beckoning through the fight—O the hard-contested fight!
LG1891	6919	The cannons ope their rosy-flashing muzzles—the hurtled balls scream,
LG1891	6920	The battle-front forms amid the smoke—the volleys pour incessant from the line,
LG1891	6921	Hark, the ringing word Charge!—now the tussle and the furious maddening yells,
LG1891	6922	Now the corpses tumble curl'd upon the ground,
LG1891	6923	Cold, cold in death, for precious life of you,
LG1891	6924	Angry cloth I saw there leaping.)
LG1891	6925	Are you he who would assume a place to teach or be a poet here in the States?
LG1891	6926	The place is august, the terms obdurate.
LG1891	6927	Who would assume to teach here may well prepare himself body and mind,
LG1891	6928	He may well survey, ponder, arm, fortify, harden, make lithe him-selfhimself,
LG1891	6929	He shall surely be question'd beforehand by me with many and stern questions.
LG1891	6930	Who are you indeed who would talk or sing to America?
LG1891	6931	Have you studied out the land, its idioms and men?
LG1891	6932	Have you learn'd the physiology, phrenology, politics, geography,pride, freedom, friendship of the land? its substratums and objects?
LG1891	6933	Have you consider'd the organic compact of the first day of the first year of Independence, sign'd by the Commissioners,ratified by the States, and read by Washington at the head of the army?
LG1891	6934	Have you possess'd yourself of the Federal Constitution?
LG1891	6935	Do you see who have left all feudal processes and poems behind them, and assumed the poems and processes of Democracy?
LG1891	6936	Are you faithful to things? do you teach what the land and sea,the bodies of men, womanhood, amativeness, heroic angers,teach?
LG1891	6937	Have you sped through fleeting customs, popularities?
LG1891	6938	Can you hold your hand against all seductions, follies, whirls, fierce contentions? are you very strong? are you really of the whole People?
LG1891	6939	Are you not of some coterie? some school or mere religion?
LG1891	6940	Are you done with reviews and criticisms of life? animating now to life itself?
LG1891	6941	Have you vivified yourself from the maternity of these States?
LG1891	6942	Have you too the old ever-fresh forbearance and impartiality?
LG1891	6943	Do you hold the like love for those hardening to maturity? for the last-born? little and big? and for the errant?
LG1891	6944	What is this you bring my America?
LG1891	6945	Is it uniform with my country?
LG1891	6946	Is it not something that has been better told or done before?
LG1891	6947	Have you not imported this or the spirit of it in some ship?
LG1891	6948	Is it not a mere tale? a rhyme? a prettiness?—is the good old cause in it?
LG1891	6949	Has it not dangled long at the heels of the poets, politicians,literats, of enemies' lands?
LG1891	6950	Does it not assume that what is notoriously gone is still here?
LG1891	6951	Does it answer universal needs? will it improve manners?
LG1891	6952	Does it sound with trumpet-voice the proud victory of the Union in that secession war?
LG1891	6953	Can your performance face the open fields and the seaside?
LG1891	6954	Will it absorb into me as I absorb food, air, to appear again in my strength, gait, face?
LG1891	6955	Have real employments contributed to it? original makers, not mere amanuenses?
LG1891	6956	Does it meet modern discoveries, calibres, facts, face to face?
LG1891	6957	What does it mean to American persons, progresses, cities? Chi-cagoChicago, KanadaCanada, Arkansas?
LG1891	6958	Does it see behind the apparent custodians the real custodians standing, menacing, silent, the mechanics, Manhattanese,Western men, Southerners, significant alike in their apathy,and in the promptness of their love?
LG1891	6959	Does it see what finally befalls, and has always finally befallen, each temporizer, patcher, outsider, partialist, alarmist, infidel,who has ever ask'd any thing of America?
LG1891	6960	What mocking and scornful negligence?
LG1891	6961	The track strew'd with the dust of skeletons,
LG1891	6962	By the roadside others disdainfully toss'd.
LG1891	6963	Rhymes and rhymers pass away, poems distill'd from poems pass away,
LG1891	6964	The swarms of reflectors and the polite pass, and leave ashes,
LG1891	6965	Admirers, importers, obedient persons, make but the soil of litera-tureliterature,
LG1891	6966	America justifies itself, give it time, no disguise can deceive it or conceal from it, it is impassive enough,
LG1891	6967	Only toward the likes of itself will it advance to meet them,
LG1891	6968	If its poets appear it will in due time advance to meet them,there is no fear of mistake,
LG1891	6969	(The proof of a poet shall be sternly deferr'd till his country absorbs him as affectionately as he has absorb'd it.)
LG1891	6970	He masters whose spirit masters, he tastes sweetest who results sweetest in the long run,
LG1891	6971	The blood of the brawn beloved of time is unconstraint;
LG1891	6972	In the need of songs, philosophy, an appropriate native grand-operagrand-opera, shipcraft, any craft,
LG1891	6973	He or she is greatest who contributes the greatest original prac-ticalpractical example.
LG1891	6974	Already a nonchalant breed, silently emerging, appears on the streets,
LG1891	6975	People's lips salute only doers, lovers, satisfiers, positive knowers,
LG1891	6976	There will shortly be no more priests, I say their work is done,
LG1891	6977	Death is without emergencies here, but life is perpetual emer-genciesemergencies here,
LG1891	6978	Are your body, days, manners, superb? after death you shall be superb,
LG1891	6979	Justice, health, self-esteem, clear the way with irresistible power;
LG1891	6980	How dare you place any thing before a man?
LG1891	6981	Fall behind me States!
LG1891	6982	A man before all—myself, typical, before all.
LG1891	6983	Give me the pay I have served for,
LG1891	6984	Give me to sing the songs of the great Idea, take all the rest,
LG1891	6985	I have loved the earth, sun, animals, I have despised riches,
LG1891	6986	I have given alms to every one that ask'd, stood up for the stupid and crazy, devoted my income and labor to others,
LG1891	6987	Hated tyrants, argued not concerning God, had patience and indulgence toward the people, taken off my hat to nothing known or unknown,
LG1891	6988	Gone freely with powerful uneducated persons and with the young,and with the mothers of families,
LG1891	6989	Read these leaves to myself in the open air, tried them by trees,stars, rivers,
LG1891	6990	Dismiss'd whatever insulted my own soul or defiled my body,
LG1891	6991	Claim'd nothing to myself which I have not carefully claim'd for others on the same terms,
LG1891	6992	Sped to the camps, and comrades found and accepted from every State,
LG1891	6993	(Upon this breast has many a dying soldier lean'd to breathe his last,
LG1891	6994	This arm, this hand, this voice, have nourish'd, rais'd, restored,
LG1891	6995	To life recalling many a prostrate form;)
LG1891	6996	I am willing to wait to be understood by the growth of the taste of myself,
LG1891	6997	Rejecting none, permitting all.
LG1891	6998	(Say O Mother, have I not to your thought been faithful?
LG1891	6999	Have I not through life kept you and yours before me?)
LG1891	7000	I swear I begin to see the meaning of these things,
LG1891	7001	It is not the earth, it is not America who is so great,
LG1891	7002	It is I who am great or to be great, it is You up there, or any one,
LG1891	7003	It is to walk rapidly through civilizations, governments, theories,
LG1891	7004	Through poems, pageants, shows, to form individuals.
LG1891	7005	Underneath all, individuals,
LG1891	7006	I swear nothing is good to me now that ignores individuals,
LG1891	7007	The American compact is altogether with individuals,
LG1891	7008	The only government is that which makes minute of individuals,
LG1891	7009	The whole theory of the universe is directed unerringly to one single individual—namely to You.
LG1891	7010	(Mother! with subtle sense severe, with the naked sword in your hand,
LG1891	7011	I saw you at last refuse to treat but directly with individuals.)
LG1891	7012	Underneath all, Nativity,
LG1891	7013	I swear I will stand by my own nativity, pious or impious so be it;
LG1891	7014	I swear I am charm'd with nothing except nativity,
LG1891	7015	Men, women, cities, nations, are only beautiful from nativity.
LG1891	7016	Underneath all is the Expression of love for men and women,
LG1891	7017	(I swear I have seen enough of mean and impotent modes of expressing love for men and women,
LG1891	7018	After this day I take my own modes of expressing love for men and women.)
LG1891	7019	I swear I will have each quality of my race in myself,
LG1891	7020	(Talk as you like, he only suits these States whose manners favor the audacity and sublime turbulence of the States.)
LG1891	7021	Underneath the lessons of things, spirits, Nature, governments,ownerships, I swear I perceive other lessons,
LG1891	7022	Underneath all to me is myself, to you yourself, (the same monoto-nousmonotonous old song.)
LG1891	7023	O I see flashing that this America is only you and me,
LG1891	7024	Its power, weapons, testimony, are you and me,
LG1891	7025	Its crimes, lies, thefts, defections, are you and me,
LG1891	7026	Its Congress is you and me, the officers, capitols, armies, ships, are you and me,
LG1891	7027	Its endless gestations of new States are you and me,
LG1891	7028	The war, (that war so bloody and grim, the war I will henceforth forget), was you and me,
LG1891	7029	Natural and artificial are you and me,
LG1891	7030	Freedom, language, poems, employments, are you and me,
LG1891	7031	Past, present, future, are you and me.
LG1891	7032	I dare not shirk any part of myself,
LG1891	7033	Not any part of America good or bad,
LG1891	7034	Not to build for that which builds for mankind,
LG1891	7035	Not to balance ranks, complexions, creeds, and the sexes,
LG1891	7036	Not to justify science nor the march of equality,
LG1891	7037	Nor to feed the arrogant blood of the brawn belov'd of time.
LG1891	7038	I am for those that have never been master'd,
LG1891	7039	For men and women whose tempers have never been master'd,
LG1891	7040	For those whom laws, theories, conventions, can never master.
LG1891	7041	I am for those who walk abreast with the whole earth,
LG1891	7042	Who inaugurate one to inaugurate all.
LG1891	7043	I will not be outfaced by irrational things,
LG1891	7044	I will penetrate what it is in them that is sarcastic upon me,
LG1891	7045	I will make cities and civilizations defer to me,
LG1891	7046	This is what I have learnt from America—it is the amount, and it I teach again.
LG1891	7047	(Democracy, while weapons were everywhere aim'd at your breast,
LG1891	7048	I saw you serenely give birth to immortal children, saw in dreams your dilating form,
LG1891	7049	Saw you with spreading mantle covering the world.)
LG1891	7050	I will confront these shows of the day and night,
LG1891	7051	I will know if I am to be less than they,
LG1891	7052	I will see if I am not as majestic as they,
LG1891	7053	I will see if I am not as subtle and real as they,
LG1891	7054	I will see if I am to be less generous than they,
LG1891	7055	I will see if I have no meaning, while the houses and ships have meaning,
LG1891	7056	I will see if the fishes and birds are to be enough for themselves,and I am not to be enough for myself.
LG1891	7057	I match my spirit against yours you orbs, growths, mountains,brutes,
LG1891	7058	Copious as you are I absorb you all in myself, and become the master myself,
LG1891	7059	America isolated yet embodying all, what is it finally except myself?
LG1891	7060	These States, what are they except myself?
LG1891	7061	I know now why the earth is gross, tantalizing, wicked, it is for my sake,
LG1891	7062	I take you specially to be mine, you terrible, rude forms.
LG1891	7063	(Mother, bend down, bend close to me your face,
LG1891	7064	I know not what these plots and wars and deferments are for,
LG1891	7065	I know not fruition's success, but I know that through war and crime your work goes on, and must yet go on.)
LG1891	7066	Thus by blue Ontario's shore,
LG1891	7067	While the winds fann'd me and the waves came trooping toward me,
LG1891	7068	I thrill'd with the power's pulsations, and the charm of my theme was upon me,
LG1891	7069	Till the tissues that held me parted their ties upon me.
LG1891	7070	And I saw the free souls of poets,
LG1891	7071	The loftiest bards of past ages strode before me,
LG1891	7072	Strange large men, long unwaked, undisclosed, were disclosed to me.
LG1891	7073	O my rapt verse, my call, mock me not!
LG1891	7074	Not for the bards of the past, not to invoke them have I launch'd you forth,
LG1891	7075	Not to call even those lofty bards here by Ontario's shores,
LG1891	7076	Have I sung so capricious and loud my savage song.
LG1891	7077	Bards for my own land only I invoke,
LG1891	7078	(For the war the war is over, the field is clear'd,)
LG1891	7079	Till they strike up marches henceforth triumphant and onward,
LG1891	7080	To cheer O Mother your boundless expectant soul.
LG1891	7081	Bards of the great Idea! bards of the peaceful inventions! (for the war, the war is over!)
LG1891	7082	Yet bards of latent armies, a million soldiers waiting ever-ready,
LG1891	7083	Bards with songs as from burning coals or the lightning's fork'd stripes!
LG1891	7084	Ample Ohio's, Kanada'sCanada's bards—bards of California! inland bards—bards of the war!
LG1891	7085	You by my charm I invoke.
LG1891	7086	LET that which stood in front go behind,
LG1891	7087	Let that which was behind advance to the front,
LG1891	7088	Let bigots, fools, unclean persons, offer new propositions,
LG1891	7089	Let the old propositions be postponed,
LG1891	7090	Let a man seek pleasure everywhere except in himself,
LG1891	7091	Let a woman seek happiness everywhere except in herself.
LG1891	7092	AS consequent from store of summer rains,
LG1891	7093	Or wayward rivulets in autumn flowing,
LG1891	7094	Or many a herb-lined brook's reticulations,
LG1891	7095	Or subterranean sea-rills making for the sea,
LG1891	7096	Songs of continued years I sing.
LG1891	7097	Life's ever-modern rapids first, (soon, soon to blend,

LG1891	7099	Some threading Ohio's farm-fields or the woods,

LG1891	7104	In you whoe'er you are my book perusing,

LG1891	7106	All, all toward the mystic ocean tending.
LG1891	7107	Currents for starting a continent new,
LG1891	7108	Overtures sent to the solid out of the liquid,

LG1891	7115	O little shells, so curious-convolute, so limpid-cold and voiceless,

LG1891	7120	Your tidings old, yet ever new and untranslatable,
LG1891	7121	Infinitesimals out of my life, and many a life,
LG1891	7122	(For not my life and years alone I give—all, all I give,)
LG1891	7123	These waifs from the deep, cast high and dry,
LG1891	7124	Wash'd on America's shores?
LG1891	7125	FOR the lands and for these passionate days and for myself,
LG1891	7126	Now I awhile retire to thee O soil of autumn fields,
LG1891	7127	Reclining on thy breast, giving myself to thee,
LG1891	7128	Answering the pulses of thy sane and equable heart,
LG1891	7129	Tuning a verse for thee.
LG1891	7130	O earth that hast no voice, confide to me a voice,
LG1891	7131	O harvest of my lands—O boundless summer growths,
LG1891	7132	O lavish brown parturient earth—O infinite teeming womb,
LG1891	7133	A song to narrate thee.
LG1891	7134	Ever upon this stage,
LG1891	7135	Is acted God's calm annual drama,
LG1891	7136	Gorgeous processions, songs of birds,
LG1891	7137	Sunrise that fullest feeds and freshens most the soul,
LG1891	7138	The heaving sea, the waves upon the shore, the musical, strong waves,
LG1891	7139	The woods, the stalwart trees, the slender, tapering trees,
LG1891	7140	The liliput countless armies of the grass,
LG1891	7141	The heat, the showers, the measureless pasturages,

LG1891	7145	The moving flocks and herds, the plains and emerald meadows,
LG1891	7146	The shows of all the varied lands and all the growths and products.
LG1891	7147	Fecund America—to-day,
LG1891	7148	Thou art all over set in births and joys!
LG1891	7149	Thou groan'st with riches, thy wealth clothes thee as a swathing-garmentswathing-garment,
LG1891	7150	Thou laughest loud with ache of great possessions,
LG1891	7151	A myriad-twining life like interlacing vines binds all thy vast demesne,
LG1891	7152	As some huge ship freighted to water's edge thou ridest into port,
LG1891	7153	As rain falls from the heaven and vapors rise from earth, so have the precious values fallen upon thee and risen out of thee;
LG1891	7154	Thou envy of the globe! thou miracle!
LG1891	7155	Thou, bathed, choked, swimming in plenty,
LG1891	7156	Thou lucky Mistress of the tranquil barns,
LG1891	7157	Thou Prairie Dame that sittest in the middle and lookest out upon thy world, and lookest East and lookest West,
LG1891	7158	Dispensatress, that by a word givest a thousand miles, a million farms, and missest nothing,
LG1891	7159	Thou all-acceptress—thou hospitable, (thou only art hospitable as God is hospitable.)
LG1891	7160	When late I sang sad was my voice,
LG1891	7161	Sad were the shows around me with deafening noises of hatred and smoke of war;
LG1891	7162	In the midst of the conflict, the heroes, I stood,
LG1891	7163	Or pass'd with slow step through the wounded and dying.
LG1891	7164	But now I sing not war,
LG1891	7165	Nor the measur'd march of soldiers, nor the tents of camps,
LG1891	7166	Nor the regiments hastily coming up deploying in line of battle;
LG1891	7167	No more the sad, unnatural shows of war.
LG1891	7168	Ask'd room those flush'd immortal ranks, the first forth-stepping armies?
LG1891	7169	Ask room alas the ghastly ranks, the armies dread that follow'd.
LG1891	7170	(Pass, pass, ye proud brigades, with your tramping sinewy legs,
LG1891	7171	With your shoulders young and strong, with your knapsacks and your muskets;
LG1891	7172	How elate I stood and watch'd you, where starting off you march'd.
LG1891	7173	Pass—then rattle drums again,
LG1891	7174	For an army heaves in sight, O another gathering army,

LG1891	7179	But on these days of brightness,

LG1891	7182	Ah the dead to me mar not, they fit well in Nature,
LG1891	7183	They fit very well in the landscape under the trees and grass,
LG1891	7184	And along the edge of the sky in the horizon's far margin.
LG1891	7185	Nor do I forget you Departed,
LG1891	7186	Nor in winter or summer my lost ones,
LG1891	7187	But most in the open air as now when my soul is rapt and at peace, like pleasing phantoms,
LG1891	7188	Your memories rising glide silently by me.
LG1891	7189	I saw the day the return of the heroes,
LG1891	7190	(Yet the heroes never surpass'd shall never return,
LG1891	7191	Them that day I saw not.)
LG1891	7192	I saw the interminable corps, I saw the processions of armies,

LG1891	7194	Streaming northward, their work done, camping awhile in clusters of mighty camps.
LG1891	7195	No holiday soldiers—youthful, yet veterans,
LG1891	7196	Worn, swart, handsome, strong, of the stock of homestead and workshop,
LG1891	7197	Harden'd of many a long campaign and sweaty march,
LG1891	7198	Inured on many a hard-fought bloody field.
LG1891	7199	A pause—the armies wait,
LG1891	7200	A million flush'd embattled conquerors wait,
LG1891	7201	The world too waits, then soft as breaking night and sure as dawn,
LG1891	7202	They melt, they disappear.
LG1891	7203	Exult O lands! victorious lands!
LG1891	7204	Not there your victory on those red shuddering fields,
LG1891	7205	But here and hence your victory.
LG1891	7206	Melt, melt away ye armies—disperse ye blue-clad soldiers,
LG1891	7207	Resolve ye back again, give up for good your deadly arms,
LG1891	7208	Other the arms the fields henceforth for you, or South or North,
LG1891	7209	With saner wars, sweet wars, life-giving wars.
LG1891	7210	Loud O my throat, and clear O soul!
LG1891	7211	The season of thanks and the voice of full-yielding,
LG1891	7212	The chant of joy and power for boundless fertility.
LG1891	7213	All till'd and untill'd fields expand before me,
LG1891	7214	I see the true arenas of my race, or first or last,
LG1891	7215	Man's innocent and strong arenas.
LG1891	7216	I see the heroes at other toils,
LG1891	7217	I see well-wielded in their hands the better weapons.
LG1891	7218	I see where the Mother of All,
LG1891	7219	With full-spanning eye gazes forth, dwells long,
LG1891	7220	And counts the varied gathering of the products.
LG1891	7221	Busy the far, the sunlit panorama,
LG1891	7222	Prairie, orchard, and yellow grain of the North,
LG1891	7223	Cotton and rice of the South and Louisianian cane,
LG1891	7224	Open unseeded fallows, rich fields of clover and timothy,
LG1891	7225	Kine and horses feeding, and droves of sheep and swine,
LG1891	7226	And many a stately river flowing and many a jocund brook,
LG1891	7227	And healthy uplands with herby-perfumed breezes,
LG1891	7228	And the good green grass, that delicate miracle the ever-recurring grass.
LG1891	7229	Toil on heroes! harvest the products!
LG1891	7230	Not alone on those warlike fields the Mother of All,
LG1891	7231	With dilated form and lambent eyes watch'd you.
LG1891	7232	Toil on heroes! toil well! handle the weapons well!
LG1891	7233	The Mother of All, yet here as ever she watches you.
LG1891	7234	Well-pleased America thou beholdest,
LG1891	7235	Over the fields of the West those crawling monsters,
LG1891	7236	The human-divine inventions, the labor-saving implements;
LG1891	7237	Beholdest moving in every direction imbued as with life the revolving hay-rakes,

LG1891	7239	The engines, thrashers of grain and cleaners of grain, well sepa-ratingseparating the straw, the nimble work of the patent pitchfork,

LG1891	7241	Beneath thy look O Maternal,
LG1891	7242	With these and else and with their own strong hands the heroes harvest.
LG1891	7243	All gather and all harvest,
LG1891	7244	Yet but for thee O Powerful, not a scythe might swing as now in security,

LG1891	7253	Cut the flax in the Middle States, or hemp or tobacco in the Borders,
LG1891	7254	Pick the pea and the bean, or pull apples from the trees or bunches of grapes from the vines,
LG1891	7255	Or aught that ripens in all these States or North or South,
LG1891	7256	Under the beaming sun and under thee.
LG1891	7257	THERE was a child went forth every day,
LG1891	7258	And the first object he look'd upon, that object he became,
LG1891	7259	And that object became part of him for the day or a certain part of the day,
LG1891	7260	Or for many years or stretching cycles of years.
LG1891	7261	The early lilacs became part of this child,
LG1891	7262	And grass and white and red morning-glories, and white and red clover, and the song of the phoebe-bird,
LG1891	7263	And the Third-month lambs and the sow's pink-faint litter, and the mare's foal and the cow's calf,
LG1891	7264	And the noisy brood of the barnyard or by the mire of the pond-sidepondside,
LG1891	7265	And the fish suspending themselves so curiously below there, and the beautiful curious liquid,
LG1891	7266	And the water-plants with their graceful flat heads, all became part of him.
LG1891	7267	The field-sprouts of Fourth-month and Fifth-month became part of him,
LG1891	7268	Winter-grain sprouts and those of the light-yellow corn, and the esculent roots of the garden,
LG1891	7269	And the apple-trees cover'd with blossoms and the fruit afterward,and wood-berries, and the commonest weeds by the road,
LG1891	7270	And the old drunkard staggering home from the outhouse of the tavern whence he had lately risen,
LG1891	7271	And the schoolmistress that pass'd on her way to the school,
LG1891	7272	And the friendly boys that pass'd, and the quarrelsome boys,
LG1891	7273	And the tidy and fresh-cheek'd girls, and the barefoot negro boy and girl,
LG1891	7274	And all the changes of city and country wherever he went.
LG1891	7275	His own parents, he that had father'd him and she that had con-ceiv'dconceiv'd him in her womb and birth'd him,
LG1891	7276	They gave this child more of themselves than that,
LG1891	7277	They gave him afterward every day, they became part of him.
LG1891	7278	The mother at home quietly placing the dishes on the supper-tablesuppertable,
LG1891	7279	The mother with mild words, clean her cap and gown, a whole-somewholesome odor falling off her person and clothes as she walks by,
LG1891	7280	The father, strong, self-sufficient, manly, mean, anger'd, unjust,
LG1891	7281	The blow, the quick loud word, the tight bargain, the crafty lure,
LG1891	7282	The family usages, the language, the company, the furniture, the yearning and swelling heart,
LG1891	7283	Affection that will not be gainsay'd, the sense of what is real, the thought if after all it should prove unreal,
LG1891	7284	The doubts of day-time and the doubts of night-time, the curious whether and how,
LG1891	7285	Whether that which appears so is so, or is it all flashes and specks?
LG1891	7286	Men and women crowding fast in the streets, if they are not flashes and specks what are they?
LG1891	7287	The streets themselves and the façades of houses, and goods in the windows,
LG1891	7288	Vehicles, teams, the heavy-plank'd wharves, the huge crossing at the ferries,
LG1891	7289	The village on the highland seen from afar at sunset, the river between,
LG1891	7290	Shadows, aureola and mist, the light falling on roofs and gables of white or brown two miles off,
LG1891	7291	The schooner near by sleepily dropping down the tide, the little boat slack-tow'd astern,
LG1891	7292	The hurrying tumbling waves, quick-broken crests, slapping,
LG1891	7293	The strata of color'd clouds, the long bar of maroon-tint away solitary by itself, the spread of purity it lies motionless in,
LG1891	7294	The horizon's edge, the flying sea-crow, the fragrance of salt marsh and shore mud,
LG1891	7295	These became part of that child who went forth every day, and who now goes, and will always go forth every day.
LG1891	7296	FAR hence amid an isle of wondrous beauty,
LG1891	7297	Crouching over a grave an ancient sorrowful mother,
LG1891	7298	Once a queen, now lean and tatter'd seated on the ground,
LG1891	7299	Her old white hair drooping dishevel'd round her shoulders,
LG1891	7300	At her feet fallen an unused royal harp,
LG1891	7301	Long silent, she too long silent, mourning her shrouded hope and heir,
LG1891	7302	Of all the earth her heart most full of sorrow because most full of love.
LG1891	7303	Yet a word ancient mother,
LG1891	7304	You need crouch there no longer on the cold ground with fore-headforehead between your knees,
LG1891	7305	O you need not sit there veil'd in your old white hair so dishevel'd,
LG1891	7306	For know you the one you mourn is not in that grave,
LG1891	7307	It was an illusion, the son you love was not really dead,
LG1891	7308	The Lord is not dead, he is risen again young and strong in another country,
LG1891	7309	Even while you wept there by your fallen harp by the grave,
LG1891	7310	What you wept for was translated, pass'd from the grave,
LG1891	7311	The winds favor'd and the sea sail'd it,
LG1891	7312	And now with rosy and new blood,
LG1891	7313	Moves to-day in a new country.
LG1891	7314	BY the city dead-house by the gate,
LG1891	7315	As idly sauntering wending my way from the clangor,
LG1891	7316	I curious pause, for lo, an outcast form, a poor dead prostitute brought,
LG1891	7317	Her corpse they deposit unclaim'd, it lies on the damp brick pavement,
LG1891	7318	The divine woman, her body, I see the body, I look on it alone,
LG1891	7319	That house once full of passion and beauty, all else I notice not,
LG1891	7320	Nor stillness so cold, nor running water from faucet, nor odors morbific impress me,
LG1891	7321	But the house alone—that wondrous house—that delicate fair house—that ruin!
LG1891	7322	That immortal house more than all the rows of dwellings ever built!
LG1891	7323	Or white-domed capitol with majestic figure surmounted, or all the old high-spired cathedrals,
LG1891	7324	That little house alone more than them all—poor, desperate house!
LG1891	7325	Fair, fearful wreck—tenement of a soul—itself a soul,
LG1891	7326	Unclaim'd, avoided house—take one breath from my tremulous lips,
LG1891	7327	Take one tear dropt aside as I go for thought of you,
LG1891	7328	Dead house of love—house of madness and sin, crumbled,crush'd,
LG1891	7329	House of life, erewhile talking and laughing—but ah, poor house,dead even then,
LG1891	7330	Months, years, an echoing, garnish'd house—but dead, dead,dead.
LG1891	7331	SOMETHING startles me where I thought I was safest,
LG1891	7332	I withdraw from the still woods I loved,
LG1891	7333	I will not go now on the pastures to walk,
LG1891	7334	I will not strip the clothes from my body to meet my lover the sea,
LG1891	7335	I will not touch my flesh to the earth as to other flesh to renew me.
LG1891	7336	O how can it be that the ground itself does not sicken?
LG1891	7337	How can you be alive you growths of spring?
LG1891	7338	How can you furnish health you blood of herbs, roots, orchards, grain?
LG1891	7339	Are they not continually putting distemper'd corpses within you?
LG1891	7340	Is not every continent work'd over and over with sour dead?
LG1891	7341	Where have you disposed of their carcasses?
LG1891	7342	Those drunkards and gluttons of so many generations?
LG1891	7343	Where have you drawn off all the foul liquid and meat?
LG1891	7344	I do not see any of it upon you to-day, or perhaps I am deceiv'd,
LG1891	7345	I will run a furrow with my plough, I will press my spade through the sod and turn it up underneath,
LG1891	7346	I am sure I shall expose some of the foul meat.
LG1891	7347	Behold this compost! behold it well!
LG1891	7348	Perhaps every mite has once form'd part of a sick person—yet behold!
LG1891	7349	The grass of spring covers the prairies,
LG1891	7350	The bean bursts noiselessly through the mould in the garden,
LG1891	7351	The delicate spear of the onion pierces upward,
LG1891	7352	The apple-buds cluster together on the apple-branches,
LG1891	7353	The resurrection of the wheat appears with pale visage out of its graves,
LG1891	7354	The tinge awakes over the willow-tree and the mulberry-tree,
LG1891	7355	The he-birds carol mornings and evenings while the she-birds sit on their nests,
LG1891	7356	The young of poultry break through the hatch'd eggs,
LG1891	7357	The new-born of animals appear, the calf is dropt from the cow, the colt from the mare,
LG1891	7358	Out of its little hill faithfully rise the potato's dark green leaves,
LG1891	7359	Out of its hill rises the yellow maize-stalk, the lilacs bloom in the dooryards,
LG1891	7360	The summer growth is innocent and disdainful above all those strata of sour dead.
LG1891	7361	What chemistry!
LG1891	7362	That the winds are really not infectious,
LG1891	7363	That this is no cheat, this transparent green-wash of the sea which is so amorous after me,
LG1891	7364	That it is safe to allow it to lick my naked body all over with its tongues,
LG1891	7365	That it will not endanger me with the fevers that have deposited themselves in it,
LG1891	7366	That all is clean forever and forever,
LG1891	7367	That the cool drink from the well tastes so good,
LG1891	7368	That blackberries are so flavorous and juicy,
LG1891	7369	That the fruits of the apple-orchard and the orange-orchard, that melons, grapes, peaches, plums, will none of them poison me,
LG1891	7370	That when I recline on the grass I do not catch any disease,
LG1891	7371	Though probably every spear of grass rises out of what was once a catching disease.
LG1891	7372	Now I am terrified at the Earth, it is that calm and patient,
LG1891	7373	It grows such sweet things out of such corruptions,
LG1891	7374	It turns harmless and stainless on its axis, with such endless successions of diseas'd corpses,
LG1891	7375	It distills such exquisite winds out of such infused fetor,
LG1891	7376	It renews with such unwitting looks its prodigal, annual, sumptu-oussumptuous crops,
LG1891	7377	It gives such divine materials to men, and accepts such leavings from them at last.
LG1891	7378	COURAGE yet, my brother or my sister!
LG1891	7379	Keep on—Liberty is to be subserv'd whatever occurs;
LG1891	7380	That is nothing that is quell'd by one or two failures, or any num-bernumber of failures,
LG1891	7381	Or by the indifference or ingratitude of the people, or by any unfaithfulness,
LG1891	7382	Or the show of the tushes of power, soldiers, cannon, penal statutes.
LG1891	7383	What we believe in waits latent forever through all the continents,
LG1891	7384	Invites no one, promises nothing, sits in calmness and light, is positive and composed, knows no discouragement,
LG1891	7385	Waiting patiently, waiting its time.
LG1891	7386	(Not songs of loyalty alone are these,
LG1891	7387	But songs of insurrection also,
LG1891	7388	For I am the sworn poet of every dauntless rebel the world over,
LG1891	7389	And he going with me leaves peace and routine behind him,
LG1891	7390	And stakes his life to be lost at any moment.)
LG1891	7391	The battle rages with many a loud alarm and frequent advance and retreat,
LG1891	7392	The infidel triumphs, or supposes he triumphs,
LG1891	7393	The prison, scaffold, garroté, handcuffs, iron necklace and lead-ballsleadballs do their work,
LG1891	7394	The named and unnamed heroes pass to other spheres,
LG1891	7395	The great speakers and writers are exiled, they lie sick in distant lands,
LG1891	7396	The cause is asleep, the strongest throats are choked with their own blood,
LG1891	7397	The young men droop their eyelashes toward the ground when they meet;
LG1891	7398	But for all this Liberty has not gone out of the place, nor the infidel enter'd into full possession.
LG1891	7399	When liberty goes out of a place it is not the first to go, nor the second or third to go,
LG1891	7400	It waits for all the rest to go, it is the last.
LG1891	7401	When there are no more memories of heroes and martyrs,
LG1891	7402	And when all life and all the souls of men and women are dis-chargeddischarged from any part of the earth,
LG1891	7403	Then only shall liberty or the idea of liberty be discharged from that part of the earth,
LG1891	7404	And the infidel come into full possession.
LG1891	7405	Then courage European revolter, revoltress!
LG1891	7406	For till all ceases neither must you cease.
LG1891	7407	I do not know what you are for, (I do not know what I am for myself, nor what any thing is for,)
LG1891	7408	But I will search carefully for it even in being foil'd,
LG1891	7409	In defeat, poverty, misconception, imprisonment—for they too are great.
LG1891	7410	Did we think victory great?
LG1891	7411	So it is—but now it seems to me, when it cannot be help'd, that defeat is great,
LG1891	7412	And that death and dismay are great.
LG1891	7413	NATIONS ten thousand years before these States, and many times ten thousand years before these States,
LG1891	7414	Garner'd clusters of ages that men and women like us grew up and travel'd their course and pass'd on,
LG1891	7415	What vast-built cities, what orderly republics, what pastoral tribes and nomads,
LG1891	7416	What histories, rulers, heroes, perhaps transcending all others,
LG1891	7417	What laws, customs, wealth, arts, traditions,
LG1891	7418	What sort of marriage, what costumes, what physiology and phrenology,
LG1891	7419	What of liberty and slavery among them, what they thought of death and the soul,
LG1891	7420	Who were witty and wise, who beautiful and poetic, who brutish and undevelop'd,
LG1891	7421	Not a mark, not a record remains—and yet all remains.
LG1891	7422	O I know that those men and women were not for nothing, any more than we are for nothing,
LG1891	7423	I know that they belong to the scheme of the world every bit as much as we now belong to it.
LG1891	7424	Afar they stand, yet near to me they stand,
LG1891	7425	Some with oval countenances learn'd and calm,
LG1891	7426	Some naked and savage, some like huge collections of insects,
LG1891	7427	Some in tents, herdsmen, patriarchs, tribes, horsemen,
LG1891	7428	Some prowling through woods, some living peaceably on farms,laboring, reaping, filling barns,
LG1891	7429	Some traversing paved avenues, amid temples, palaces, factories,libraries, shows, courts, theatres, wonderful monuments.
LG1891	7430	Are those billions of men really gone?
LG1891	7431	Are those women of the old experience of the earth gone?
LG1891	7432	Do their lives, cities, arts, rest only with us?
LG1891	7433	Did they achieve nothing for good for themselves?
LG1891	7434	I believe of all those men and women that fill'd the unnamed lands, every one exists this hour here or elsewhere, invisible to us,
LG1891	7435	In exact proportion to what he or she grew from in life, and out of what he or she did, felt, became, loved, sinn'd, in life.
LG1891	7436	I believe that was not the end of those nations or any person of them, any more than this shall be the end of my nation, or of me;
LG1891	7437	Of their languages, governments, marriage, literature, products,games, wars, manners, crimes, prisons, slaves, heroes, poets,
LG1891	7438	I suspect their results curiously await in the yet unseen world,counterparts of what accrued to them in the seen world,
LG1891	7439	I suspect I shall meet them there,
LG1891	7440	I suspect I shall there find each old particular of those unnamed lands.
LG1891	7441	MANHATTAN'S streets I saunter'd pondering,
LG1891	7442	On Time, Space, Reality—on such as these, and abreast with them Prudence.
LG1891	7443	The last explanation always remains to be made about prudence,
LG1891	7444	Little and large alike drop quietly aside from the prudence that suits immortality.
LG1891	7445	The soul is of itself,
LG1891	7446	All verges to it, all has reference to what ensues,
LG1891	7447	All that a person does, says, thinks, is of consequence,
LG1891	7448	Not a move can a man or woman make, that affects him or her in a day, month, any part of the direct lifetime, or the hour of death,
LG1891	7449	But the same affects him or her onward afterward through the indirect lifetime.
LG1891	7450	The indirect is just as much as the direct,
LG1891	7451	The spirit receives from the body just as much as it gives to the body, if not more.
LG1891	7452	Not one word or deed, not venereal sore, discoloration, privacy of the onanist,
LG1891	7453	Putridity of gluttons or rum-drinkers, peculation, cunning, betrayal,murder, seduction, prostitution,
LG1891	7454	But has results beyond death as really as before death.
LG1891	7455	Charity and personal force are the only investments worth any thing.
LG1891	7456	No specification is necessary, all that a male or female does, that is vigorous, benevolent, clean, is so much profit to him or her,
LG1891	7457	In the unshakable order of the universe and through the whole scope of it forever.
LG1891	7458	Who has been wise receives interest,
LG1891	7459	Savage, felon, President, judge, farmer, sailor, mechanic, literat,young, old, it is the same,
LG1891	7460	The interest will come round—all will come round.
LG1891	7461	Singly, wholly, to affect now, affected their time, will forever affect,all of the past and all of the present and all of the future,
LG1891	7462	All the brave actions of war and peace,
LG1891	7463	All help given to relatives, strangers, the poor, old, sorrowful, young children, widows, the sick, and to shunn'd persons,
LG1891	7464	All self-denial that stood steady and aloof on wrecks, and saw others fill the seats of the boats,
LG1891	7465	All offering of substance or life for the good old cause, or for a friend's sake, or opinion's sake,
LG1891	7466	All pains of enthusiasts scoff'd at by their neighbors,
LG1891	7467	All the limitless sweet love and precious suffering of mothers,
LG1891	7468	All honest men baffled in strifes recorded or unrecorded,
LG1891	7469	All the grandeur and good of ancient nations whose fragments we inherit,
LG1891	7470	All the good of the dozens of ancient nations unknown to us by name, date, location,
LG1891	7471	All that was ever manfully begun, whether it succeeded or no,
LG1891	7472	All suggestions of the divine mind of man or the divinity of his mouth, or the shaping of his great hands,
LG1891	7473	All that is well thought or said this day on any part of the globe,or on any of the wandering stars, or on any of the fix'd stars, by those there as we are here,
LG1891	7474	All that is henceforth to be thought or done by you whoever you are, or by any one,
LG1891	7475	These inure, have inured, shall inure, to the identities from which they sprang, or shall spring.
LG1891	7476	Did you guess any thing lived only its moment?
LG1891	7477	The world does not so exist, no parts palpable or impalpable so exist,
LG1891	7478	No consummation exists without being from some long previous consummation, and that from some other,
LG1891	7479	Without the farthest conceivable one coming a bit nearer the beginning than any.
LG1891	7480	Whatever satisfies souls is true;
LG1891	7481	Prudence entirely satisfies the craving and glut of souls,
LG1891	7482	Itself only finally satisfies the soul,
LG1891	7483	The soul has that measureless pride which revolts from every lesson but its own.
LG1891	7484	Now I breathe the word of the prudence that walks abreast with time, space, reality,
LG1891	7485	That answers the pride which refuses every lesson but its own.
LG1891	7486	What is prudence is indivisible,
LG1891	7487	Declines to separate one part of life from every part,
LG1891	7488	Divides not the righteous from the unrighteous or the living from the dead,
LG1891	7489	Matches every thought or act by its correlative,
LG1891	7490	Knows no possible forgiveness or deputed atonement,
LG1891	7491	Knows that the young man who composedly peril'd his life and lost it has done exceedingly well for himself without doubt,
LG1891	7492	That he who never peril'd his life, but retains it to old age in riches and ease, has probably achiev'd nothing for himself worth mentioning,
LG1891	7493	Knows that only that person has really learn'd who has learn'd to prefer results,
LG1891	7494	Who favors body and soul the same,
LG1891	7495	Who perceives the indirect assuredly following the direct,
LG1891	7496	Who in his spirit in any emergency whatever neither hurries nor avoids death.
LG1891	7497	O sight of pity, shame and dole!
LG1891	7498	O fearful thought—a convict soul.
LG1891	7499	RANG the refrain along the hall, the prison,
LG1891	7500	Rose to the roof, the vaults of heaven above,
LG1891	7501	Pouring in floods of melody in tones so pensive sweet and strong the like whereof was never heard,
LG1891	7502	Reaching the far-off sentry and the armed guards, who ceas'd their pacing,
LG1891	7503	Making the hearer's pulses stop for ecstasy and awe.
LG1891	7504	The sun was low in the west one winter day,
LG1891	7505	When down a narrow aisle amid the thieves and outlaws of the land,
LG1891	7506	(There by the hundreds seated, sear-faced murderers, wily counter-feiterscounterfeiters,

LG1891	7513	A soul confined by bars and bands,
LG1891	7514	Cries, help! O help! and wrings her hands,
LG1891	7515	Blinded her eyes, bleeding her breast,
LG1891	7516	Nor pardon finds, nor balm of rest.
LG1891	7517	Ceaseless she paces to and fro,
LG1891	7518	O heart-sick days! O nights of woe!
LG1891	7519	Nor hand of friend, nor loving face,
LG1891	7520	Nor favor comes, nor word of grace.
LG1891	7521	It was not I that sinn'd the sin,
LG1891	7522	The ruthless body dragg'd me in;
LG1891	7523	Though long I strove courageously,
LG1891	7524	The body was too much for me.
LG1891	7525	Dear prison'd soul bear up a space,

LG1891	7527	To set thee free and bear thee home,
LG1891	7528	The heavenly pardoner death shall come.
LG1891	7529	Convict no more, nor shame, nor dole!

LG1891	7548	O sight of pity, shame and dole!

LG1891	7550	WARBLE me now for joy of lilac-time, (returning in reminiscence,)
LG1891	7551	Sort me O tongue and lips for Nature's sake, souvenirs of earliest summer,

LG1891	7553	Put in April and May, the hylas croaking in the ponds, the elastic air,

LG1891	7575	A warble for joy of lilac-time, returning in reminiscence.
LG1891	7576	WHAT may we chant, O thou within this tomb?
LG1891	7577	What tablets, outlines, hang for thee, O millionnaire?
LG1891	7578	The life thou lived'st we know not,
LG1891	7579	But that thou walk'dst thy years in barter, 'mid the haunts of brokers,
LG1891	7580	Nor heroism thine, nor war, nor glory.
LG1891	7581	Silent, my soul,

LG1891	7592	A happy painless mother birth'd a perfect child.

LG1891	7625	OUT from behind this bending rough-cut mask,

LG1891	7649	VOCALISM, measure, concentration, determination, and the divine power to speak words;
LG1891	7650	Are you full-lung'd and limber-lipp'd from long trial? from vigor-ousvigorous practice? from physique?
LG1891	7651	Do you move in these broad lands as broad as they?
LG1891	7652	Come duly to the divine power to speak words?
LG1891	7653	For only at last after many years, after chastity, friendship, procrea-tionprocreation, prudence, and nakedness,

LG1891	7661	O what is it in me that makes me tremble so at voices?
LG1891	7662	Surely whoever speaks to me in the right voice, him or her I shall follow,
LG1891	7663	As the water follows the moon, silently, with fluid steps, anywhere around the globe.
LG1891	7664	All waits for the right voices;
LG1891	7665	Where is the practis'd and perfect organ? where is the develop'd soul?
LG1891	7666	For I see every word utter'd thence has deeper, sweeter, new sounds, impossible on less terms.
LG1891	7667	I see brains and lips closed, tympans and temples unstruck,
LG1891	7668	Until that comes which has the quality to strike and to unclose,
LG1891	7669	Until that comes which has the quality to bring forth what lies slumbering forever ready in all words.
LG1891	7670	MY spirit to yours dear brother,
LG1891	7671	Do not mind because many sounding your name do not under-standunderstand you,
LG1891	7672	I do not sound your name, but I understand you,
LG1891	7673	I specify you with joy O my comrade to salute you, and to salute those who are with you, before and since, and those to come also,

LG1891	7683	YOU felons on trial in courts,
LG1891	7684	You convicts in prison-cells, you sentenced assassins chain'd and handcuff'd with iron,

LG1891	7686	Me ruthless and devilish as any, that my wrists are not chain'd with iron, or my ankles with iron?

LG1891	7698	LAWS for creations,
LG1891	7699	For strong artists and leaders, for fresh broods of teachers and perfect literats for America,
LG1891	7700	For noble savans and coming musicians.
LG1891	7701	All must have reference to the ensemble of the world, and the compact truth of the world,

LG1891	7703	What do you suppose creation is?
LG1891	7704	What do you suppose will satisfy the soul, except to walk free and own no superior?

LG1891	7707	And that that is what the oldest and newest myths finally mean?

LG1891	7715	I WAS looking a long while for Intentions,
LG1891	7716	For a clew to the history of the past for myself, and for these chants—and now I have found it,
LG1891	7717	It is not in those paged fables in the libraries, (them I neither accept nor reject,)

LG1891	7719	It is in the present—it is this earth to-day,
LG1891	7720	It is in Democracy—(the purport and aim of all the past,)
LG1891	7721	It is the life of one man or one woman to-day—the average man of to-day,

LG1891	7725	OF persons arrived at high positions, ceremonies, wealth, scholar-shipsscholarships, and the like;

LG1891	7733	WHY, who makes much of a miracle?
LG1891	7734	As to me I know of nothing else but miracles,
LG1891	7735	Whether I walk the streets of Manhattan,
LG1891	7736	Or dart my sight over the roofs of houses toward the sky,
LG1891	7737	Or wade with naked feet along the beach just in the edge of the water,
LG1891	7738	Or stand under trees in the woods,
LG1891	7739	Or talk by day with any one I love, or sleep in the bed at night with any one I love,
LG1891	7740	Or sit at table at dinner with the rest,
LG1891	7741	Or look at strangers opposite me riding in the car,
LG1891	7742	Or watch honey-bees busy around the hive of a summer forenoon,
LG1891	7743	Or animals feeding in the fields,
LG1891	7744	Or birds, or the wonderfulness of insects in the air,
