LG1891	7747	These with the rest, one and all, are to me miracles,
LG1891	7748	The whole referring, yet each distinct and in its place.
LG1891	7749	To me every hour of the light and dark is a miracle,
LG1891	7750	Every cubic inch of space is a miracle,
LG1891	7751	Every square yard of the surface of the earth is spread with the same,
LG1891	7752	Every foot of the interior swarms with the same.
LG1891	7753	To me the sea is a continual miracle,
LG1891	7754	The fishes that swim—the rocks—the motion of the waves—the ships with men in them,
LG1891	7755	What stranger miracles are there?
LG1891	7756	WHERE the city's ceaseless crowd moves on the livelong day,

LG1891	7758	By the curb toward the edge of the flagging,

LG1891	7772	IS reform needed? is it through you?
LG1891	7773	The greater the reform needed, the greater the Personality you need to accomplish it.

LG1891	7779	UNFOLDED out of the folds of the woman man comes unfolded,and is always to come unfolded,

LG1891	7781	Unfolded out of the friendliest woman is to come the friendliest man,
LG1891	7782	Unfolded only out of the perfect body of a woman can a man be form'd of perfect body,

LG1891	7806	OTHERS may praise what they like;

LG1891	7810	WHO learns my lesson complete?
LG1891	7811	Boss, journeyman, apprentice, churchman and atheist,
LG1891	7812	The stupid and the wise thinker, parents and offspring, merchant,clerk, porter and customer,
LG1891	7813	Editor, author, artist, and schoolboy—draw nigh and commence;
LG1891	7814	It is no lesson—it lets down the bars to a good lesson,
LG1891	7815	And that to another, and every one to another still.
LG1891	7816	The great laws take and effuse without argument,
LG1891	7817	I am of the same style, for I am their friend,
LG1891	7818	I love them quits and quits, I do not halt and make salaams.
LG1891	7819	I lie abstracted and hear beautiful tales of things and the reasons of things,
LG1891	7820	They are so beautiful I nudge myself to listen.
LG1891	7821	I cannot say to any person what I hear—I cannot say it to myself —it is very wonderful.
LG1891	7822	It is no small matter, this round and delicious globe moving so exactly in its orbit for ever and ever, without one jolt or the untruth of a single second,
LG1891	7823	I do not think it was made in six days, nor in ten thousand years,nor ten billions of years,
LG1891	7824	Nor plann'd and built one thing after another as an architect plans and builds a house.
LG1891	7825	I do not think seventy years is the time of a man or woman,
LG1891	7826	Nor that seventy millions of years is the time of a man or woman,
LG1891	7827	Nor that years will ever stop the existence of me, or any one else.
LG1891	7828	Is it wonderful that I should be immortal? as every one is im-mortalimmortal;
LG1891	7829	I know it is wonderful, but my eyesight is equally wonderful, and how I was conceived in my mother's womb is equally wonderful,
LG1891	7830	And pass'd from a babe in the creeping trance of a couple of summers and winters to articulate and walk—all this is equally wonderful.
LG1891	7831	And that my soul embraces you this hour, and we affect each other without ever seeing each other, and never perhaps to see each other, is every bit as wonderful.
LG1891	7832	And that I can think such thoughts as these is just as wonderful,
LG1891	7833	And that I can remind you, and you think them and know them to be true, is just as wonderful.
LG1891	7834	And that the moon spins round the earth and on with the earth, is equally wonderful,
LG1891	7835	And that they balance themselves with the sun and stars is equally wonderful.
LG1891	7836	ALL submit to them where they sit, inner, secure, unapproachable to analysis in the soul,
LG1891	7837	Not traditions, not the outer authorities are the judges,
LG1891	7838	They are the judges of outer authorities and of all traditions,
LG1891	7839	They corroborate as they go only whatever corroborates them-selvesthemselves, and touches themselves;
LG1891	7840	For all that, they have it forever in themselves to corroborate far and near without one exception.

LG1891	7845	O STAR of France,
LG1891	7846	The brightness of thy hope and strength and fame,
LG1891	7847	Like some proud ship that led the fleet so long,
LG1891	7848	Beseems to-day a wreck driven by the gale, a mastless hulk,
LG1891	7849	And 'mid its teeming madden'd half-drown'd crowds,
LG1891	7850	Nor helm nor helmsman.
LG1891	7851	Dim smitten star,
LG1891	7852	Orb not of France alone, pale symbol of my soul, its dearest hopes,

LG1891	7854	Of aspirations toward the far ideal, enthusiast's dreams of brother-hoodbrotherhood,

LG1891	7858	Strange, passionate, mocking, frivolous land.

LG1891	7862	In that amid thy many faults thou ever aimedst highly,

LG1891	7872	Product of deathly fire and turbulent chaos,

LG1891	7884	IN a far-away northern county in the placid pastoral region,
LG1891	7885	Lives my farmer friend, the theme of my recitative, a famous tamer of oxen,
LG1891	7886	There they bring him the three-year-olds and the four-year-olds to break them,
LG1891	7887	He will take the wildest steer in the world and break him and tame him,
LG1891	7888	He will go fearless without any whip where the young bullock chafes up and down the yard,
LG1891	7889	The bullock's head tosses restless high in the air with raging eyes,
LG1891	7890	Yet see you! how soon his rage subsides—how soon this tamer tames him;
LG1891	7891	See you! on the farms hereabout a hundred oxen young and old,and he is the man who has tamed them,
LG1891	7892	They all know him, all are affectionate to him;
LG1891	7893	See you! some are such beautiful animals, so lofty looking;
LG1891	7894	Some are buff-color'd, some mottled, one has a white line running along his back, some are brindled,
LG1891	7895	Some have wide flaring horns (a good sign)—see you! the bright hides,
LG1891	7896	See, the two with stars on their foreheads—see, the round bodies and broad backs,
LG1891	7897	How straight and square they stand on their legs—what fine sagacious eyes!
LG1891	7898	How they watch their tamer—they wish him near them—how they turn to look after him!
LG1891	7899	What yearning expression! how uneasy they are when he moves away from them;
LG1891	7900	Now I marvel what it can be he appears to them, (books, politics,poems, depart—all else departs,)
LG1891	7901	I confess I envy only his fascination—my silent, illiterate friend,
LG1891	7902	Whom a hundred oxen love there in his life on farms,
LG1891	7903	In the northern county far, in the placid pastoral region.
LG1891	7904	AN old man's thought of school,
LG1891	7905	An old man gathering youthful memories and blooms that youth itself cannot.
LG1891	7906	Now only do I know you,
LG1891	7907	O fair auroral skies—O morning dew upon the grass!
LG1891	7908	And these I see, these sparkling eyes,
LG1891	7909	These stores of mystic meaning, these young lives,
LG1891	7910	Building, equipping like a fleet of ships, immortal ships,
LG1891	7911	Soon to sail out over the measureless seas,
LG1891	7912	On the soul's voyage.
LG1891	7913	Only a lot of boys and girls?
LG1891	7914	Only the tiresome spelling, writing, ciphering classes?
LG1891	7915	Only a public school?
LG1891	7916	Ah more, infinitely more;
LG1891	7917	(As George Fox rais'd his warning cry, "Is it this pile of brick and mortar, these dead floors, windows, rails, you call the church?
LG1891	7918	Why this is not the church at all—the church is living, ever living souls.")
LG1891	7919	And you America,
LG1891	7920	Cast you the real reckoning for your present?
LG1891	7921	The lights and shadows of your future, good or evil?
LG1891	7922	To girlhood, boyhood look, the teacher and the school.
LG1891	7923	WANDERING at morn,
LG1891	7924	Emerging from the night from gloomy thoughts, thee in my thoughts,
LG1891	7925	Yearning for thee harmonious Union! thee, singing bird divine!
LG1891	7926	Thee coil'd in evil times my country, with craft and black dismay,with every meanness, treason thrust upon thee,
LG1891	7927	This common marvel I beheld—the parent thrush I watch'd feed-ingfeeding its young,

LG1891	7929	Fail not to certify and cheer my soul.
LG1891	7930	There ponder'd, felt I,
LG1891	7931	If worms, snakes, loathsome grubs, may to sweet spiritual songs be turn'd,
LG1891	7932	If vermin so transposed, so used and bless'd may be,

LG1891	7934	Who knows but these may be the lessons fit for you?
LG1891	7935	From these your future song may rise with joyous trills,
LG1891	7936	Destin'd to fill the world.
LG1891	7937	THROUGH the soft evening air enwinding all,
LG1891	7938	Rocks, woods, fort, cannon, pacing sentries, endless wilds,

LG1891	7949	While Nature, sovereign of this gnarl'd realm,
LG1891	7950	Lurking in hidden barbaric grim recesses,
LG1891	7951	Acknowledging rapport however far remov'd,
LG1891	7952	(As some old root or soil of earth its last-born flower or fruit,)
LG1891	7953	Listens well pleas'd.
LG1891	7954	WITH all thy gifts America,
LG1891	7955	Standing secure, rapidly tending, overlooking the world,

LG1891	7959	The towering feminine of thee? the beauty, health, completion,fit for thee?
LG1891	7960	The mothers fit for thee?
LG1891	7961	IN a little house keep I pictures suspended, it is not a fix'd house,
LG1891	7962	It is round, it is only a few inches from one side to the other;
LG1891	7963	Yet behold, it has room for all the shows of the world, all memo-riesmemories!

LG1891	7990	A festival song,

LG1891	8205	Struggles of many a captain, tales of many a sailor dead,

LG1891	8252	Year at whose wide-flung door I sing!

LG1891	8321	Ah more than any priest O soul we too believe in God,

LG1891	8324	Sailing these seas or on the hills, or waking in the night,

LG1891	8370	O secret of the earth and sky!

LG1891	8378	Passage, immediate passage! the blood burns in my veins!

LG1891	8380	Cut the hawsers—haul out—shake out every sail!
LG1891	8381	Have we not stood here like trees in the ground long enough?

LG1891	8386	For we are bound where mariner has not yet dared to go,
LG1891	8387	And we will risk the ship, ourselves and all.
LG1891	8388	O my brave soul!
LG1891	8389	O farther farther sail!
LG1891	8390	O daring joy, but safe! are they not all the seas of God?
LG1891	8391	O farther, farther, farther sail!
LG1891	8392	A BATTER'D, wreck'd old man,
LG1891	8393	Thrown on this savage shore, far, far from home,
LG1891	8394	Pent by the sea and dark rebellious brows, twelve dreary months,
LG1891	8395	Sore, stiff with many toils, sicken'd and nigh to death,
LG1891	8396	I take my way along the island's edge,
LG1891	8397	Venting a heavy heart.
LG1891	8398	I am too full of woe!
LG1891	8399	Haply I may not live another day;
LG1891	8400	I cannot rest O God, I cannot eat or drink or sleep,
LG1891	8401	Till I put forth myself, my prayer, once more to Thee,
LG1891	8402	Breathe, bathe myself once more in Thee, commune with Thee,
LG1891	8403	Report myself once more to Thee.
LG1891	8404	Thou knowest my years entire, my life,

LG1891	8431	One effort more, my altar this bleak sand;
LG1891	8432	That Thou O God my life hast lighted,
LG1891	8433	With ray of light, steady, ineffable, vouchsafed of Thee,

LG1891	8435	Beyond all signs, descriptions, languages;

LG1891	8437	Old, poor, and paralyzed, I thank Thee.
LG1891	8438	My terminus near,
LG1891	8439	The clouds already closing in upon me,
LG1891	8440	The voyage balk'd, the course disputed, lost,
LG1891	8441	I yield my ships to Thee.
LG1891	8442	My hands, my limbs grow nerveless,
LG1891	8443	My brain feels rack'd, bewilder'd,
LG1891	8444	Let the old timbers part, I will not part,
LG1891	8445	I will cling fast to Thee, O God, though the waves buffet me,
LG1891	8446	Thee, Thee at least I know.
LG1891	8447	Is it the prophet's thought I speak, or am I raving?
LG1891	8448	What do I know of life? what of myself?
LG1891	8449	I know not even my own work past or present,
LG1891	8450	Dim ever-shifting guesses of it spread before me,
LG1891	8451	Of newer better worlds, their mighty parturition,
LG1891	8452	Mocking, perplexing me.
LG1891	8453	And these things I see suddenly, what mean they?
LG1891	8454	As if some miracle, some hand divine unseal'd my eyes,
LG1891	8455	Shadowy vast shapes smile through the air and sky,
LG1891	8456	And on the distant waves sail countless ships,
LG1891	8457	And anthems in new tongues I hear saluting me.
LG1891	8458	I WANDER all night in my vision,
LG1891	8459	Stepping with light feet, swiftly and noiselessly stepping and stopping,
LG1891	8460	Bending with open eyes over the shut eyes of sleepers,
LG1891	8461	Wandering and confused, lost to myself, ill-assorted, contradic-torycontradictory,
LG1891	8462	Pausing, gazing, bending, and stopping.
LG1891	8463	How solemn they look there, stretch'd and still,
LG1891	8464	How quiet they breathe, the little children in their cradles.
LG1891	8465	The wretched features of ennuyés, the white features of corpses,the livid faces of drunkards, the sick-gray faces of onanists,
LG1891	8466	The gash'd bodies on battle-fields, the insane in their strong-door'd rooms, the sacred idiots, the new-born emerging from gates, and the dying emerging from gates,
LG1891	8467	The night pervades them and infolds them.
LG1891	8468	The married couple sleep calmly in their bed, he with his palm on the hip of the wife, and she with her palm on the hip of the husband,
LG1891	8469	The sisters sleep lovingly side by side in their bed,
LG1891	8470	The men sleep lovingly side by side in theirs,
LG1891	8471	And the mother sleeps with her little child carefully wrapt.
LG1891	8472	The blind sleep, and the deaf and dumb sleep,
LG1891	8473	The prisoner sleeps well in the prison, the runaway son sleeps,
LG1891	8474	The murderer that is to be hung next day, how does he sleep?
LG1891	8475	And the murder'd person, how does he sleep?
LG1891	8476	The female that loves unrequited sleeps,
LG1891	8477	And the male that loves unrequited sleeps,
LG1891	8478	The head of the money-maker that plotted all day sleeps,
LG1891	8479	And the enraged and treacherous dispositions, all, all sleep.
LG1891	8480	I stand in the dark with drooping eyes by the worst-suffering and the most restless,
LG1891	8481	I pass my hands soothingly to and fro a few inches from them,
LG1891	8482	The restless sink in their beds, they fitfully sleep.
LG1891	8483	Now I pierce the darkness, new beings appear,
LG1891	8484	The earth recedes from me into the night,
LG1891	8485	I saw that it was beautiful, and I see that what is not the earth is beautiful.
LG1891	8486	I go from bedside to bedside, I sleep close with the other sleepers each in turn,
LG1891	8487	I dream in my dream all the dreams of the other dreamers,
LG1891	8488	And I become the other dreamers.
LG1891	8489	I am a dance—play up there! the fit is whirling me fast!
LG1891	8490	I am the ever-laughing—it is new moon and twilight,
LG1891	8491	I see the hiding of douceurs, I see nimble ghosts whichever way I look,
LG1891	8492	Cache and cache again deep in the ground and sea, and where it is neither ground nor sea.
LG1891	8493	Well do they do their jobs those journeymen divine,
LG1891	8494	Only from me can they hide nothing, and would not if they could,
LG1891	8495	I reckon I am their boss and they make me a pet besides,
LG1891	8496	And surround me and lead me and run ahead when I walk,
LG1891	8497	To lift their cunning covers to signify me with stretch'd arms, and resume the way;
LG1891	8498	Onward we move, a gay gang of blackguards! with mirth-shouting music and wild-flapping pennants of joy!
LG1891	8499	I am the actor, the actress, the voter, the politician,
LG1891	8500	The emigrant and the exile, the criminal that stood in the box,
LG1891	8501	He who has been famous and he who shall be famous after to-day,
LG1891	8502	The stammerer, the well-form'd person, the wasted or feeble person.
LG1891	8503	I am she who adorn'd herself and folded her hair expectantly,
LG1891	8504	My truant lover has come, and it is dark.
LG1891	8505	Double yourself and receive me darkness,
LG1891	8506	Receive me and my lover too, he will not let me go without him.
LG1891	8507	I roll myself upon you as upon a bed, I resign myself to the dusk.
LG1891	8508	He whom I call answers me and takes the place of my lover,
LG1891	8509	He rises with me silently from the bed.
LG1891	8510	Darkness, you are gentler than my lover, his flesh was sweaty and panting,
LG1891	8511	I feel the hot moisture yet that he left me.
LG1891	8512	My hands are spread forth, I pass them in all directions,
LG1891	8513	I would sound up the shadowy shore to which you are journeying.
LG1891	8514	Be careful darkness! already what was it touch'd me?
LG1891	8515	I thought my lover had gone, else darkness and he are one,
LG1891	8516	I hear the heart-beat, I follow, I fade away.
LG1891	8517	I descend my western course, my sinews are flaccid,
LG1891	8518	Perfume and youth course through me and I am their wake.
LG1891	8519	It is my face yellow and wrinkled instead of the old woman's,
LG1891	8520	I sit low in a straw-bottom chair and carefully darn my grandson's stockings.
LG1891	8521	It is I too, the sleepless widow looking out on the winter mid-nightmidnight,
LG1891	8522	I see the sparkles of starshine on the icy and pallid earth.
LG1891	8523	A shroud I see and I am the shroud, I wrap a body and lie in the coffin,
LG1891	8524	It is dark here under ground, it is not evil or pain here, it is blank here, for reasons.
LG1891	8525	(It seems to me that every thing in the light and air ought to be happy,
LG1891	8526	Whoever is not in his coffin and the dark grave let him know he has enough.)
LG1891	8527	I see a beautiful gigantic swimmer swimming naked through the eddies of the sea,
LG1891	8528	His brown hair lies close and even to his head, he strikes out with courageous arms, he urges himself with his legs,
LG1891	8529	I see his white body, I see his undaunted eyes,
LG1891	8530	I hate the swift-running eddies that would dash him head-fore-mosthead-foremost on the rocks.
LG1891	8531	What are you doing you ruffianly red-trickled waves?
LG1891	8532	Will you kill the courageous giant? will you kill him in the prime of his middle age?
LG1891	8533	Steady and long he struggles,
LG1891	8534	He is baffled, bang'd, bruis'd, he holds out while his strength holds out,
LG1891	8535	The slapping eddies are spotted with his blood, they bear him away, they roll him, swing him, turn him,
LG1891	8536	His beautiful body is borne in the circling eddies, it is continually bruis'd on rocks,
LG1891	8537	Swiftly and out of sight is borne the brave corpse.
LG1891	8538	I turn but do not extricate myself,
LG1891	8539	Confused, a past-reading, another, but with darkness yet.
LG1891	8540	The beach is cut by the razory ice-wind, the wreck-guns sound,
LG1891	8541	The tempest lulls, the moon comes floundering through the drifts.
LG1891	8542	I look where the ship helplessly heads end on, I hear the burst as she strikes, I hear the howls of dismay, they grow fainter and fainter.
LG1891	8543	I cannot aid with my wringing fingers,
LG1891	8544	I can but rush to the surf and let it drench me and freeze upon me.
LG1891	8545	I search with the crowd, not one of the company is wash'd to us alive,
LG1891	8546	In the morning I help pick up the dead and lay them in rows in a barn.
LG1891	8547	Now of the older war-days, the defeat at Brooklyn,
LG1891	8548	Washington stands inside the lines, he stands on the intrench'd hills amid a crowd of officers,
LG1891	8549	His face is cold and damp, he cannot repress the weeping drops,
LG1891	8550	He lifts the glass perpetually to his eyes, the color is blanch'd from his cheeks,
LG1891	8551	He sees the slaughter of the southern braves confided to him by their parents.
LG1891	8552	The same at last and at last when peace is declared,
LG1891	8553	He stands in the room of the old tavern, the well-belov'd soldiers all pass through,

LG1891	8555	The chief encircles their necks with his arm and kisses them on the cheek,
LG1891	8556	He kisses lightly the wet cheeks one after another, he shakes hands and bids good-by to the army.
LG1891	8557	Now what my mother told me one day as we sat at dinner together,
LG1891	8558	Of when she was a nearly grown girl living home with her parents on the old homestead.
LG1891	8559	A red squaw came one breakfast-time to the old homestead,
LG1891	8560	On her back she carried a bundle of rushes for rush-bottoming chairs,
LG1891	8561	Her hair, straight, shiny, coarse, black, profuse, half-envelop'd her face,
LG1891	8562	Her step was free and elastic, and her voice sounded exquisitely as she spoke.
LG1891	8563	My mother look'd in delight and amazement at the stranger,
LG1891	8564	She look'd at the freshness of her tall-borne face and full and pliant limbs,
LG1891	8565	The more she look'd upon her she loved her,
LG1891	8566	Never before had she seen such wonderful beauty and purity,
LG1891	8567	She made her sit on a bench by the jamb of the fireplace, she cook'd food for her,
LG1891	8568	She had no work to give her, but she gave her remembrance and fondness.
LG1891	8569	The red squaw staid all the forenoon, and toward the middle of the afternoon she went away,
LG1891	8570	O my mother was loth to have her go away,
LG1891	8571	All the week she thought of her, she watch'd for her many a month,
LG1891	8572	She remember'd her many a winter and many a summer,
LG1891	8573	But the red squaw never came nor was heard of there again.
LG1891	8574	A show of the summer softness—a contact of something unseen —an amour of the light and air,
LG1891	8575	I am jealous and overwhelm'd with friendliness,
LG1891	8576	And will go gallivant with the light and air myself.
LG1891	8577	O love and summer, you are in the dreams and in me,
LG1891	8578	Autumn and winter are in the dreams, the farmer goes with his thrift,
LG1891	8579	The droves and crops increase, the barns are well-fill'd.
LG1891	8580	Elements merge in the night, ships make tacks in the dreams,
LG1891	8581	The sailor sails, the exile returns home,
LG1891	8582	The fugitive returns unharm'd, the immigrant is back beyond months and years,
LG1891	8583	The poor Irishman lives in the simple house of his childhood with the well-known neighbors and faces,
LG1891	8584	They warmly welcome him, he is barefoot again, he forgets he is well off,
LG1891	8585	The Dutchman voyages home, and the Scotchman and Welshman voyage home, and the native of the Mediterranean voy-agesvoyages home,
LG1891	8586	To every port of England, France, Spain, enter well-fill'd ships,
LG1891	8587	The Swiss foots it toward his hills, the Prussian goes his way, the Hungarian his way, and the Pole his way,
LG1891	8588	The Swede returns, and the Dane and Norwegian return.
LG1891	8589	The homeward bound and the outward bound,
LG1891	8590	The beautiful lost swimmer, the ennuyé, the onanist, the female that loves unrequited, the money-maker,
LG1891	8591	The actor and actress, those through with their parts and those waiting to commence,
LG1891	8592	The affectionate boy, the husband and wife, the voter, the nominee that is chosen and the nominee that has fail'd,
LG1891	8593	The great already known and the great any time after to-day,
LG1891	8594	The stammerer, the sick, the perfect-form'd, the homely,
LG1891	8595	The criminal that stood in the box, the judge that sat and sen-tencedsentenced him, the fluent lawyers, the jury, the audience,

LG1891	8597	The consumptive, the erysipalite, the idiot, he that is wrong'd,
LG1891	8598	The antipodes, and every one between this and them in the dark,
LG1891	8599	I swear they are averaged now—one is no better than the other,
LG1891	8600	The night and sleep have liken'd them and restored them.
LG1891	8601	I swear they are all beautiful,
LG1891	8602	Every one that sleeps is beautiful, every thing in the dim light is beautiful,
LG1891	8603	The wildest and bloodiest is over, and all is peace.
LG1891	8604	Peace is always beautiful,
LG1891	8605	The myth of heaven indicates peace and night.
LG1891	8606	The myth of heaven indicates the soul,
LG1891	8607	The soul is always beautiful, it appears more or it appears less, it comes or it lags behind,
LG1891	8608	It comes from its embower'd garden and looks pleasantly on itself and encloses the world,
LG1891	8609	Perfect and clean the genitals previously jetting, and perfect and clean the womb cohering,
LG1891	8610	The head well-grown proportion'd and plumb, and the bowels and joints proportion'd and plumb.
LG1891	8611	The soul is always beautiful,
LG1891	8612	The universe is duly in order, every thing is in its place,
LG1891	8613	What has arrived is in its place and what waits shall be in its place,
LG1891	8614	The twisted skull waits, the watery or rotten blood waits,
LG1891	8615	The child of the glutton or venerealee waits long, and the child of the drunkard waits long, and the drunkard himself waits long,

LG1891	8618	The sleepers are very beautiful as they lie unclothed,

LG1891	8625	The breath of the boy goes with the breath of the man, friend is inarm'd by friend,
LG1891	8626	The scholar kisses the teacher and the teacher kisses the scholar,the wrong'd is made right,

LG1891	8628	The felon steps forth from the prison, the insane becomes sane,the suffering of sick persons is reliev'd,

LG1891	8631	Stiflings and passages open, the paralyzed become supple,
LG1891	8632	The swell'd and convuls'd and congested awake to themselves in condition,
LG1891	8633	They pass the invigoration of the night and the chemistry of the night, and awake.
LG1891	8634	I too pass from the night,
LG1891	8635	I stay a while away O night, but I return to you again and love you.
LG1891	8636	Why should I be afraid to trust myself to you?
LG1891	8637	I am not afraid, I have been well brought forward by you,
LG1891	8638	I love the rich running day, but I do not desert her in whom I lay so long,
LG1891	8639	I know not how I came of you and I know not where I go with you, but I know I came well and shall go well.
LG1891	8640	I will stop only a time with the night, and rise betimes,
LG1891	8641	I will duly pass the day O my mother, and duly return to you.
LG1891	8642	LET the reformers descend from the stands where they are forever bawling—let an idiot or insane person appear on each of the stands;
LG1891	8643	Let judges and criminals be transposed—let the prison-keepers be put in prison—let those that were prisoners take the keys;
LG1891	8644	Let them that distrust birth and death lead the rest.
LG1891	8645	TO think of time—of all that retrospection,
LG1891	8646	To think of to-day, and the ages continued henceforward.
LG1891	8647	Have you guess'd you yourself would not continue?
LG1891	8648	Have you dreaded these earth-beetles?
LG1891	8649	Have you fear'd the future would be nothing to you?
LG1891	8650	Is to-day nothing? is the beginningless past nothing?
LG1891	8651	If the future is nothing they are just as surely nothing.
LG1891	8652	To think that the sun rose in the east—that men and women were flexible, real, alive—that every thing was alive,

LG1891	8654	To think that we are now here and bear our part.
LG1891	8655	Not a day passes, not a minute or second without an accouche-mentaccouchement,

LG1891	8657	The dull nights go over and the dull days also,
LG1891	8658	The soreness of lying so much in bed goes over,
LG1891	8659	The physician after long putting off gives the silent and terrible look for an answer,
LG1891	8660	The children come hurried and weeping, and the brothers and sisters are sent for,
LG1891	8661	Medicines stand unused on the shelf, (the camphor-smell has long pervaded the rooms,)

LG1891	8664	The breath ceases and the pulse of the heart ceases,

LG1891	8666	It is palpable as the living are palpable.

LG1891	8669	To think the thought of death merged in the thought of materials,
LG1891	8670	To think of all these wonders of city and country, and others taking great interest in them, and we taking no interest in them.

LG1891	8673	(I see one building the house that serves him a few years, or seventy or eighty years at most,

LG1891	8677	A reminiscence of the vulgar fate,
LG1891	8678	A frequent sample of the life and death of workmen,
LG1891	8679	Each after his kind.
LG1891	8680	Cold dash of waves at the ferry-wharf, posh and ice in the river,half-frozen mud in the streets,

LG1891	8682	A hearse and stages, the funeral of an old Broadway stage-driver,the cortege mostly drivers.
LG1891	8683	Steady the trot to the cemetery, duly rattles the death-bell,
LG1891	8684	The gate is pass'd, the new-dug grave is halted at, the living alight,the hearse uncloses,
LG1891	8685	The coffin is pass'd out, lower'd and settled, the whip is laid on the coffin, the earth is swiftly shovel'd in,
LG1891	8686	The mound above is flatted with the spades—silence,
LG1891	8687	A minute—no one moves or speaks—it is done,
LG1891	8688	He is decently put away—is there any thing more?
LG1891	8689	He was a good fellow, free-mouth'd, quick-temper'd, not bad-lookingbadlooking,
LG1891	8690	Ready with life or death for a friend, fond of women, gambled,ate hearty, drank hearty,
LG1891	8691	Had known what it was to be flush, grew low-spirited toward the last, sicken'd, was help'd by a contribution,

LG1891	8693	Thumb extended, finger uplifted, apron, cape, gloves, strap, wet-weather clothes, whip carefully chosen,

LG1891	8701	To think how much pleasure there is,

LG1891	8722	The orchestra have sufficiently tuned their instruments, the baton has given the signal.

LG1891	8725	The law of the past cannot be eluded,
LG1891	8726	The law of the present and future cannot be eluded,

LG1891	8749	Then indeed suspicion of death.
LG1891	8750	Do you suspect death? if I were to suspect death I should die now,

LG1891	8752	Pleasantly and well-suited I walk,
LG1891	8753	Whither I walk I cannot define, but I know it is good,
LG1891	8754	The whole universe indicates that it is good,
LG1891	8755	The past and the present indicate that it is good.
LG1891	8756	How beautiful and perfect are the animals!
LG1891	8757	How perfect the earth, and the minutest thing upon it!
LG1891	8758	What is called good is perfect, and what is called bad is just as perfect,

LG1891	8760	Slowly and surely they have pass'd on to this, and slowly and surely they yet pass on.

LG1891	8762	The trees have, rooted in the ground! the weeds of the sea have!the animals!

LG1891	8764	That the exquisite scheme is for it, and the nebulous float is for it,and the cohering is for it!

LG1891	8793	CHANTING the square deific, out of the One advancing, out of the sides,

LG1891	8797	Not Time affects me—I am Time, old, modern as any,

LG1891	8800	Aged beyond computation, yet ever new, ever with those mighty laws rolling,

LG1891	8805	Consolator most mild, the promis'd one advancing,
LG1891	8806	With gentle hand extended, the mightier God am I,

LG1891	8809	All sorrow, labor, suffering, I, tallying it, absorb in myself,
LG1891	8810	Many times have I been rejected, taunted, put in prison, and crucified, and many times shall be again,

LG1891	8812	Wending my way through the homes of men, rich or poor, with the kiss of affection,

LG1891	8817	And my sweet love bequeath'd here and elsewhere never dies.
LG1891	8818	Aloof, dissatisfied, plotting revolt,
LG1891	8819	Comrade of criminals, brother of slaves,

LG1891	8869	I NEED no assurances, I am a man who is pre-occupied of his own soul;
LG1891	8870	I do not doubt that from under the feet and beside the hands and face I am cognizant of, are now looking faces I am not cognizant of, calm and actual faces,

LG1891	8881	QUICKSAND years that whirl me I know not whither,

LG1891	8887	THAT music always round me, unceasing, unbeginning, yet long untaught I did not hear,

LG1891	8900	A NOISELESS patient spider,

LG1891	8905	And you O my soul where you stand,

LG1891	8915	To pass on, (O living! always living!) and leave the corpses behind.
LG1891	8916	FROM all the rest I single out you, having a message for you,
LG1891	8917	You are to die—let others tell you what they please, I cannot prevaricate,
LG1891	8918	I am exact and merciless, but I love you—there is no escape for you.
LG1891	8919	Softly I lay my right hand upon you, you just feel it,
LG1891	8920	I do not argue, I bend my head close and half envelop it,
LG1891	8921	I sit quietly by, I remain faithful,
LG1891	8922	I am more than nurse, more than parent or neighbor,
LG1891	8923	I absolve you from all except yourself spiritual bodily, that is eter-naleternal, you yourself will surely escape,
LG1891	8924	The corpse you will leave will be but excrementitious.
LG1891	8925	The sun bursts through in unlooked-for directions,
LG1891	8926	Strong thoughts fill you and confidence, you smile,
LG1891	8927	You forget you are sick, as I forget you are sick,
LG1891	8928	You do not see the medicines, you do not mind the weeping friends, I am with you,
LG1891	8929	I exclude others from you, there is nothing to be commiserated,
LG1891	8930	I do not commiserate, I congratulate you.
LG1891	8931	NIGHT on the prairies,
LG1891	8932	The supper is over, the fire on the ground burns low,
LG1891	8933	The wearied emigrants sleep, wrapt in their blankets;
LG1891	8934	I walk by myself—I stand and look at the stars, which I think now I never realized before.
LG1891	8935	Now I absorb immortality and peace,
LG1891	8936	I admire death and test propositions.
LG1891	8937	How plenteous! how spiritual! how resumé!
LG1891	8938	The same old man and soul—the same old aspirations, and the same content.
LG1891	8939	I was thinking the day most splendid till I saw what the not-day exhibited,
LG1891	8940	I was thinking this globe enough till there sprang out so noiseless around me myriads of other globes.
LG1891	8941	Now while the great thoughts of space and eternity fill me I will measure myself by them,
LG1891	8942	And now touch'd with the lives of other globes arrived as far along as those of the earth,
LG1891	8943	Or waiting to arrive, or pass'd on farther than those of the earth,
LG1891	8944	I henceforth no more ignore them than I ignore my own life,
LG1891	8945	Or the lives of the earth arrived as far as mine, or waiting to arrive.
LG1891	8946	O I see now that life cannot exhibit all to me, as the day cannot,
LG1891	8947	I see that I am to wait for what will be exhibited by death.
LG1891	8948	AS I sit with others at a great feast, suddenly while the music is playing,
LG1891	8949	To my mind, (whence it comes I know not,) spectral in mist of a wreck at sea,
LG1891	8950	Of certain ships, how they sail from port with flying streamers and wafted kisses, and that is the last of them,
LG1891	8951	Of the solemn and murky mystery about the fate of the President,
LG1891	8952	Of the flower of the marine science of fifty generations founder'd off the Northeast coast and going down—of the steamship Arctic going down,
LG1891	8953	Of the veil'd tableau—women gather'd together on deck, pale,heroic, waiting the moment that draws so close—O the moment!
LG1891	8954	A huge sob—a few bubbles—the white foam spirting up—and then the women gone,
LG1891	8955	Sinking there while the passionless wet flows on—and I now pondering, Are those women indeed gone?
LG1891	8956	Are souls drown'd and destroy'd so?
LG1891	8957	Is only matter triumphant?
LG1891	8958	AT the last, tenderly,
LG1891	8959	From the walls of the powerful fortress'd house,

LG1891	8972	PENSIVE and faltering,

LG1891	8977	THOU Mother with thy equal brood,
LG1891	8978	Thou varied chain of different States, yet one identity only,

LG1891	8990	As a strong bird on pinions free,
LG1891	8991	Joyous, the amplest spaces heavenward cleaving,

LG1891	9007	Thought of man justified, blended with God,
LG1891	9008	Through thy idea, lo, the immortal reality!
LG1891	9009	Through thy reality, lo, the immortal idea!
LG1891	9010	Brain of the New World, what a task is thine,

LG1891	9034	Beautiful world of new superber birth that rises to my eyes,

LG1891	9246	What charm thy music works! thou makest pass before me,
LG1891	9247	Ladies and cavaliers long dead, barons are in their castle halls, the troubadours are singing,
LG1891	9248	Arm'd knights go forth to redress wrongs, some in quest of the holy Graal;
LG1891	9249	I see the tournament, I see the contestants incased in heavy armor seated on stately champing horses,
LG1891	9250	I hear the shouts, the sounds of blows and smiting steel;
LG1891	9251	I see the Crusaders' tumultuous armies—hark, how the cymbals clang,
LG1891	9252	Lo, where the monks walk in advance, bearing the cross on high.
LG1891	9253	Blow again trumpeter! and for thy theme,
LG1891	9254	Take now the enclosing theme of all, the solvent and the setting,
LG1891	9255	Love, that is pulse of all, the sustenance and the pang,
LG1891	9256	The heart of man and woman all for love,
LG1891	9257	No other theme but love—knitting, enclosing, all-diffusing love.
LG1891	9258	O how the immortal phantoms crowd around me!
LG1891	9259	I see the vast alembic ever working, I see and know the flames that heat the world,
LG1891	9260	The glow, the blush, the beating hearts of lovers,
LG1891	9261	So blissful happy some, and some so silent, dark, and nigh to death;
LG1891	9262	Love, that is all the earth to lovers—love, that mocks time and space,
LG1891	9263	Love, that is day and night—love, that is sun and moon and stars,
LG1891	9264	Love, that is crimson, sumptuous, sick with perfume,
LG1891	9265	No other words but words of love, no other thought but love.
LG1891	9266	Blow again trumpeter—conjure war's alarums.
LG1891	9267	Swift to thy spell a shuddering hum like distant thunder rolls,
LG1891	9268	Lo, where the arm'd men hasten—lo, mid the clouds of dust the glint of bayonets,
LG1891	9269	I see the grime-faced cannoneers, I mark the rosy flash amid the smoke, I hear the cracking of the guns;
LG1891	9270	Nor war alone—thy fearful music-song, wild player, brings every sight of fear,
LG1891	9271	The deeds of ruthless brigands, rapine, murder—I hear the cries for help!
LG1891	9272	I see ships foundering at sea, I behold on deck and below deck the terrible tableaus.
LG1891	9273	O trumpeter, methinks I am myself the instrument thou playest,
LG1891	9274	Thou melt'st my heart, my brain—thou movest, drawest, chan-gestchangest them at will;
LG1891	9275	And now thy sullen notes send darkness through me,
LG1891	9276	Thou takest away all cheering light, all hope,
LG1891	9277	I see the enslaved, the overthrown, the hurt, the opprest of the whole earth,
LG1891	9278	I feel the measureless shame and humiliation of my race, it becomes all mine,
LG1891	9279	Mine too the revenges of humanity, the wrongs of ages, baffled feuds and hatreds,
LG1891	9280	Utter defeat upon me weighs—all lost—the foe victorious,
LG1891	9281	(Yet 'mid the ruins Pride colossal stands unshaken to the last,
LG1891	9282	Endurance, resolution to the last.)
LG1891	9283	Now trumpeter for thy close,
LG1891	9284	Vouchsafe a higher strain than any yet,
LG1891	9285	Sing to my soul, renew its languishing faith and hope,
LG1891	9286	Rouse up my slow belief, give me some vision of the future,
LG1891	9287	Give me for once its prophecy and joy.
LG1891	9288	O glad, exulting, culminating song!
LG1891	9289	A vigor more than earth's is in thy notes,
LG1891	9290	Marches of victory—man disenthral'd—the conqueror at last,
LG1891	9291	Hymns to the universal God from universal man—all joy!
LG1891	9292	A reborn race appears—a perfect world, all joy!
LG1891	9293	Women and men in wisdom innocence and health—all joy!
LG1891	9294	Riotous laughing bacchanals fill'd with joy!
LG1891	9295	War, sorrow, suffering gone—the rank earth purged—nothing but joy left!
LG1891	9296	The ocean fill'd with joy—the atmosphere all joy!
LG1891	9297	Joy! joy! in freedom, worship, love! joy in the ecstasy of life!
LG1891	9298	Enough to merely be! enough to breathe!
LG1891	9299	Joy! joy! all over joy!
LG1891	9300	THEE for my recitative,
LG1891	9301	Thee in the driving storm even as now, the snow, the winter-day declining,
LG1891	9302	Thee in thy panoply, thy measur'd dual throbbing and thy beat convulsive,
LG1891	9303	Thy black cylindric body, golden brass and silvery steel,
LG1891	9304	Thy ponderous side-bars, parallel and connecting rods, gyrating,shuttling at thy sides,
LG1891	9305	Thy metrical, now swelling pant and roar, now tapering in the distance,
LG1891	9306	Thy great protruding head-light fix'd in front,
LG1891	9307	Thy long, pale, floating vapor-pennants, tinged with delicate purple,
LG1891	9308	The dense and murky clouds out-belching from thy smoke-stack,
LG1891	9309	Thy knitted frame, thy springs and valves, the tremulous twinkle of thy wheels,
LG1891	9310	Thy train of cars behind, obedient, merrily following,
LG1891	9311	Through gale or calm, now swift, now slack, yet steadily careering;
LG1891	9312	Type of the modern—emblem of motion and power—pulse of the continent,
LG1891	9313	For once come serve the Muse and merge in verse, even as here I see thee,
LG1891	9314	With storm and buffeting gusts of wind and falling snow,
LG1891	9315	By day thy warning ringing bell to sound its notes,
LG1891	9316	By night thy silent signal lamps to swing.
LG1891	9317	Fierce-throated beauty!
LG1891	9318	Roll through my chant with all thy lawless music, thy swinging lamps at night,
LG1891	9319	Thy madly-whistled laughter, echoing, rumbling like an earth-quakeearthquake, rousing all,
LG1891	9320	Law of thyself complete, thine own track firmly holding,
LG1891	9321	(No sweetness debonair of tearful harp or glib piano thine,)
LG1891	9322	Thy trills of shrieks by rocks and hills return'd,
LG1891	9323	Launch'd o'er the prairies wide, across the lakes,
LG1891	9324	To the free skies unpent and glad and strong.
LG1891	9325	O MAGNET-SOUTH! O glistening perfumed South! my South!
LG1891	9326	O quick mettle, rich blood, impulse and love! good and evil! O all dear to me!
LG1891	9327	O dear to me my birth-things—all moving things and the trees where I was born—the grains, plants, rivers,
LG1891	9328	Dear to me my own slow sluggish rivers where they flow, distant,over flats of silvery sands or through swamps,
LG1891	9329	Dear to me the Roanoke, the Savannah, the Altamahaw, the Pedee, the Tombigbee, the Santee, the Coosa and the Sabine,
LG1891	9330	O pensive, far away wandering, I return with my soul to haunt their banks again,
LG1891	9331	Again in Florida I float on transparent lakes, I float on the Okee-chobeeOkeechobee, I cross the hummock-land or through pleasant openings or dense forests,
LG1891	9332	I see the parrots in the woods, I see the papaw-tree and the blos-somingblossoming titi;
LG1891	9333	Again, sailing in my coaster on deck, I coast off Georgia, I coast up the Carolinas,
LG1891	9334	I see where the live-oak is growing, I see where the yellow-pine,the scented bay-tree, the lemon and orange, the cypress,the graceful palmetto,
LG1891	9335	I pass rude sea-headlands and enter Pamlico sound through an inlet, and dart my vision inland;
LG1891	9336	O the cotton plant! the growing fields of rice, sugar, hemp!
LG1891	9337	The cactus guarded with thorns, the laurel-tree with large white flowers,
LG1891	9338	The range afar, the richness and barrenness, the old woods charged with mistletoe and trailing moss,
LG1891	9339	The piney odor and the gloom, the awful natural stillness, (here in these dense swamps the freebooter carries his gun, and the fugitive has his conceal'd hut;)
LG1891	9340	O the strange fascination of these half-known half-impassable swamps, infested by reptiles, resounding with the bellow of the alligator, the sad noises of the night-owl and the wild-cat, and the whirr of the rattlesnake,
LG1891	9341	The mocking-bird, the American mimic, singing all the forenoon,singing through the moon-lit night,
LG1891	9342	The humming-bird, the wild turkey, the raccoon, the opossum;
LG1891	9343	A Kentucky corn-field, the tall, graceful, long-leav'd corn, slender,flapping, bright green, with tassels, with beautiful ears each well-sheath'd in its husk;
LG1891	9344	O my heart! O tender and fierce pangs, I can stand them not, I will depart;
LG1891	9345	O to be a Virginian where I grew up! O to be a Carolinian!
LG1891	9346	O longings irrepressible! O I will go back to old Tennessee and never wander more.
LG1891	9347	I WAS asking for something specific and perfect for my city,
LG1891	9348	Whereupon lo! upsprang the aboriginal name.
LG1891	9349	Now I see what there is in a name, a word, liquid, sane, unruly,musical, self-sufficient,
LG1891	9350	I see that the word of my city is that word from of old,
LG1891	9351	Because I see that word nested in nests of water-bays, superb,
LG1891	9352	Rich, hemm'd thick all around with sailships and steamships, an island sixteen miles long, solid-founded,
LG1891	9353	Numberless crowded streets, high growths of iron, slender, strong,light, splendidly uprising toward clear skies,
LG1891	9354	Tides swift and ample, well-loved by me, toward sundown,
LG1891	9355	The flowing sea-currents, the little islands, larger adjoining islands,the heights, the villas,
LG1891	9356	The countless masts, the white shore-steamers, the lighters, the ferry-boats, the black sea-steamers well-model'd,
LG1891	9357	The down-town streets, the jobbers' houses of business, the houses of business of the ship-merchants and money-brokers, the river-streets,
LG1891	9358	Immigrants arriving, fifteen or twenty thousand in a week,
LG1891	9359	The carts hauling goods, the manly race of drivers of horses, the brown-faced sailors,
LG1891	9360	The summer air, the bright sun shining, and the sailing clouds aloft,
LG1891	9361	The winter snows, the sleigh-bells, the broken ice in the river,passing along up or down with the flood-tide or ebb-tide,
LG1891	9362	The mechanics of the city, the masters, well-form'd, beautiful-facedbeautiful-faced, looking you straight in the eyes,

LG1891	9364	A million people—manners free and superb—open voices—hospitality—the most courageous and friendly young men,
LG1891	9365	City of hurried and sparkling waters! city of spires and masts!
LG1891	9366	City nested in bays! my city!
LG1891	9367	O ME, man of slack faith so long,
LG1891	9368	Standing aloof, denying portions so long,
LG1891	9369	Only aware to-day of compact all-diffused truth,
LG1891	9370	Discovering to-day there is no lie or form of lie, and can be none,but grows as inevitably upon itself as the truth does upon itself,
LG1891	9371	Or as any law of the earth or any natural production of the earth does.
LG1891	9372	(This is curious and may not be realized immediately, but it must be realized,
LG1891	9373	I feel in myself that I represent falsehoods equally with the rest,
LG1891	9374	And that the universe does.)
LG1891	9375	Where has fail'd a perfect return indifferent of lies or the truth?
LG1891	9376	Is it upon the ground, or in water or fire? or in the spirit of man?or in the meat and blood?
LG1891	9377	Meditating among liars and retreating sternly into myself, I see that there are really no liars or lies after all,
LG1891	9378	And that nothing fails its perfect return, and that what are called lies are perfect returns,
LG1891	9379	And that each thing exactly represents itself and what has pre-cededpreceded it,
LG1891	9380	And that the truth includes all, and is compact just as much as space is compact,
LG1891	9381	And that there is no flaw or vacuum in the amount of the truth—but that all is truth without exception;
LG1891	9382	And henceforth I will go celebrate any thing I see or am,
LG1891	9383	And sing and laugh and deny nothing.
LG1891	9384	THAT which eludes this verse and any verse,
LG1891	9385	Unheard by sharpest ear, unform'd in clearest eye or cunningest mind,
LG1891	9386	Nor lore nor fame, nor happiness nor wealth,
LG1891	9387	And yet the pulse of every heart and life throughout the world incessantly,
LG1891	9388	Which you and I and all pursuing ever ever miss,
LG1891	9389	Open but still a secret, the real of the real, an illusion,
LG1891	9390	Costless, vouchsafed to each, yet never man the owner,
LG1891	9391	Which poets vainly seek to put in rhyme, historians in prose,
LG1891	9392	Which sculptor never chisel'd yet, nor painter painted,
LG1891	9393	Which vocalist never sung, nor orator nor actor ever utter'd,
LG1891	9394	Invoking here and now I challenge for my song.
LG1891	9395	Indifferently, 'mid public, private haunts, in solitude,
LG1891	9396	Behind the mountain and the wood,
LG1891	9397	Companion of the city's busiest streets, through the assemblage,
LG1891	9398	It and its radiations constantly glide.
LG1891	9399	In looks of fair unconscious babes,
LG1891	9400	Or strangely in the coffin'd dead,
LG1891	9401	Or show of breaking dawn or stars by night,
LG1891	9402	As some dissolving delicate film of dreams,
LG1891	9403	Hiding yet lingering.
LG1891	9404	Two little breaths of words comprising it,
LG1891	9405	Two words, yet all from first to last comprised in it.
LG1891	9406	How ardently for it!
LG1891	9407	How many ships have sail'd and sunk for it!
LG1891	9408	How many travelers started from their homes and ne'er return'd!
LG1891	9409	How much of genius boldly staked and lost for it!
LG1891	9410	What countless stores of beauty, love, ventur'd for it!
LG1891	9411	How all superbest deeds since Time began are traceable to it—and shall be to the end!
LG1891	9412	How all heroic martyrdoms to it!
LG1891	9413	How, justified by it, the horrors, evils, battles of the earth!
LG1891	9414	How the bright fascinating lambent flames of it, in every age and land, have drawn men's eyes,
LG1891	9415	Rich as a sunset on the Norway coast, the sky, the islands, and the cliffs,
LG1891	9416	Or midnight's silent glowing northern lights unreachable.
LG1891	9417	Haply God's riddle it, so vague and yet so certain,
LG1891	9418	The soul for it, and all the visible universe for it,
LG1891	9419	And heaven at last for it.
LG1891	9420	WHO has gone farthest? for I would go farther,
LG1891	9421	And who has been just? for I would be the most just person of the earth,
LG1891	9422	And who most cautious? for I would be more cautious,
LG1891	9423	And who has been happiest? O I think it is I—I think no one was ever happier than I,
LG1891	9424	And who has lavish'd all? for I lavish constantly the best I have,
LG1891	9425	And who proudest? for I think I have reason to be the proudest son alive—for I am the son of the brawny and tall-topt city,
LG1891	9426	And who has been bold and true? for I would be the boldest and truest being of the universe,
LG1891	9427	And who benevolent? for I would show more benevolence than all the rest,
LG1891	9428	And who has receiv'd the love of the most friends? for I know what it is to receive the passionate love of many friends,
LG1891	9429	And who possesses a perfect and enamour'd body? for I do not believe any one possesses a more perfect or enamour'd body than mine,
LG1891	9430	And who thinks the amplest thoughts? for I would surround those thoughts,
LG1891	9431	And who has made hymns fit for the earth? for I am mad with de-vouringdevouring ecstasy to make joyous hymns for the whole earth.
LG1891	9432	AH poverties, wincings, and sulky retreats,
LG1891	9433	Ah you foes that in conflict have overcome me,
LG1891	9434	(For what is my life or any man's life but a conflict with foes, the old, the incessant war?)
LG1891	9435	You degradations, you tussle with passions and appetites,
LG1891	9436	You smarts from dissatisfied friendships, (ah wounds the sharpest of all!)
LG1891	9437	You toil of painful and choked articulations, you meannesses,

LG1891	9439	You broken resolutions, you racking angers, you smother'd ennuis!
LG1891	9440	Ah think not you finally triumph, my real self has yet to come forth,
LG1891	9441	It shall yet march forth o'ermastering, till all lies beneath me,
LG1891	9442	It shall yet stand up the soldier of ultimate victory.
LG1891	9443	OF public opinion,
LG1891	9444	Of a calm and cool fiat sooner or later, (how impassive! how certain and final!)

LG1891	9452	Of the shining sun by them—of the inherent light, greater than the rest,
LG1891	9453	Of the envelopment of all by them, and the effusion of all from them.
LG1891	9454	THEY shall arise in the States,
LG1891	9455	They shall report Nature, laws, physiology, and happiness,
LG1891	9456	They shall illustrate Democracy and the kosmos,
LG1891	9457	They shall be alimentive, amative, perceptive,
LG1891	9458	They shall be complete women and men, their pose brawny and supple, their drink water, their blood clean and clear,
LG1891	9459	They shall fully enjoy materialism and the sight of products, they shall enjoy the sight of the beef, lumber, bread-stuffs, of Chicago the great city,
LG1891	9460	They shall train themselves to go in public to become orators and oratresses,
LG1891	9461	Strong and sweet shall their tongues be, poems and materials of poems shall come from their lives, they shall be makers and finders,
LG1891	9462	Of them and of their works shall emerge divine conveyers, to convey gospels,

LG1891	9464	Death, the future, the invisible faith, shall all be convey'd.
LG1891	9465	WEAVE in, weave in, my hardy life,
LG1891	9466	Weave yet a soldier strong and full for great campaigns to come,
LG1891	9467	Weave in red blood, weave sinews in like ropes, the senses, sight weave in,

LG1891	9471	For great campaigns of peace the same the wiry threads to weave,

LG1891	9473	OUT of the murk of heaviest clouds,

LG1891	9498	FROM far Dakota's cañons,

LG1891	9502	The Indian ambuscade, the craft, the fatal environment,

LG1891	9508	The ancient banner perfectly maintain'd,

LG1891	9510	As sitting in dark days,
LG1891	9511	Lone, sulky, through the time's thick murk looking in vain for light,for hope,

LG1891	9516	Thou of the tawny flowing hair in battle,
LG1891	9517	I erewhile saw, with erect head, pressing ever in front, bearing a bright sword in thy hand,

LG1891	9521	After thy many battles in which never yielding up a gun or a color,
LG1891	9522	Leaving behind thee a memory sweet to soldiers,
LG1891	9523	Thou yieldest up thyself.
LG1891	9524	IN midnight sleep of many a face of anguish,
LG1891	9525	Of the look at first of the mortally wounded, (of that indescribable look,)

LG1891	9527	I dream, I dream, I dream.
LG1891	9528	Of scenes of Nature, fields and mountains,
LG1891	9529	Of skies so beauteous after a storm, and at night the moon so unearthly bright,

LG1891	9532	Long have they pass'd, faces and trenches and fields,

LG1891	9536	THICK-SPRINKLED bunting! flag of stars!
LG1891	9537	Long yet your road, fateful flag—long yet your road, and lined with bloody death,
LG1891	9538	For the prize I see at issue at last is the world,
LG1891	9539	All its ships and shores I see interwoven with your threads greedy banner;
LG1891	9540	Dream'd again the flags of kings, highest borne, to flaunt unrival'd?
LG1891	9541	O hasten flag of man—O with sure and steady step, passing highest flags of kings,
LG1891	9542	Walk supreme to the heavens mighty symbol—run up above them all,
LG1891	9543	Flag of stars! thick-sprinkled bunting!
LG1891	9544	WHAT best I see in thee,
LG1891	9545	Is not that where thou mov'st down history's great highways,
LG1891	9546	Ever undimm'd by time shoots warlike victory's dazzle,
LG1891	9547	Or that thou sat'st where Washington sat, ruling the land in peace,
LG1891	9548	Or thou the man whom feudal Europe feted, venerable Asia swarm'd upon,
LG1891	9549	Who walk'd with kings with even pace the round world's prome-nadepromenade;
LG1891	9550	But that in foreign lands, in all thy walks with kings,
