LG1891	9554	Were all so justified.
LG1891	9555	SPIRIT that form'd this scene,
LG1891	9556	These tumbled rock-piles grim and red,
LG1891	9557	These reckless heaven-ambitious peaks,
LG1891	9558	These gorges, turbulent-clear streams, this naked freshness,
LG1891	9559	These formless wild arrays, for reasons of their own,
LG1891	9560	I know thee, savage spirit—we have communed together,
LG1891	9561	Mine too such wild arrays, for reasons of their own;
LG1891	9562	Was't charged against my chants they had forgotten art?
LG1891	9563	To fuse within themselves its rules precise and delicatesse?
LG1891	9564	The lyrist's measur'd beat, the wrought-out temple's grace—column and polish'd arch forgot?
LG1891	9565	But thou that revelest here—spirit that form'd this scene,
LG1891	9566	They have remember'd thee.
LG1891	9567	AS I walk these broad majestic days of peace,
LG1891	9568	(For the war, the struggle of blood finish'd, wherein, O terrific Ideal,
LG1891	9569	Against vast odds erewhile having gloriously won,
LG1891	9570	Now thou stridest on, yet perhaps in time toward denser wars,
LG1891	9571	Perhaps to engage in time in still more dreadful contests, dangers,
LG1891	9572	Longer campaigns and crises, labors beyond all others,)
LG1891	9573	Around me I hear that eclat of the world, politics, produce,
LG1891	9574	The announcements of recognized things, science,
LG1891	9575	The approved growth of cities and the spread of inventions.
LG1891	9576	I see the ships, (they will last a few years,)
LG1891	9577	The vast factories with their foremen and workmen,
LG1891	9578	And hear the indorsement of all, and do not object to it.
LG1891	9579	But I too announce solid things,
LG1891	9580	Science, ships, politics, cities, factories, are not nothing,
LG1891	9581	Like a grand procession to music of distant bugles pouring,triumphantly moving, and grander heaving in sight,
LG1891	9582	They stand for realities—all is as it should be.
LG1891	9583	Then my realities;
LG1891	9584	What else is so real as mine?
LG1891	9585	Libertad and the divine average, freedom to every slave on the face of the earth,
LG1891	9586	The rapt promises and luminè of seers, the spiritual world, these centuries-lasting songs,
LG1891	9587	And our visions, the visions of poets, the most solid announce-mentsannouncements of any.
LG1891	9588	THIS is thy hour O Soul, thy free flight into the wordless,
LG1891	9589	Away from books, away from art, the day erased, the lesson done,
LG1891	9590	Thee fully forth emerging, silent, gazing, pondering the themes thou lovest best,
LG1891	9591	Night, sleep, death and the stars.
LG1891	9592	AS the time draws nigh glooming a cloud,
LG1891	9593	A dread beyond of I know not what darkens me.
LG1891	9594	I shall go forth,
LG1891	9595	I shall traverse the States awhile, but I cannot tell whither or how long,
LG1891	9596	Perhaps soon some day or night while I am singing my voice will suddenly cease.
LG1891	9597	O book, O chants! must all then amount to but this?
LG1891	9598	Must we barely arrive at this beginning of us?—and yet it is enough, O soul;
LG1891	9599	O soul, we have positively appear'd—that is enough.
LG1891	9600	YEARS of the modern! years of the unperform'd!
LG1891	9601	Your horizon rises, I see it parting away for more august dramas,
LG1891	9602	I see not America only, not only Liberty's nation but other nations preparing,
LG1891	9603	I see tremendous entrances and exits, new combinations, the soli-daritysolidarity of races,
LG1891	9604	I see that force advancing with irresistible power on the world's stage,
LG1891	9605	(Have the old forces, the old wars, played their parts? are the acts suitable to them closed?)
LG1891	9606	I see Freedom, completely arm'd and victorious and very haughty,with Law on one side and Peace on the other,
LG1891	9607	A stupendous trio all issuing forth against the idea of caste;
LG1891	9608	What historic denouements are these we so rapidly approach?
LG1891	9609	I see men marching and countermarching by swift millions,
LG1891	9610	I see the frontiers and boundaries of the old aristocracies broken,
LG1891	9611	I see the landmarks of European kings removed,
LG1891	9612	I see this day the People beginning their landmarks, (all others give way;)
LG1891	9613	Never were such sharp questions ask'd as this day,
LG1891	9614	Never was average man, his soul, more energetic, more like a God,
LG1891	9615	Lo, how he urges and urges, leaving the masses no rest!
LG1891	9616	His daring foot is on land and sea everywhere, he colonizes the Pacific, the archipelagoes,
LG1891	9617	With the steamship, the electric telegraph, the newspaper, the wholesale engines of war,
LG1891	9618	With these and the world-spreading factories he interlinks all geography, all lands;
LG1891	9619	What whispers are these O lands, running ahead of you, passing under the seas?
LG1891	9620	Are all nations communing? is there going to be but one heart to the globe?
LG1891	9621	Is humanity forming en-masse? for lo, tyrants tremble, crowns grow dim,
LG1891	9622	The earth, restive, confronts a new era, perhaps a general divine war,
LG1891	9623	No one knows what will happen next, such portents fill the days and nights;
LG1891	9624	Years prophetical! the space ahead as I walk, as I vainly try to pierce it, is full of phantoms,
LG1891	9625	Unborn deeds, things soon to be, project their shapes around me,
LG1891	9626	This incredible rush and heat, this strange ecstatic fever of dreams O years!
LG1891	9627	Your dreams O years, how they penetrate through me! (I know not whether I sleep or wake;)
LG1891	9628	The perform'd America and Europe grow dim, retiring in shadow behind me,
LG1891	9629	The unperform'd, more gigantic than ever, advance, advance upon me.
LG1891	9630	ASHES of soldiers South or North,
LG1891	9631	As I muse retrospective murmuring a chant in thought,
LG1891	9632	The war resumes, again to my sense your shapes,
LG1891	9633	And again the advance of the armies.
LG1891	9634	Noiseless as mists and vapors,
LG1891	9635	From their graves in the trenches ascending,
LG1891	9636	From cemeteries all through Virginia and Tennessee,
LG1891	9637	From every point of the compass out of the countless graves,
LG1891	9638	In wafted clouds, in myriads large, or squads of twos or threes or single ones they come,
LG1891	9639	And silently gather round me.
LG1891	9640	Now sound no note O trumpeters,
LG1891	9641	Not at the head of my cavalry parading on spirited horses,
LG1891	9642	With sabres drawn and glistening, and carbines by their thighs, (ah my brave horsemen!
LG1891	9643	My handsome tan-faced horsemen! what life, what joy and pride,
LG1891	9644	With all the perils were yours.)
LG1891	9645	Nor you drummers, neither at reveillé at dawn,
LG1891	9646	Nor the long roll alarming the camp, nor even the muffled beat for a burial,
LG1891	9647	Nothing from you this time O drummers bearing my warlike drums.
LG1891	9648	But aside from these and the marts of wealth and the crowded promenade,
LG1891	9649	Admitting around me comrades close unseen by the rest and voiceless,
LG1891	9650	The slain elate and alive again, the dust and debris alive,
LG1891	9651	I chant this chant of my silent soul in the name of all dead soldiers.
LG1891	9652	Faces so pale with wondrous eyes, very dear, gather closer yet,
LG1891	9653	Draw close, but speak not.
LG1891	9654	Phantoms of countless lost,
LG1891	9655	Invisible to the rest henceforth become my companions,
LG1891	9656	Follow me ever—desert me not while I live.
LG1891	9657	Sweet are the blooming cheeks of the living—sweet are the musi-calmusical voices sounding,
LG1891	9658	But sweet, ah sweet, are the dead with their silent eyes.
LG1891	9659	Dearest comrades, all is over and long gone,
LG1891	9660	But love is not over—and what love, O comrades!
LG1891	9661	Perfume from battle-fields rising, up from the foetor arising.
LG1891	9662	Perfume therefore my chant, O love, immortal love,
LG1891	9663	Give me to bathe the memories of all dead soldiers,
LG1891	9664	Shroud them, embalm them, cover them all over with tender pride.
LG1891	9665	Perfume all—make all wholesome,
LG1891	9666	Make these ashes to nourish and blossom,
LG1891	9667	O love, solve all, fructify all with the last chemistry.
LG1891	9668	Give me exhaustless, make me a fountain,
LG1891	9669	That I exhale love from me wherever I go like a moist perennial dew,
LG1891	9670	For the ashes of all dead soldiers South or North.
LG1891	9671	OF these years I sing,
LG1891	9672	How they pass and have pass'd through convuls'd pains, as through parturitions,
LG1891	9673	How America illustrates birth, muscular youth, the promise, the sure fulfilment, the absolute success, despite of people— illustrates evil as well as good,
LG1891	9674	The vehement struggle so fierce for unity in one's-self;
LG1891	9675	How many hold despairingly yet to the models departed, caste, myths, obedience, compulsion, and to infidelity,
LG1891	9676	How few see the arrived models, the athletes, the Western States, or see freedom or spirituality, or hold any faith in results,
LG1891	9677	(But I see the athletes, and I see the results of the war glorious and inevitable, and they again leading to other results.)
LG1891	9678	How the great cities appear—how the Democratic masses, turbu-lentturbulent, wilful, as I love them,
LG1891	9679	How the whirl, the contest, the wrestle of evil with good, the sounding and resounding, keep on and on,
LG1891	9680	How society waits unform'd, and is for a while between things ended and things begun,
LG1891	9681	How America is the continent of glories, and of the triumph of freedom and of the Democracies, and of the fruits of so-cietysociety, and of all that is begun,
LG1891	9682	And how the States are complete in themselves—and how all triumphs and glories are complete in themselves, to lead onward,
LG1891	9683	And how these of mine and of the States will in their turn be con-vuls'dconvuls'd, and serve other parturitions and transitions,
LG1891	9684	And how all people, sights, combinations, the democratic masses too, serve—and how every fact, and war itself, with all its horrors, serves,
LG1891	9685	And how now or at any time each serves the exquisite transition of death.
LG1891	9686	Of seeds dropping into the ground, of births,
LG1891	9687	Of the steady concentration of America, inland, upward, to im-pregnableimpregnable and swarming places,
LG1891	9688	Of what Indiana, Kentucky, Arkansas, and the rest, are to be,
LG1891	9689	Of what a few years will show there in Nebraska, Colorado, Nevada, and the rest,
LG1891	9690	(Or afar, mounting the Northern Pacific to Sitka or AliaskaAlaska,)
LG1891	9691	Of what the feuillage of America is the preparation for—and of what all sights, North, South, East and West, are,
LG1891	9692	Of this Union welded in blood, of the solemn price paid, of the unnamed lost ever present in my mind;
LG1891	9693	Of the temporary use of materials for identity's sake,
LG1891	9694	Of the present, passing, departing—of the growth of completer men than any yet,
LG1891	9695	Of all sloping down there where the fresh free giver the mother, the Mississippi flows,
LG1891	9696	Of mighty inland cities yet unsurvey'd and unsuspected,
LG1891	9697	Of the new and good names, of the modern developments, of inalienable homesteads,
LG1891	9698	Of a free and original life there, of simple diet and clean and sweet blood,
LG1891	9699	Of litheness, majestic faces, clear eyes, and perfect physique there,
LG1891	9700	Of immense spiritual results future years far West, each side of the Anahuacs,
LG1891	9701	Of these songs, well understood there, (being made for that area,)
LG1891	9702	Of the native scorn of grossness and gain there,
LG1891	9703	(O it lurks in me night and day—what is gain after all to savage-nesssavageness and freedom?)
LG1891	9704	SPLENDOR of ended day floating and filling me,
LG1891	9705	Hour prophetic, hour resuming the past,
LG1891	9706	Inflating my throat, you divine average,
LG1891	9707	You earth and life till the last ray gleams I sing.
LG1891	9708	Open mouth of my soul uttering gladness,
LG1891	9709	Eyes of my soul seeing perfection,
LG1891	9710	Natural life of me faithfully praising things,
LG1891	9711	Corroborating forever the triumph of things.
LG1891	9712	Illustrious every one!
LG1891	9713	Illustrious what we name space, sphere of unnumber'd spirits,
LG1891	9714	Illustrious the mystery of motion in all beings, even the tiniest insect,
LG1891	9715	Illustrious the attribute of speech, the senses, the body,
LG1891	9716	Illustrious the passing light—illustrious the pale reflection on the new moon in the western sky,
LG1891	9717	Illustrious whatever I see or hear or touch, to the last.
LG1891	9718	Good in all,
LG1891	9719	In the satisfaction and aplomb of animals,
LG1891	9720	In the annual return of the seasons,
LG1891	9721	In the hilarity of youth,
LG1891	9722	In the strength and flush of manhood,
LG1891	9723	In the grandeur and exquisiteness of old age,
LG1891	9724	In the superb vistas of death.
LG1891	9725	Wonderful to depart!
LG1891	9726	Wonderful to be here!
LG1891	9727	The heart, to jet the all-alike and innocent blood!
LG1891	9728	To breathe the air, how delicious!
LG1891	9729	To speak—to walk—to seize something by the hand!
LG1891	9730	To prepare for sleep, for bed, to look on my rose-color'd flesh!
LG1891	9731	To be conscious of my body, so satisfied, so large!
LG1891	9732	To be this incredible God I am!
LG1891	9733	To have gone forth among other Gods, these men and women I love.
LG1891	9734	Wonderful how I celebrate you and myself!
LG1891	9735	How my thoughts play subtly at the spectacles around!
LG1891	9736	How the clouds pass silently overhead!
LG1891	9737	How the earth darts on and on! and how the sun, moon, stars,dart on and on!
LG1891	9738	How the water sports and sings! (surely it is alive!)
LG1891	9739	How the trees rise and stand up, with strong trunks, with branches and leaves!
LG1891	9740	(Surely there is something more in each of the trees, some living soul.)
LG1891	9741	O amazement of things—even the least particle!
LG1891	9742	O spirituality of things!
LG1891	9743	O strain musical flowing through ages and continents, now reaching me and America!
LG1891	9744	I take your strong chords, intersperse them, and cheerfully pass them forward.
LG1891	9745	I too carol the sun, usher'd or at noon, or as now, setting,
LG1891	9746	I too throb to the brain and beauty of the earth and of all the growths of the earth,
LG1891	9747	I too have felt the resistless call of myself.
LG1891	9748	As I steam'd down the Mississippi,
LG1891	9749	As I wander'd over the prairies,
LG1891	9750	As I have lived, as I have look'd through my windows my eyes,
LG1891	9751	As I went forth in the morning, as I beheld the light breaking in the east,
LG1891	9752	As I bathed on the beach of the Eastern Sea, and again on the beach of the Western Sea,
LG1891	9753	As I roam'd the streets of inland Chicago, whatever streets I have roam'd,
LG1891	9754	Or cities or silent woods, or even amid the sights of war,
LG1891	9755	Wherever I have been I have charged myself with contentment and triumph.
LG1891	9756	I sing to the last the equalities modern or old,
LG1891	9757	I sing the endless finalés of things,
LG1891	9758	I say Nature continues, glory continues,
LG1891	9759	I praise with electric voice,
LG1891	9760	For I do not see one imperfection in the universe,
LG1891	9761	And I do not see one cause or result lamentable at last in the universe.
LG1891	9762	O setting sun! though the time has come,
LG1891	9763	I still warble under you, if none else does, unmitigated adoration.
LG1891	9764	AS at thy portals also death,
LG1891	9765	Entering thy sovereign, dim, illimitable grounds,
LG1891	9766	To memories of my mother, to the divine blending, maternity,
LG1891	9767	To her, buried and gone, yet buried not, gone not from me,
LG1891	9768	(I see again the calm benignant face fresh and beautiful still,
LG1891	9769	I sit by the form in the coffin,
LG1891	9770	I kiss and kiss convulsively again the sweet old lips, the cheeks,the closed eyes in the coffin;)
LG1891	9771	To her, the ideal woman, practical, spiritual, of all of earth, life,love, to me the best,
LG1891	9772	I grave a monumental line, before I go, amid these songs,
LG1891	9773	And set a tombstone here.
LG1891	9774	THE business man the acquirer vast,
LG1891	9775	After assiduous years surveying results, preparing for departure,
LG1891	9776	Devises houses and lands to his children, bequeaths stocks, goods,funds for a school or hospital,
LG1891	9777	Leaves money to certain companions to buy tokens, souvenirs of gems and gold.
LG1891	9778	But I, my life surveying, closing,
LG1891	9779	With nothing to show to devise from its idle years,
LG1891	9780	Nor houses nor lands, nor tokens of gems or gold for my friends,
LG1891	9781	Yet certain remembrances of the war for you, and after you,
LG1891	9782	And little souvenirs of camps and soldiers, with my love,
LG1891	9783	I bind together and bequeath in this bundle of songs.
LG1891	9784	PENSIVE on her dead gazing I heard the Mother of All,
LG1891	9785	Desperate on the torn bodies, on the forms covering the battle-fieldsbattlefields gazing,
LG1891	9786	(As the last gun ceased, but the scent of the powder-smoke linger'd,)
LG1891	9787	As she call'd to her earth with mournful voice while she stalk'd,
LG1891	9788	Absorb them well O my earth, she cried, I charge you lose not my sons, lose not an atom,
LG1891	9789	And you streams absorb them well, taking their dear blood,
LG1891	9790	And you local spots, and you airs that swim above lightly impalpable,
LG1891	9791	And all you essences of soil and growth, and you my rivers' depths,
LG1891	9792	And you mountain sides, and the woods where my dear children's blood trickling redden'd,
LG1891	9793	And you trees down in your roots to bequeath to all future trees,
LG1891	9794	My dead absorb or South or North—my young men's bodies absorb, and their precious precious blood,
LG1891	9795	Which holding in trust for me faithfully back again give me many a year hence,
LG1891	9796	In unseen essence and odor of surface and grass, centuries hence,
LG1891	9797	In blowing airs from the fields back again give me my darlings,give my immortal heroes,
LG1891	9798	Exhale me them centuries hence, breathe me their breath, let not an atom be lost,
LG1891	9799	O years and graves! O air and soil! O my dead, an aroma sweet!
LG1891	9800	Exhale them perennial sweet death, years, centuries hence.
LG1891	9801	NOT alone those camps of white, old comrades of the wars,
LG1891	9802	When as order'd forward, after a long march,
LG1891	9803	Footsore and weary, soon as the light lessens we halt for the night,
LG1891	9804	Some of us so fatigued carrying the gun and knapsack, dropping asleep in our tracks,
LG1891	9805	Others pitching the little tents, and the fires lit up begin to sparkle,
LG1891	9806	Outposts of pickets posted surrounding alert through the dark,
LG1891	9807	And a word provided for countersign, careful for safety,
LG1891	9808	Till to the call of the drummers at daybreak loudly beating the drums,
LG1891	9809	We rise up refresh'd, the night and sleep pass'd over, and resume our journey,
LG1891	9810	Or proceed to battle.
LG1891	9811	Lo, the camps of the tents of green,
LG1891	9812	Which the days of peace keep filling, and the days of war keep filling,
LG1891	9813	With a mystic army, (is it too order'd forward? is it too only halt ing awhile,
LG1891	9814	Till night and sleep pass over?)
LG1891	9815	Now in those camps of green, in their tents dotting the world,
LG1891	9816	In the parents, children, husbands, wives, in them, in the old and young,
LG1891	9817	Sleeping under the sunlight, sleeping under the moonlight, content and silent there at last,
LG1891	9818	Behold the mighty bivouac-field and waiting-camp of all,
LG1891	9819	Of the corps and generals all, and the President over the corps and generals all,
LG1891	9820	And of each of us O soldiers, and of each and all in the ranks we fought,
LG1891	9821	(There without hatred we all, all meet.)
LG1891	9822	For presently O soldiers, we too camp in our place in the bivouac-campsbivouccamps of green,
LG1891	9823	But we need not provide for outposts, nor word for the counter-signcountersign,
LG1891	9824	Nor drummer to beat the morning drum.
LG1891	9825	THE sobbing of the bells, the sudden death-news everywhere,
LG1891	9826	The slumberers rouse, the rapport of the People,
LG1891	9827	(Full well they know that message in the darkness,
LG1891	9828	Full well return, respond within their breasts, their brains, the sad reverberations,)
LG1891	9829	The passionate toll and clang—city to city, joining, sounding,passing,
LG1891	9830	Those heart-beats of a Nation in the night.
LG1891	9831	AS they draw to a close,
LG1891	9832	Of what underlies the precedent songs—of my aims in them,
LG1891	9833	Of the seed I have sought to plant in them,
LG1891	9834	Of joy, sweet joy, through many a year, in them,
LG1891	9835	(For them, for them have I lived, in them my work is done,)
LG1891	9836	Of many an aspiration fond, of many a dream and plan;
LG1891	9837	Through Space and Time fused in a chant, and the flowing eternal identity,
LG1891	9838	To Nature encompassing these, encompassing God—to the joy-ousjoyous, electric all,
LG1891	9839	To the sense of Death, and accepting exulting in Death in its turn the same as life,
LG1891	9840	The entrance of man to sing;
LG1891	9841	To compact you, ye parted, diverse lives,
LG1891	9842	To put rapport the mountains and rocks and streams,
LG1891	9843	And the winds of the north, and the forests of oak and pine,
LG1891	9844	With you O soul.
LG1891	9845	JOY, shipmate, joy!
LG1891	9846	(Pleas'd to my soul at death I cry,)
LG1891	9847	Our life is closed, our life begins,
LG1891	9848	The long, long anchorage we leave,
LG1891	9849	The ship is clear at last, she leaps!
LG1891	9850	She swiftly courses from the shore,
LG1891	9851	Joy, shipmate, joy.
LG1891	9852	THE untold want by life and land ne'er granted,
LG1891	9853	Now voyager sail thou forth to seek and find.
LG1891	9854	WHAT are those of the known but to ascend and enter the Unknown?
LG1891	9855	And what are those of life but for Death?
LG1891	9856	THESE carols sung to cheer my passage through the world I see,
LG1891	9857	For completion I dedicate to the Invisible World.
LG1891	9858	NOW finalè to the shore,
LG1891	9859	Now land and life finalè and farewell,
LG1891	9860	Now Voyager depart, (much, much for thee is yet in store,)
LG1891	9861	Often enough hast thou adventur'd o'er the seas,
LG1891	9862	Cautiously cruising, studying the charts,
LG1891	9863	Duly again to port and hawser's tie returning;
LG1891	9864	But now obey thy cherish'd secret wish,
LG1891	9865	Embrace thy friends, leave all in order,
LG1891	9866	To port and hawser's tie no more returning,
LG1891	9867	Depart upon thy endless cruise old Sailor.
LG1891	9868	TO conclude, I announce what comes after me.
LG1891	9869	I remember I said before my leaves sprang at all,
LG1891	9870	I would raise my voice jocund and strong with reference to con-summationsconsummations.
LG1891	9871	When America does what was promis'd,
LG1891	9872	When through these States walk a hundred millions of superb persons,
LG1891	9873	When the rest part away for superb persons and contribute to them,
LG1891	9874	When breeds of the most perfect mothers denote America,
LG1891	9875	Then to me and mine our due fruition.
LG1891	9876	I have press'd through in my own right,
LG1891	9877	I have sung the body and the soul, war and peace have I sung,and the songs of life and death,
LG1891	9878	And the songs of birth, and shown that there are many births.
LG1891	9879	I have offer'd my style to every one, I have journey'd with confi-dentconfident step;
LG1891	9880	While my pleasure is yet at the full I whisper So long!
LG1891	9881	And take the young woman's hand and the young man's hand for the last time.
LG1891	9882	I announce natural persons to arise,
LG1891	9883	I announce justice triumphant,
LG1891	9884	I announce uncompromising liberty and equality,
LG1891	9885	I announce the justification of candor and the justification of pride.
LG1891	9886	I announce that the identity of these States is a single identity only,
LG1891	9887	I announce the Union more and more compact, indissoluble,
LG1891	9888	I announce splendors and majesties to make all the previous poli-ticspolitics of the earth insignificant.
LG1891	9889	I announce adhesiveness, I say it shall be limitless, unloosen'd,
LG1891	9890	I say you shall yet find the friend you were looking for.
LG1891	9891	I announce a man or woman coming, perhaps you are the one, (So long!)
LG1891	9892	I announce the great individual, fluid as Nature, chaste, affection-ateaffectionate, compassionate, fully arm'd.
LG1891	9893	I announce a life that shall be copious, vehement, spiritual, bold,
LG1891	9894	I announce an end that shall lightly and joyfully meet its transla-tiontranslation.
LG1891	9895	I announce myriads of youths, beautiful, gigantic, sweet-blooded,
LG1891	9896	I announce a race of splendid and savage old men.
LG1891	9897	O thicker and faster—(So long!)
LG1891	9898	O crowding too close upon me,
LG1891	9899	I foresee too much, it means more than I thought,
LG1891	9900	It appears to me I am dying.
LG1891	9901	Hasten throat and sound your last,
LG1891	9902	Salute me—salute the days once more. Peal the old cry once more.
LG1891	9903	Screaming electric, the atmosphere using,
LG1891	9904	At random glancing, each as I notice absorbing,
LG1891	9905	Swiftly on, but a little while alighting,
LG1891	9906	Curious envelop'd messages delivering,
LG1891	9907	Sparkles hot, seed ethereal down in the dirt dropping,
LG1891	9908	Myself unknowing, my commission obeying, to question it never daring,
LG1891	9909	To ages and ages yet the growth of the seed leaving,
LG1891	9910	To troops out of the war arising, they the tasks I have set promul-gingpromulging,
LG1891	9911	To women certain whispers of myself bequeathing, their affection me more clearly explaining,
LG1891	9912	To young men my problems offering—no dallier I—I the mus- cle of their brains trying,
LG1891	9913	So I pass, a little time vocal, visible, contrary,
LG1891	9914	Afterward a melodious echo, passionately bent for, (death making me really undying,)
LG1891	9915	The best of me then when no longer visible, for toward that I have been incessantly preparing.
LG1891	9916	What is there more, that I lag and pause and crouch extended with unshut mouth?
LG1891	9917	Is there a single final farewell?
LG1891	9918	My songs cease, I abandon them,
LG1891	9919	From behind the screen where I hid I advance personally solely to you.
LG1891	9920	Camerado, this is no book,
LG1891	9921	Who touches this touches a man,
LG1891	9922	(Is it night? are we here together alone?)
LG1891	9923	It is I you hold and who holds you,
LG1891	9924	I spring from the pages into your arms—decease calls me forth.
LG1891	9925	O how your fingers drowse me,
LG1891	9926	Your breath falls around me like dew, your pulse lulls the tympans of my ears,
LG1891	9927	I feel immerged from head to foot,
LG1891	9928	Delicious, enough.
LG1891	9929	Enough O deed impromptu and secret,
LG1891	9930	Enough O gliding present—enough O summ'd-up past.
LG1891	9931	Dear friend whoever you are take this kiss,
LG1891	9932	I give it especially to you, do not forget me,
LG1891	9933	I feel like one who has done work for the day to retire awhile,
LG1891	9934	I receive now again of my many translations, from my avataras as-cendingascending, while others doubtless await me,
LG1891	9935	An unknown sphere more real than I dream'd, more direct, darts awakening rays about me, So long!
LG1891	9936	Remember my words, I may again return,
LG1891	9937	I love you, I depart from materials,
LG1891	9938	I am as one disembodied, triumphant, dead.
